  - **Line counts, and this entry's bill.** The script goes from 173 newlines at 7,135 bytes to 193 at 8,357, LF on both sides and terminated on both sides. This entry is six content lines plus a blank separator, seven rows at the head of the Fixed section, so every line at and below the old 48 shifts by seven: the file's one live self-pointer moves from target 265 to target 272 and was re-anchored in the same edit as this insert, confirmed by text witness rather than by arithmetic -- line 272 now opens the bless-or-revert owner-call bullet that sat at 265 -- and the nine frozen mentions of line 185 stay at 185 as #579 rules. Those targets do shift with the text, and this round measured what that costs: seven of the nine printed advisory before the insert and six print advisory after, because the mention that used to land on a merged-table-row bullet now lands on an unrelated wrong-reason bullet whose line happens to carry a token its own prose names. #579 forbids re-billing a frozen mention, so the gate reads one stale pointer as resolving on a token coincidence -- which is why the census composition is published below rather than a bare total. The mechanism is worth naming, because it is an instrument weakness and not a document defect: the advisory tier decides with a substring test, and the token that matched here is the three-letter word all, which the gate would equally have accepted inside install or small. A stale pointer can therefore turn from advisory into silently resolving on a token collision, and no row survives the shift to tell a human to look. Tightening that tier to a word-boundary test changes what a green gate reports, so it is filed for its own round instead of riding in on this one. The entry mints four line anchors, which the citation gate finds by shape rather than by quoting, and writes no backticked span at all, so the bare-path census, which reads only backticked line-less spans, is untouched by construction.
  - **Gates, at a fixed point.** All eight re-run after the digits were published and re-run again: citation census 891 checked, 686 resolving to a populated line, 0 hard, 193 advisory, 12 allowed, exit 0, with three consecutive printings byte-identical; bare-path census 786 claims (631 file, 155 directory), 714 resolved at the repository root, 0 hard, 44 advisory, 21 allowed, 16 wildcard families; tables 25 files, 101 tables, 718 body rows, 0 hard, 0 advisory; links 59 relative links and 55 anchor claims across 31 files, all resolving; doc examples 96 gated blocks across 32 owned pages, 6 advisory across 20 generated pages, 440 non-owned pages not opened; doc tool params 16 server and 10 Edge tools declaring a schema against 8 parameter tables and 23 example calls; schema params 16 declared against 16 with a production dispatch arm; TS client 5 of 5 PASS. This round wrote two files, scripts/check_citations.py and CHANGELOG.md, and neither is a Rust, TOML or CI path, so fmt, both clippy legs, both workspace profiles, both lib rows and the dashboard suites are unmoved by construction -- the last because they drive the shipped binary and cannot observe a file under scripts/. The gate scripts are untracked, so the revert path for this one is the saved pre-edit bytes, not a git checkout.

- **#590 the link gate was missing three link forms, two false-red classes and a way to lose its whole corpus, and the repair's first draft shipped a fourth false red (2026-10-14).** `scripts/check_links.py` had its walk widened and an anchor tier added last round, and the two items that round named instead of fixing -- the angle-bracket form and a target containing a closing paren -- turned out to be part of a six-class grammar gap that only a negative control could count. The pre-edit gate is green on this tree and that green is correct here; it is green because six classes of claim go unread, not because the tree is clean, which is the distinction #588 filed as instrument blindness and deliberately left open.
  - **Why the corpus cannot show any of it, measured before anything was changed.** Across the 31 owned pages the new capture pattern and the old one return index-identical captures on every page -- 0 differences -- and the forms the widening reads are simply absent: 0 angle-bracket inline targets, 0 empty targets, 0 targets carrying a quoted title, and 4 reference-style definitions outside a fence, all four of them https:// and so skipped by the prefix rule either way. Run against the finished tree, both scripts print the identical line -- 59 relative links and 55 anchor claims across 31 files, exit 0. A latent class is still a class: the gate's job is to read whatever a page claims, and a right verdict from a partly-blind instrument is not the same thing as a correct instrument.
  - **The six classes, named.** A percent-encoded path was looked up with its escapes still in the name, so a link GitHub follows printed "no such file". An autolink in angle brackets was dropped by an explicit skip, so neither a working one nor a broken one ever became a claim. A target containing a closing paren was truncated at that paren by the capture itself. A bare target followed by a quoted title reached the filesystem with the title glued to it, because the split on a closing paren that the comment claimed handled titles could never fire -- the capture cannot contain one. A reference-style definition, whose target the page never repeats inline, was never read at all. And the walk pruned on the absolute parts of each path, so a clone placed under any directory whose name is on the prune list loses its entire corpus while still printing a confident green. The first four are GitHub's own link grammar, the fifth is a claim form this tree does use, and the sixth is not about markdown at all.
  - **The repair -- and the false red its own first draft shipped.** The link pattern now reads either an angle-bracketed target, escapes and parentheses included, or a bare one, and a single helper peels brackets and a trailing title from whichever branch produced it, so both forms reach the filesystem decoded and bare; paths and fragments go through percent-decoding, because a URL is encoded and the heading id a fragment names is not; a second pass reads definition lines and skips fenced ones, because a bracketed line inside a sample is source code rather than a link; and the prune moved onto the path relative to the repository. **The first draft of this repair is itself a finding:** it removed the angle-bracket skip while leaving the brackets inside the capture, so every autolink the widened gate read carried a pair of brackets into the filesystem lookup -- caught on the fixture before it could reach a page, because the control was written before the code, and the shipped version is the second one.
  - **Sensitivity, proved on two throwaway trees.** A twenty-case truth table -- eleven claims that are true, six defects that are real, one fenced line that looks like a definition and is not, and two cases that pin the tallies -- reads under the shipped gate 12 links, 5 anchors, 4 broken and 2 dead, with all twenty verdicts matching. The pre-edit gate on the identical tree mismatches 6 of 20: it reads 6 of the 12 link claims, prints 4 rows of which 3 are false reds on files that exist, and finds 1 of the 4 genuinely broken links. The second tree is a single page holding a single broken link, parked inside a directory named build: there the pre-edit walk reports "All 0 relative links and 0 anchor claims resolve OK across 0 files." and exits 0, while the shipped walk prints "BROKEN LINKS: 1/1" and exits 1. That second pair is the whole argument for the relative-parts fix -- a gate that read nothing is otherwise indistinguishable from a gate that found nothing, and only its own file count tells them apart.
  - **Three things this repair does not claim.** A failure row now prints the decoded target rather than the literal span, so the row names the missing file and no longer names which of the three forms carried the claim; every other byte of the output format is unchanged, which is what keeps #588's quotation of this gate's own output inside this file still matching what it prints. A fragment is percent-decoded because URL grammar says a fragment identifier is encoded, and no claim on this tree exercises that branch. And a title-bearing autolink, the one place where the two grammar halves overlap, is read by peeling brackets rather than by a full CommonMark title parser, so a title that itself contained a closing angle bracket would still truncate.
  - **Line counts, and this entry's bill.** The script goes from 91 newlines at 3,098 bytes to 140 at 5,280, LF on both sides and terminated on both sides. No document cites a line number into it -- grepped before the edit, zero occurrences of a numbered cite naming that file anywhere in the tree -- so its growth falsifies no anchor, and it stays one of the two gate scripts git tracks while six sit untracked, which is #493's open index question rather than something to settle here. These seven lines and their blank separator insert at the head of *Fixed*, so this file's single live self-pointer moves from 257 to 265 under the shift, confirmed by re-reading the text now opening line 265 as the bless-or-revert owner-call bullet and not by arithmetic, while the nine historical mentions #579 froze keep their digits and this entry writes no pointer into this file. Gates on the finished tree, each exit read back from its own log and both doc censuses re-run three times after these figures went in: links 59 relative links and 55 anchor claims across 31 files, exit 0 -- the same triple the widened gate printed before this entry landed, because these seven lines mint no link-shaped span; citations 887 checked / 681 resolve to a populated line / 0 hard / 194 advisory / 12 allowed, exit 0; the bare-path census 786 claims (631 file, 155 directory) / 714 resolved at the repository root / 0 hard / 44 advisory / 21 allowed / 16 wildcard, accounting 786 against 786, exit 0. The citation delta against the reading #589 published is composition rather than subtraction: checked holds at 887 because this entry mints no citation form at all, populated falls by three to 681 and advisory rises by three to 194 because the eight-line shift moves line 185 itself onto text that no longer carries the token three of the nine frozen mentions expect -- the mention at pre-edit line 269 and the pair at pre-edit 288 -- which is the churn #579 accepts on a frozen mention instead of a broken anchor; the advisory rows naming that target went from four to seven, now printed on citing lines 204, 235, 277, 285 twice and 296 twice, while the two that stay populated sit at 286 and 440. The path census rises by exactly one claim and one root resolution over the 785 claims (630 file, 155 directory) / 713 root published with #589 -- this entry's single backticked script path, resolving at the repository root -- with directory, advisory, allowed and wildcard counts all unmoved. The five gates that read neither a citation nor a path claim print the tuples they printed before the insert: tables 25 files / 101 tables / 718 body rows / 0 hard / 0 advisory, doc examples 96 gated json blocks across 32 owned pages with 6 advisory blocks in 20 generated files skipped and 440 pages not opened, tool params 16 server plus 10 Edge tools against 8 parameter tables and 23 example calls, schema params 16 declared against 16 production dispatch arms, and the TypeScript client arm gate 5 of 5 PASS. No Rust, TOML, schema, workflow or test file moved, so both workspace profiles, both lib rows, formatting and both lint legs keep the figures published in #576 and #577, and the dashboard suites cannot observe a docs-and-tooling round.

- **#589 the doc-example gate opened 337 of this tree's 492 markdown pages, 285 of them authored somewhere else, and printed all 337 as one coverage figure while a block in any of them could fail the build.**
  - **What the old walk read, measured before anything was changed.** It was an `os.walk` from the repository root pruning six directory names (target, node_modules, .git, quickjs, micropython, cpython), which left 337 pages open and 102 fenced json blocks parsed: 96 of those blocks sit on the 32 pages this repository authors, 6 on the 20 generated wiki pages (already advisory, and still are), and 0 on the 285 remaining open pages -- 284 vendored guest-source trees under `bench_tools/` deeper than a tool directory's own README, plus the pytest artefact README under sdk/python. So on today's tree the verdict did not move: 0 hard failures before, 0 after. The defect is latent and structural -- nothing in the gate distinguished "this file is ours" from "this file arrived with a runtime", so the first schematic block written into a vendored README would print `BAD` against a file nobody here owns, and the fixer's two options would both be wrong: edit the vendored tree (the next regeneration overwrites it) or admit a non-repo path into an allowlist keyed on repo claims.
  - **The ownership rule, taken from the gates that already have one.** The new walk prunes on a named set (the six old names plus `.qoder`, dist, build, bench_tools and the pytest cache directory) and gates exactly the pages that survive it, with two carve-outs: `.qoder` is advisory rather than gated because a knowledge-card pass writes it, and owned_nested() keeps a tool directory's own first-level README in the gated set -- one path element under bench_tools, named README.md, and nothing deeper. That carve-out is measured, not assumed: git ls-files prints `bench_tools/tinygo_wasm/README.md`, so that page was authored here and its 1 block must stay hard-gated, while the other 284 pages under `bench_tools/` are untracked vendor trees. The alternative -- gate on trackedness alone -- was tried and rejected: `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` is untracked, first-party, and carries 1 gated block, so a git-ls-files universe would have silently dropped a real check while looking principled.
  - **Proved as a set, not as a headline.** Re-running the pre-fix walk and the new one against the same tree gives 102 block coordinates each, keyed on (page, start line, parse detail), and both set differences are empty: no block that was parsed is now skipped, and none was invented. The page counts reconcile: 32 gated + 20 advisory + 440 never opened = 492, which is also the whole-tree recursive markdown count. Two consecutive runs print byte-identical output (1,134 bytes) at exit 0. Three things this repair explicitly does not claim: it does not raise coverage, it does not change any verdict on today's tree, and it does not make the generated wiki checked -- it makes the gate's own sentence report which pages it owns.
  - **Sensitivity proved on a copy, never on a repo file.** A nine-page fixture in a temp tree (one owned page that parses, one owned page that does not, one sibling-glob page, a first-level `bench_tools` README, two deeper vendored READMEs, the pytest artefact, a generated page, a `target/` page) was run through both scripts from its own `scripts/` directory. Pre-fix: exit 1, six `BAD` rows, three of them on pages this repository does not author. Repaired: exit 1, three `BAD` rows -- the same three owned defects, including the first-level tool README -- the same one advisory row, and a headline stating 4 non-owned pages were never opened. The gate still fails on every owned example and no longer fails on a vendored one.

Widening each was measured on a copy rather than by editing the gate: the citation census reads 887 checked, 685 resolving to a populated line, 0 hard, 190 advisory, 12 allowed identically both ways, and the table gate goes 25 files to 31 while still finding 101 tables, 718 body rows, 0 hard, 0 advisory. The six pages they never open carry no citations and no tables, so widening them is a reporting change with no verdict content -- which is why each waited for its own round; #591 landed the citations gate and #592 the tables one, and on the landed 32-page corpus the table gate still reads 101 tables, 718 body rows, 0 hard, 0 advisory.
  - **Line counts, and this entry's bill.** `scripts/check_doc_examples.py` goes from 103 newlines at 3,905 bytes to 139 at 5,589, LF on both sides. This entry is eight lines (header + six bullets + the blank separator), inserted at the head of the Fixed section, so every line at and below the old 48 shifts by 8: the file's one live self-pointer moves from line 249 to line 257 and is re-anchored in the same edit, and the nine frozen mentions of line 185 stay at line 185 as #579 rules -- their targets shift with the text and the census can only register that as advisory-token churn. Citation census after the edit: Citation census after the edit: 887 checked -- unchanged, because this entry mints no line anchor -- 684 of them resolving to a populated line, 191 advisory, 0 hard, 12 allowed, exit 0. The one line that moved from populated to advisory is a frozen mention of line 185 whose target shifted with the text and no longer carries a token its prose names: advisory-token lines citing that target went 3 to 4, which is churn #579 accepts on a frozen mention, not a broken anchor. - **#588 the link gate read 25 of this repository's 31 first-party markdown pages and skipped every one of the 55 in-page anchor claims those pages carry (2026-10-14).** `scripts/check_links.py` built its universe from a two-glob list -- root markdown plus the docs directory one level deep -- which stops at the first subdirectory, and its skip prefixes dropped every target beginning with `#`, so an anchor was never a claim at all. The repair replaces the globs with a pruned recursive walk and adds a heading-anchor tier that reads each claim against the id GitHub would generate for the heading it names. **Neither half found a broken target in a first-party page**, which is why this is recorded as instrument blindness rather than as a doc defect: 56 claims move from unchecked to checked and all 56 pass.
  - **What the two-glob walk left unread, measured against the whole tree.** The tree holds 492 markdown files; 31 of them are first-party pages once the vendored and generated trees are pruned, and the old walk opened 25. The six it never opened are `benches/README.md`, `client/README.md`, `examples/README.md` and the SDK pages `sdk/go/README.md`, `sdk/python/README.md` and `sdk/typescript/README.md`; together they carry 16 link-shaped spans, 15 of them external and exactly **one** relative path claim -- the TypeScript README's dot-relative pointer into `docs/CLIENT_INTEGRATION.md`, which the widened walk reads from that page's own directory two levels down and finds resolving -- so the widened walk takes the readable link population from 58 to 59 and reports no new failure. A seventh candidate, `sdk/python/.pytest_cache/README.md`, is a pytest artefact and is pruned by name.
  - **The anchor tier, and the id grammar it has to imitate.** A heading is lower-cased, stripped of punctuation, its runs of spaces turned into hyphens, and a repeated heading is suffixed -1, -2; a heading printed inside a fenced block renders as text and gets no id, so fence contents are excluded from the slug set. On the finished tree the tier reads **55** claims and every one of them names a heading that exists. They are all in-page: the 31 first-party pages carry **zero** file-plus-fragment links, so the cross-page branch of the tier is exercised by the fixture alone and is never the reason for a verdict on this tree.
  - **Sensitivity, proved on a copy rather than by mutating a repo page.** A twelve-claim fixture, ten claims on one page and two on a page two directories down, reads under the new gate 7 links and 5 anchors and prints **3 broken links and 3 dead anchors**. The three links are a missing file, a missing file named with a fragment, and a broken link on the page two directories down; the three anchors are a heading that was renamed away, a wrong cross-page fragment, and a heading that exists only inside a fenced block. The shipped version of the gate on the identical fixture reads 5 links and 0 anchors and prints **2**: the two-deep page is never opened, so its broken link is invisible, and no anchor is ever checked. The five greens are reported with the reds, because a tier that flags an anchor to a real heading, a plain link two directories down, a correct cross-page fragment, or a duplicate heading reached both bare and with its -1 suffix would be a false-positive factory rather than a gate.
  - **Three things this repair does not claim.** The angle-bracket link form and a target containing a closing paren -- which the link grammar truncates at -- are still skipped, and both are zero-occurrence classes across the 31 pages (measured: 0 and 0), so they hide nothing today; they are named here instead of being fixed against a population that does not exist. The prune is a set of directory names rather than a rule about ownership, so a first-party page placed under a directory named build or dist would be skipped silently -- the same unread-page class this entry repairs, one name away. This entry's six lines insert at the head of *Fixed*, so this file's single live self-pointer moves from 242 to 249 under the shift -- witnessed by re-reading the text now opening line 249 as the bless-or-revert owner-call bullet rather than by arithmetic -- while the nine historical mentions #579 froze keep their digits and this entry writes no pointer into this file. This entry mints ten path claims and no citation form at all, so the path census rises from the 767 (616 file, 151 directory) / 696 root published with #585 by exactly those ten, every one resolving at the repository root with advisory, allowed and wildcard unmoved, while the citation tuple is byte-identical across the seven-line shift -- the same three frozen self-mention advisories print, on citing lines that moved 212, 262 and 262 to 219, 269 and 269, changing position and not class. **One of the ten was a false claim this round minted, and the census caught it on its first run.** The draft quoted the TypeScript README's pointer in that page's own two-level dot-relative spelling, and a claim spelled that way on a root-level page walks out of the repository instead of naming it, so the gate printed exactly one HARD row, on this entry's second line, and exited 1. The repair re-spells the span as the target the README reaches and keeps the dot-relative shape as prose, one line, one claim, resolving at the root -- and the red left with it. A round that widens an instrument then writes its own entry inside that instrument's scope, and the census read the new lines the moment they landed -- which is exactly what this gate is for.

- **#585 the citation gate read 14 file suffixes and the path gate 19 while this repository tracks 27, so every line-numbered claim about a batch script, a Go or TypeScript SDK file, or a Makefile was unchecked by construction (2026-10-14).** Both doc gates decide what counts as a file claim from a suffix alternation, and the two alternations had drifted apart with nothing watching either. A suffix missing from a list is not a red row -- it is an unchecked claim, which is why every census since the citation gate was written stayed green while the batch-script anchors recorded in #583 and #586 were invisible to the one instrument built to read them. The repair widens both lists in place to one 29-name vocabulary and gives the path gate `vocabulary_guard()`, which tests the list itself from two directions so that the next file type added to the tree surfaces as a finding instead of as a silence.

Nothing about the Makefile changed between the round that wrote the sentence and this one; only the instrument learned to read it.

No Rust, TOML, CI, schema or test file moved, so both workspace profiles, both lib rows, formatting and both lint legs keep the figures published in #576 and #577, and the dashboard suites cannot observe a docs-and-tooling round.

- **#587 the benchmark page misdescribed five of the nine targets it exists to document, and the round that repaired it minted false claims of its own (2026-10-14).** `benches/README.md` is the only written account of what `cargo bench` runs on this tree, and the path walk reads that page for resolvable spans, never for accuracy against code, so a wrong sentence there is invisible to every gate. Measured inventory: Cargo.toml declares nine bench targets and nine harness-false lines, three Criterion suites and six custom reporters; the six read no process arguments at all, so every Criterion flag the page quotes applies only to the three suites, while the bench command still runs all nine.
  - **The five inventory bullets, each rewritten to the measured shape.** `comprehensive_benchmark` was described as covering WASM plus transport protocols across JSON-RPC stdio/HTTP, NDA shmem and JSON shmem: it drives 12 language blocks -- mruby is skipped by its own comment at `benches/comprehensive_benchmark.rs:947` and the TypeScript block loads the QuickJS module -- through exactly one transport call site, the stdio JSON path against `bench_tools/node_tool.js`, so four of the five pipelines its header named were never measured by it, and its recommendations block still prints "For highest throughput: Use NDA binary protocol with shmem transport" for a transport that binary never times. `wasm_tool_latency` times cold, warm and a real 100-call P99 for 12 WASM runtimes and no native call at all, yet its header announced a native comparison and its footer printed three constants attributed to another file. `metering_cache_bench` was described by Part A of four implemented parts, `wasm_vs_native` by its hot path only, and `tool_compatibility_test` restated its own header's 16 tools across 10 languages, 20 cases each, without saying the suite compares WASM output against native.
  - **Two over-claims came from this round's own repair text, and both were caught by re-measuring the sentence rather than the source.** The first draft asserted every Criterion group sets a throughput: measured, `benches/binary_protocol_bench.rs` declares twelve benchmark groups and thirteen named ids, ten groups set Elements(1), the large-payload group sets Bytes(1024) at line 265, and the size-comparison group sets none. The second draft wrote that `src/benchmark.rs` never times a native tool call; it does, twice -- the bench_echo arm at `src/benchmark.rs:853` over 1000 iterations and the file_read arm at `src/benchmark.rs:937` -- so the attribution I deleted from the latency footer was not baseless, it was mis-scoped: those arms time different tools than the text_analyze path that binary measures, and no tracked artifact in the repo records the printed 950 ns, so the shipped wording says transcribed estimate and nothing more.
  - **The witness sentence, the worse kind of error: a plausible citation to an artifact that does not hold the figures.** Mid-round text claimed the tracked transcript of the comparison run behind the removed "Performance Targets" table is `bench_nda/bench_full_run.txt`. That file is tracked (its own ls-files lookup exits 0) and is the same harness, and it carries none of the three figures the table was judged against -- grepping 0.149, 0.197 and 0.878 over it returns zero matches -- because it is a v3.0.0 run over 17 tools whose tables put Node/stdio tools/list at 0.073 ms against Rust JSON/stdio at 0.165 ms. The run behind `docs/COMPARISON.md:44` and `docs/USER_GUIDE.md:976` is a dated root capture that is both untracked and ignored by the bench-log pattern at `.gitignore:66`, and COMPARISON's own methodology line already states raw output is not committed; the replacement names the tracked file as a different run of the same harness and stops there.
  - **Line counts, gates, and this entry's bill.** The page went from 168 lines at HEAD to 262 in the working tree across the two rounds that rewrote it; this round's edits are one-for-one except the one bullet that grew from a line to four, and every one of them sits below the page's single inbound line cite, so the only positional event is the allowance row for the criterion output directory, which moved with the rewrite from line 66 to line 134 and still lands on the same reasoned exemption -- the allowed figure holds at 21 and no dead allowlist row was minted. Both source repairs are comment and print text: the latency binary stays at 228 lines, and the comprehensive header swap replaces two lines with two, though that file's total moved from 1098 at HEAD to 1032 in the tree under earlier rounds' deletions. All ten claims this entry writes land in one instrument each -- five bare spans for the path gate and five path-number cites for the census -- and the readings, published after the insert rather than before it, are byte-identical across two runs and exit 0 on both: 758 path claims (608 file, 150 directory), 687 resolving at the repository root, 0 hard, 43 advisory, 21 allowed, 15 wildcard; and 868 cites checked, 673 populated, 0 hard, 187 advisory, 8 allowed. The census delta is read off its printed rows rather than inferred from the headlines: four of this entry's five cites resolve, the fifth prints as a token advisory, and advisory still falls by one because two of the nine frozen self-mentions that name line 185 landed on incidental token matches once the insert shifted that line's content -- the populated-means-a-token-occurs class earlier rounds record, not a repair. No source file, schema, CI step or manifest moved, so no test total changes and the dashboard suites cannot observe any of it; and no CI step runs the bench command at all (zero occurrences of that string in the workflows), which is why none of these sentences was ever contradicted by a pipeline.

- **#586 three of the batch Edge script's own documented flags were inert: an unescaped `)` inside an `echo` payload is the token that closes the enclosing `if ... else ...` block, so the branch the flag exists to skip runs anyway (2026-10-13).** Found by its side effect rather than by reading for it: the run written to witness the #584 timestamp repair printed `[2/6] Skipping build (--skip-build flag set` -- message truncated exactly where the payload's own `)` was eaten as the block terminator -- and then printed `[2/6] Building WASM binary for wasm32-wasmer-wasi...`, which is the `else` arm of the very block that had just announced it was skipping the build. The parser does two damaging things at once: it steals the terminator and then reads the following `else (` as continuous with the truncated `if`, so a skip flag performs the skipped work, and the only clue a reader gets is a missing character in the message. Five sites, all repaired with the file's own caret-escape convention.
  - **Population, bounded by sweeping the file for the shape rather than by the three sites that happened to fire.** Every unescaped closing paren on an indented line inside a block: `deploy/deploy-edge.bat:95` (the `--skip-build` message), `:125` (the size warning), `:139` (`--skip-tests`), `:165` (a hint printed under a failed `wasmer deploy`) and `:175` (`--dry-run` verification) -- five, and the same sweep after the repair reports zero residual on indented lines. The file already carries the fix's convention for the same hazard: `^<` and `^>` at `:185`, `:205` and `:206`, so `^)` is the matching escape rather than an invention, and the repair is five caret insertions with no line added or removed.
  - **Pre-fix damage, measured at three of the five sites -- and one non-determinism recorded rather than reconciled.** At `:95`, twice against the shipped script on this host with `--skip-build`: one run printed the truncated skip line and then exited **1** on the inner guard's `[ERROR] WASM binary not found at ... and --skip-build was specified.`, and the other printed the truncated skip line and entered the build branch, running `python deploy/build-edge.py`. Same file, same flag, two different wrong outcomes, because the recovery after the stolen `)` depends on the text that follows it rather than on the structure the author wrote; both readings are kept because neither run's shape lets you predict the other. `:139` and `:175` were measured on a verbatim excerpt with the two external commands replaced by markers: `--skip-tests` printed `[4/6] Skipping tests (--skip-tests flag set` immediately followed by `[4/6] Running tests...` and the cargo marker, and `--dry-run` printed `[6/6] Skipping verification (dry run mode` immediately followed by `[6/6] Verifying deployment health...` and the `wasmer edge list` marker. So two of the three flags that #583's repaired header advertises did the opposite of what they say.
  - **Post-fix, one scratch run witnesses four of the five sites and the fifth is named as unexercised.** From a scratch directory holding a stub `wasmer.toml`, a 6 MiB stub `velocity-edge.wasm` and a stub `deploy/build-edge.py`, with all three flags set: the script printed `[2/6] Skipping build (--skip-build flag set)` and never entered the build branch -- the build marker was checked for absence after the run, not inferred -- printed the size warning complete as `[WARNING] Binary size 6291464 exceeds recommended limit of 5242880 bytes (5MB).`, printed `[4/6] Skipping tests (--skip-tests flag set)` with no test run after it, printed `[6/6] Skipping verification (dry run mode)` with no `wasmer edge list` after it, and reached the summary block. `:165` is escaped but **not exercised**: it is reachable only when a real `wasmer deploy` fails, which a dry run by definition never attempts. The run's exit was read from the file the harness wrote (REAL_EXIT_POSTFIX_RUN=0), never from the pipe.
  - **Line counts, and the fact that no instrument in this repo can see either repair.** `deploy/deploy-edge.bat` holds 210 editor lines on both sides -- 6,897 bytes before and 6,902 after, the +5 being the five carets and nothing else -- CRLF-uniform with 0 lone CR and 0 lone LF, so every anchor #583 names for this file (`:8`, `:12`, `:33`, `:83`, `:195`) still denotes the same text. `deploy/deploy-edge.sh` is untouched: bash has no block terminator for an argument to steal, so a `)` in an echo string is only text and no shell-side twin of this defect exists. The census cannot witness the repair at all: `scripts/check_citations.py:29` is the extension list -- `rs`, `toml`, `md`, `c`, `h`, `py`, `mjs`, `js`, `sh`, `yaml`, `yml`, `json`, `css`, `html` -- and the batch suffix is absent from it, while `scripts/check_doc_paths.py` drops any span carrying a colon, so a `.bat` form with a line number is invisible to both instruments and only a bare `deploy/deploy-edge.bat` span enters the path census. That asymmetry is open item **#585**; it means every line-numbered claim about this file, in #583 and in both entries of this round, is unchecked by construction rather than passing. **This round's bill, measured on the finished tree.** All seven doc instruments ran on it and every exit was written to a log and re-read there rather than taken from a pipe: citations, doc examples, links, markdown tables, tool schema params, doc tool params and bare paths each exit **0**. The five that read no citation and no path claim print the identical tuples they printed before the insert -- 25 files / 101 tables / 718 body rows / 0 hard / 0 advisory, 58 relative links across 25 files, 102 fenced json blocks across 337 markdown files, 16 declared parameters against 16 dispatch arms, and 16 server plus 10 Edge tools against 8 parameter tables and 23 example calls. The citation census moves from 860 checked / 666 resolve to a populated line / 0 hard / 186 advisory / 8 allowed to **863 / 667 / 0 / 188 / 8**, which closes as 863 = 667 + 188 + 8, and its composition is read off the printings rather than subtracted from the headlines: these two entries mint **three** counted forms and all three resolve -- the census script's own extension-list line, the shell twin's UTC timestamp line, and the vendored micropython tool's line that shells out to the missing binary -- while the other two advisories are #579's cost paid a third time, since two of the frozen mentions naming line 185 sat at 187 and 238 before this insert and sit at 198 and 249 after it with their digits untouched, and the shift moved unrelated text under them, so 666 - 2 + 3 = 667 and 186 + 2 = 188 rather than any split of the new lines alone. The path census moves from 741 claims (592 file, 149 directory) / 670 resolved at the repository root / 0 hard / 43 advisory / 21 allowed / 15 wildcard to **746 (597 file, 149 directory) / 675 / 0 / 43 / 21 / 15**, accounting 746 against 746, and the five new claims are one build-helper path, two batch-script paths, the shell twin and this gate's own file, all five resolving at the root; they are named in plain prose above and here because a code span for each would mint five more claims and move the figure it is trying to report -- the same instrument-blindness class as #585, in the other direction. Single-segment spans written in these entries, wasmer.toml and velocity-edge.wasm, never enter that census at all, since its shape rule requires a separator. This file holds 686 lines before the insert and 697 after, CRLF-uniform with 0 lone CR and 0 lone LF at 636,298 bytes, the eleven being these two entries plus their blank separators, and the live self-pointer's digit moves from 217 to 228 under that shift -- confirmed by re-reading line 228's opening text as the bless-or-revert owner-call bullet rather than by arithmetic alone -- while the nine frozen mentions keep the positions their own runs recorded. No src, Cargo.toml, workflow, schema or test file moved, so no test total changed and the dashboard suites cannot observe either repair.

- **#584 the batch Edge script built its run timestamp from `wmic`, which Windows 11 26200 no longer ships, so both prints rendered the slice text instead of a time (2026-10-13).** This file's #583 entry recorded the defect and deliberately left it alone on the grounds that repairing it changes what the script *does*, not what it says; this round takes that repair, and the round above it (#586) is a defect this verification walked into.
  - **What the shipped line did, and why the script kept going.** `deploy/deploy-edge.bat:32` read `for /f "tokens=2 delims==" %%I in ('wmic os get localdatetime /value 2^>nul') do set "dt=%%I"`, and `where wmic` on this host finds nothing while the invocation reports `'wmic' is not recognized as an internal or external command`; because the `2^>nul` puts that error where no guard can see it, `dt` is never assigned, `:33` slices an undefined variable, and both prints render the unexpanded slice text -- ` Timestamp: ~0,4dt:~4,2dt:~6,2dt:~8,2dt:~10,2dt:~12,2Z` -- at `:37` and again in the summary at `:195`. The shell twin printed a correct time in the same run, `2026-10-13T23:04:21Z`, so the defect was host-specific and silently cosmetic: no exit code, no warning, no failed step.
  - **Three candidate spellings, each run on this host the way the script would run it, and the one that is wrong for a reason you can only see by comparing clocks.** `(Get-Date).ToUniversalTime().ToString(yyyyMMddHHmmss)` with the format bare is a PowerShell ParserError (`Missing ')' in method call`) that still leaves the batch at `rc=0`, because `for /f` discards the child's failure -- the empty-variable case above is exactly that shape -- so it repairs nothing; `Get-Date -Format yyyyMMddHHmmss` printed `2026-10-14T01:21:51Z` against a reference window that closed at `2026-10-13T23:21:51Z`, i.e. **two hours ahead**, because the host is UTC+2, `-Format` yields local time, and the literal `Z` in the slice template at `:33` would mislabel it. What shipped is the quoted-format form, `for /f "delims=" %%I in ('powershell -NoProfile -Command "(Get-Date).ToUniversalTime().ToString('yyyyMMddHHmmss')"') do set "dt=%%I"`, which printed `20261013232150` and formatted to `2026-10-13T23:21:50Z` inside that same window; `:31`'s comment now states why the source is UTC. The zone choice is not cosmetic: `deploy/deploy-edge.sh:37` builds the same `DEPLOY_TIMESTAMP` with `date -u`, so a local-time batch twin would print a different time than the shell twin under one flag.
  - **Population, line-neutrality, and this entry's bill.** `wmic` sits on three tracked lines: the defective one, this file's own #583 record of it, and `bench_tools/micropython_wasm/micropython-1.24.1/tools/uf2conv.py:263`, a vendored upstream tool that shells out to it on Windows hosts -- so one repair, not three, and the vendored one stays out of scope. The edit replaces one command line with one command line and adds nothing else, so the script stayed at 210 lines and each anchor #583 names for it (`:8`, `:12`, `:33`, `:83`, `:195`) was re-read after the write rather than trusted to arithmetic. Post-fix witnesses: `2026-10-13T23:23:13Z` inside a reference window of 23:23:12Z-23:23:14Z, and `2026-10-13T23:27:44Z` inside 23:27:43Z-23:27:45Z. The gate and census figures for this round are published once, in the bill bullet of the entry above this one, so the same reading is not carried twice through two code paths.

- **#583 both Edge deploy scripts advertised an invocation their own first guard refuses, and two docs repeated it (2026-10-13).** `deploy/deploy-edge.sh:6` read `Usage: ./deploy-edge.sh [--skip-tests] [--skip-build] [--dry-run]` and `deploy/deploy-edge.bat:8` read `Usage: deploy-edge.bat` with the same flag list, while neither script recovers its own directory: a sweep of both files for `cd`, `chdir`, `pushd`, `dirname`, `BASH_SOURCE` and `%~dp0` returns zero matches, so every path they touch is relative to whichever directory the reader is standing in. `deploy/deploy-edge.sh:12` and `deploy/deploy-edge.bat:12` each point the WASM artifact at `target/wasm32-wasmer-wasi/release/velocity-edge.wasm`, and the step-1 guard at `deploy/deploy-edge.sh:91-95` / `deploy/deploy-edge.bat:83-87` exits 1 with "wasmer.toml not found in current directory. Run this script from the project root." `wasmer.toml` exists at the repository root and in `nested-poc/`, not in `deploy/`. Both quoted usage spans carry spaces, so the path gate drops them at its space rule and this entry mints no path claim for the defective form.
  - **The refusal was run, not inferred.** `--dry-run` from inside the `deploy` directory exits **1** on both scripts, each printing those same two lines after having already passed every earlier check on this host: rustc 1.100.0-nightly, the scripts' own WASIX prerequisite probe, wasmer 7.4.1, and a live `wasmer whoami` answering `Logged into registry wasmer.io as user unitbuilds`. The guard that fires is therefore the cwd guard, not a missing dependency -- which is what makes the header worse than merely terse: following it produces an error whose text already states the thing the header should have said. Logs at `~/g583_neg_sh.txt` and `~/g583_neg_bat.txt`.
  - **Population bounded by sweeping the name, not by picking the doc that looked wrong.** Eleven tracked lines in the tree name `deploy-edge.sh` or `deploy-edge.bat` (excluding `.git` and `target`): the two script headers, three invocation instructions, and the rest prose name-mentions inside historical entries. Two invocation sites were already correct -- `docs/edge_operations.md:318` and its table twin at `:551`, the latter re-pointed last round -- and widening the sweep to `deploy-edge` with no extension adds five lines, all of them correct references to `.github/workflows/deploy-edge.yml`. Three repairs were needed: the two headers and `docs/edge_deployment_quickstart.md:19`, whose Step 2 block printed the bare script name while the block immediately after it runs `python deploy/build-edge.py`, a repository-root command -- so that page already contradicted itself.
  - **The migration block carried a second, older defect: a prerequisite this same file records as superseded.** The "Migration from Previous Versions" block near the bottom of this file printed `rustup target add wasm32-wasip1` as the Edge prerequisite. That is the exact command the *Changed* bullet above it, "Deploy scripts require WASIX toolchain", records as having been replaced, and `docs/edge_user_guide.md:557` refutes it in one sentence: installing a standard `wasm32-wasip1` target does not provide WASIX HTTP support. The replacement prints the two steps the deploy workflow actually runs -- `rustup run wasix rustc --version` at `.github/workflows/deploy-edge.yml:49`, then `cargo install cargo-wasix --locked` at `:53` -- so the historical block now tells a reader to verify the toolchain the current scripts require instead of installing one they cannot use.
  - **A second defect found by running the scripts, recorded here and repaired as #584 in the round above this one.** `deploy/deploy-edge.bat:32` builds its timestamp from `wmic os get localdatetime`, `:33` slices that variable into `%DEPLOY_TIMESTAMP%`, and it prints at `:37` and again at `:195`. `wmic` is gone from this host (Windows 11 26200: `where wmic` finds nothing and the invocation reports `'wmic' is not recognized as an internal or external command`), and because the `for /f` sends its error to `nul` the script continues, so both prints render the unexpanded slice text rather than a time. The shell twin formatted correctly in the same run (`2026-10-13T23:04:21Z`). Repairing the batch means changing what the script *does*, not what it says, so it was filed as its own item rather than folded into this round's prose repairs -- **superseded:** #584 landed it two entries above, chose the `powershell Get-Date` substitute by a three-way measured comparison, and replaced one command line with one command line, which is why the four line numbers quoted in this bullet still denote the same text.
  - **Gates, line counts, and this entry's own bill.** All four repairs keep their files' line counts identical, measured against HEAD -- which for these three files differs from the working tree only by prior rounds' one-for-one lines: `deploy/deploy-edge.sh` 211 lines both sides, `deploy/deploy-edge.bat` 210, `docs/edge_deployment_quickstart.md` 107. This file held 679 lines before the insert and holds 686 after, CRLF-uniform throughout (0 lone CR, 0 lone LF). Inserting these six lines at the top of *Fixed* shifts every line below by seven -- the six entry lines plus the blank separator that keeps the newest entry off the heading -- and the file's single live self-pointer -- the *Changed*-section re-anchor bill that names the bless-or-revert bullet -- moves from 210 to 217, paid by digit bump and witnessed by re-reading the target line's text before and after; the nine historical mentions stay at the positions their own runs recorded, and per that convention this entry writes no pointer into this file. No code, schema, protocol or build input changed, so no test total moved and the dashboard suites cannot observe any of it. The seven doc/script gates were re-run on the finished tree, each with its real exit code read back from its own log: check_citations.py, check_doc_examples.py, check_links.py, check_md_tables.py, check_tool_schema_params.py, check_doc_tool_params.py and check_doc_paths.py all exit **0**, the table gate reads 25 files / 101 tables / 718 body rows and the link gate 58 relative links across 25 files, both unchanged from the reading this entry was written against. The citation census moves from 853 checked / 659 populated / 0 hard / 186 advisory / 8 allowed to **860 / 666 / 0 / 186 / 8**, and its composition is read off the instrument's printings rather than subtracted from the headlines: this entry writes **11** path-and-number forms but only **7** are counted, because the checker's extension list has no entry for the batch script's suffix, so the four citations into the .bat file are invisible to it -- a blind spot recorded as its own item rather than papered over. Of those seven, six resolve and one prints as a token advisory, the shell header's usage line, whose citing sentence names the repair instead of quoting the repaired text. Advisory therefore held at 186 while populated rose by seven only because an *existing* row left the advisory set as these lines went in: the frozen self-mention this file has declined to re-anchor sat on line 180 before the insert and sits on line 187 after it, still naming line 185, and the shift put an unrelated bullet under that digit -- so the pointer is now wrong in a new way while grading green, which is the union-token blind spot the previous entry documented and not a repair. The path census moves from 735 claims (588 file, 147 directory) / 665 resolved at the repository root / 0 hard / 43 advisory / 20 allowed / 15 wildcard to **741 (592 file, 149 directory) / 670 / 0 / 43 / 21 / 15**, accounting 741 against 741, and its one new allowance is this entry's quoted build-output path landing on the rule that already exempts that artifact.

- **#582 the bare-path gate could not read a dot, so a tree carrying five broken path claims reported zero (2026-10-13).** `scripts/check_doc_paths.py` decides what counts as a path claim before any resolution tier runs, and its shape gate anchored the character class at `[A-Za-z0-9_*]`: a span whose first character is `.` was rejected at that character, so no ./ or ../ claim ever reached dispatch. The page-relative tier exists precisely to read a claim written relative to the file naming it, and it therefore printed its zero on every input -- the same dead-tier class the previous entry records for globs and braces, and the same distinction: not a coverage gap but a blindness. Measured, the pre-fix shape gate's `checked` figure excludes all 13 dot-prefixed backtick spans in the walk, which is why none of this round's five findings could have been reported by it, since all five are dot forms: three `examples/README.md:187` cross-references written as ../GETTING_STARTED.md, ../MIGRATION.md and ../CLIENT_INTEGRATION.md for files that live under `docs/`, `docs/edge_operations.md:551` naming ./deploy-edge.sh for a script at `deploy/deploy-edge.sh`, and `docs/COMPREHENSIVE_BENCHMARK.md:203`'s invented ./bench_tools/build_all.sh. The fix gives the shape gate a leading (?:\.{1,2}/)+ alternative that requires a real segment after it -- so a bare ./ stays out as the cwd marker it is, which is what `docs/USER_GUIDE.md:1018` writes -- adds a DOTREL token, a `dot_readings()` helper and one `classify()` branch: a dot claim is read twice, first at the repository root and then relative to the page writing it, because every command block in these docs executes from the root while their prose cross-references are page-relative and the span alone cannot say which the author meant. The first reading that resolves wins and is reported as the tier it resolved in; a claim that resolves under neither is hard and prints the candidates it tried; a page reading that walks out of the repository is hard with that stated as the reason; and a dot span carrying a brace or a glob is translated to its first reading before the family tiers see it.
  - **Every figure in the chain, decomposed by reverting one half at a time rather than by subtraction of headlines.** Previous entry's fixed point 703 → same walk with the fix in 709 → widened walk 715. Re-running the shipped code with only the shape gate reverted, at the old walk, reads **703 / 568 file / 135 directory / 642 root / 0 hard / 41 advisory / 17 allowed / 13 wildcard** -- identical to the figure that entry published, which prices this round's four doc repairs at a net zero change in claim count. With the fix at that same scope, 709, and the two tier figures that moved say where the six extra claims are: root 642→644 and page-relative 0→4, exactly the six dot claims that now reach `checked`. Widening the walk alone, at the shipped gate, is 709→715, +2 file and +4 directory claims from the package READMEs, of which two land in the allowance list (`benches/README.md:66` and `sdk/typescript/README.md:15`). Final reading, stated after this entry rather than before it: two consecutive runs on the finished tree are byte-identical and both exit 0 at 735 path claims checked (588 file, 147 directory), 665 resolving at the repository root, 4 relative to the page naming them, 0 hard, 43 advisory claims printed as 47 advisory lines because one brace span can name several families, 20 allowed, 15 wildcard claims matched a family, accounting 735 against 735 -- and the same walk with these six lines deleted reads 715 / 576 / 139 / 648 / 0 hard / 41 advisory / 19 allowed / 13 wildcard over four runs, two of them byte-identical, so both halves are printed because this entry is itself twenty path claims and one of the allowed sites, every path name in the sentences above this one written as a code span. The citation census moves by its own publication the same way: 844 checked, 656 populated, 0 hard, 180 advisory before, and 853 / 659 / 0 / 186 after. Read off the instrument's own printings, the nine cites this entry mints are eight token advisories plus one resolving -- which is deliberately not the delta's split, and saying so is the point: the census gained three resolving and six advisory because four of this file's own frozen mentions changed class as these six lines went in, three landing on text that repeats their tokens and one leaving it, so the existing rows netted two more resolving and two fewer advisory among themselves while the new rows added one resolving and eight advisory, and the two pairs sum to the published delta. Those four are #579's cost paid again, this time in classification rather than count, and they are the reason a composition cannot be read off a headline difference. The eight advisories are correct anchors; that checker compares the citing sentence against the target line and these sentences name the repair rather than quoting the repaired line. Of the 13 dot spans, six are claims (the four `examples/README.md` links, ./CHANGELOG.md in this file and the re-pointed ./deploy/deploy-edge.sh); four are dropped by the extension rule (./transport, ./types, ./errors, ./velocity_mcp), one by the segment rule (./) and two by the space rule, since they are command lines rather than claims.
  - **A second instrument defect, latent and proven only on a fixture -- and the honest statement of what that is worth.** The page directory was taken with `str(Path(where).parent)`, which on Windows yields sdk\typescript, while the page-relative tier joins and normalises with posixpath; a bare claim in a page two levels deep can therefore never resolve, on this platform, in the tier written to resolve it. Measured on this tree the old and new spellings are indistinguishable: with `str(Path(...).parent)` patched back in the full-scope reading is byte-identical to the shipped one (715 / 648 root / selfdir 4 / tail 22 / advisory 41 / 0 hard), because all four page-relative claims sit one level deep under `examples/`, where the two spellings coincide. The defect is real and currently unexercised, which is why it was fixed against a synthetic tree rather than a moved figure: the control script kept at ~/g582_neg2_control.py runs three variants over a fixture with a depth-2 page, and its readings are -- shipped: 7 claims, 3 hard, root 1, selfdir 1, advisory 1; old separator: the same 7 claims and the same 3 hard, but **selfdir 0 and advisory 2**; old shape gate: **checked falls to 3**, one hard, both of the fixture's dot-form findings gone. So the separator defect is a *mis-attribution*, not a red: a valid page-relative claim is re-labelled an ambiguous-tail advisory and silently loses its resolution, which is why no green or red on the real tree could have shown it. Fixed with `.as_posix()` and `import posixpath`, not `os.path.normpath`, which emits backslashes on Windows and would have re-introduced the same defect one layer down.

Measured against the call graph, neither plugin path constructs a `Sandbox`, and the sandbox that frame execution does build runs exactly one check before it spawns. Recorded last round as #574 (plugin-sandbox misattribution) and #575 (enforcement overclaim) and repaired here as prose only: no Rust, TOML, CI, schema or manifest file moved, so both workspace profiles, both lib rows, formatting and both lint legs are unmoved by construction and were not re-run.
  - **The three paths, each at its own spawn site.** A manifest plugin tool is assembled for launch at `src/plugins/mod.rs:322` from the substituted `executor.command` and its arguments, after `validate_plugin_argument` has rejected null bytes, anything over 10 KB and -- for shell-interpreter executors -- the thirteen shell metacharacters, with a wall-clock deadline capped at 300 s, 1 MiB of stdout and 256 KiB of stderr, and the manifest's own `env` map handed to the child verbatim. A WASM plugin tool is a Wasmer instance under an instruction-metering budget, which is not this `sandbox`. Only NDA frame execution builds one: `Sandbox::new()` has exactly two production call sites, `src/nda_executor.rs:96` and `src/nda_executor.rs:150`, and every spawn they lead to passes one gate -- the interpreter allowlist.
  - **Enforced versus advertised, which is where #575 sat.** Before a spawn: `check_interpreter`, a seven-name allowlist; the child starts in a fresh directory under the temp root; `VELOCITY_CSHARP_ENGINE` is removed from its environment; the wall clock and the two output caps apply. Not enforced: `check_file_access` (`src/sandbox.rs:309`) and `check_network_access` (`src/sandbox.rs:330`) have zero production call sites, so no path consults them and a sandboxed child can open any absolute path and reach any host it resolves; the 256 MB memory figure and the no-network flag travel to the child as environment hints that only cooperative code honours; only Windows adds an out-of-band cap, through `apply_job_object_limits`; and the Linux seccomp filters are definitions no spawn path installs, so on Linux and macOS nothing above the allowlist is OS-enforced. Refusals are still audited, but `Interpreter` is the only category production can record, so `FileSystem` and `Network` entries appear only in unit tests.
