The repair is a 20-line block (`crates/velocity-mcp-edge/src/main.rs:410-429`): bind the headers, let-else the header, then per chain entry parse as a bare `IpAddr` first and as `SocketAddr` second, and **skip** trusted entries rather than stopping at them, returning the rightmost untrusted address with the peer as fallback. The `ip:port` form is preserved because it measured GREEN on HEAD. The edit is line-neutral in the production region: the function still opens at `:409` and closes at `:429`, so `is_trusted_proxy` stays at `:433`, `with_cors` at `:677` and `handle_mcp_post` at `:761` — re-read by line number after the edit, not inherited from the entry above.
  - **The evidence (`crates/velocity-mcp-edge/tests/integration/security_tests.rs` at HEAD).** Four assertions on the constant `true`, at `:315` ("Empty 'old' parameter rejection implemented in tools.rs tool_text_transform"), `:331` ("Rate limit XFF bypass fixed in main.rs extract_client_ip"), `:373` ("Empty API key handling verified in ServerConfig::from_env") and `:388` ("Memory exhaustion prevention implemented via Limited in handle_mcp_post") — each with a comment conceding the mock server has no security middleware, so the test "validates the code path exists". A fifth was structurally vacuous: `test_security_cors_vary_header` (`:339`) asserted inside `if let Some(vary_val) = resp.headers().get("vary")`, and the mock never emits `Vary`, so the assertion never executed on any run. `:373` additionally mutated the process environment unguarded (`set_var("VELOCITY_API_KEY", "")` … `remove_var`), which is the #526 class of hazard. **Why they belong in `src/main.rs`'s `mod tests` instead:** reachability was established before writing anything. Every test binary in the crate (`crates/velocity-mcp-edge/tests/integration/common.rs`, `crates/velocity-mcp-edge/tests/common.rs`) runs a hand-copied mock handler with no hyper service layers, and no test spawns the real server binary; `handle_mcp_post`'s `Limited` cap takes a `Request<hyper::body::Incoming>`, which cannot be constructed in a test; and `ServerConfig::from_env`, `extract_client_ip`, `is_trusted_proxy` and `with_cors` are bin-private symbols. The only place these can be genuinely gated is the bin's own `mod tests`. So nine tests went there — five `client_ip_*` (untrusted-peer, bare-address-from-trusted-peer, rightmost-chain-skip, `ip:port` form, no-parse fallback), four `cors_*` / `config_from_env_*` (allow-list hit marks `Vary`; no origins configured → no headers; origin outside the list refused; blank/whitespace `VELOCITY_API_KEY` = auth disabled, trimmed value kept, with `var_os` save/restore) — and the four tautologies plus the vacuous CORS test were **deleted rather than rewritten**, because their subject matter is now covered where it can actually run. `test_security_text_transform_empty_old_parameter` was converted instead of deleted: tool dispatch is real through `process_mcp_request` → `EdgeToolExecutor::call_tool` → `dispatch_edge_tool`, so it now drives the control case (`old: "a"` → `"XYZbc"`) and asserts the rejection string the production code emits (`crates/velocity-mcp-edge/src/tools.rs:440-442`). The deletions are annotated in place with the reason, including that the previous versions "asserted `true` instead, so they passed either way".
  - **Lint debt cleared in the same pass, because it is the precondition for #505.** Clippy's own report at HEAD: `CLIPPY_REAL_EXIT=101`, 36 errors in `velocity-mcp-edge (test "integration_tests")`, including `assertions_on_constants` on exactly those four tautologies and `needless_borrows_for_generic_args` on the reqwest URL arguments. 33 call sites converted from `.method(&format!(…))` to `.method(format!(…))` — 18 in `http_tests.rs`, 15 in `security_tests.rs`, **0** borrowed sites left across the crate's test tree. The final gate is `cargo +stable clippy -p velocity-mcp-edge --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` → `CLIPPY_EDGE_REAL_EXIT=0`, 0 errors.
  - **Measured after:** edge bin **78 passed / 0 failed** (69 pre-existing + the 9 new; HEAD read 76 passed / 2 failed on the same 78), integration **32 / 0** (was 36 before the four deletions), lib 39 / 0, e2e 13 / 0, unit_tests 79 / 0. Workspace `cargo +stable test --workspace` → `WS_REAL_EXIT=0`, **1,159 passed / 0 failed / 55 ignored** across 30 binaries; the featured leg `--features http,database,oauth2` → `FEAT_REAL_EXIT=0`, **1,231 / 0 / 55**. Both totals move +5 from the recorded baselines (1,154 / 1,226) and the +5 is exactly this round's arithmetic: +9 new bin tests −4 deleted tautologies. `cargo +stable fmt --all --check` → exit 0 (rustfmt 1.9.0-stable / rustc 1.98.0; the machine default is 1.100.0-nightly, and CI installs `@stable`, so the gate was run through `+stable` deliberately). Citation census → exit 0, **597 checked / 464 resolve to a populated line / 0 hard failures / 125 advisory / 8 allowed** (464 + 125 + 8 = 597), after the workflow re-anchoring the same batch required; `scripts/check_doc_examples.py` → exit 0, every gated JSON example parses.
  - **Docs kept current.** `docs/edge_user_guide.md:207` (`RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE`) rewritten in place, line count preserved, to state that `X-Forwarded-For` is honoured only when the TCP peer is localhost or a private address and that the peer address is the bucket key otherwise — the row previously asserted the header drove the key without stating the trust condition. A grep for `X-Forwarded-For`, `spoof`, `rightmost` and `leftmost` over `docs/`, `README.md` and the crate found no other claim to update.
  - **What this does not cover, stated as a limit rather than as a pass.** The dashboard suites were **not** re-run and are not evidence for this entry: this change is inside the `velocity-mcp-edge` binary, while those five suites drive the native server on `:3900`, whose HTTP stack takes the peer from axum's `ConnectInfo` and is untouched. And per the standing rule that behaviour changes to a shipping binary are an operator call — this one changes who gets throttled, because proxied deployments go from one shared bucket to per-client buckets, which can surface 429s to clients that previously never hit them — the revert path is recorded: restore HEAD's closure into `extract_client_ip` (`crates/velocity-mcp-edge/src/main.rs:409`; `git show HEAD:crates/velocity-mcp-edge/src/main.rs` prints the `req.headers()…find_map(…parse::<SocketAddr>…).filter(…).map(…).unwrap_or(peer_addr.ip())` chain that HEAD compiled) and drop the nine `mod tests` cases. #527 joins the bless-or-revert batch alongside #501, #506, #508, #512, #524 and #525.

- **A `cargo fmt` failure used to delete every test step from CI, on all three operating systems (2026-10-12).** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` ran `Check formatting` as the fourth step of the `Build & Test (${{ matrix.os }})` job, ahead of `Clippy lint`, both builds and both test steps. A failing step aborts its own job, so the formatting verdict doubled as the gate on whether the suite ran at all. Read off GitHub's own job payloads (`gh api .../actions/runs/<id>/jobs`), the last **five** runs on `main` — `34823957314`, `34825799965`, `34843584303`, `35001812106`, `35286038255` (2026-09-14 → 2026-09-17) — all have the same shape: `Check formatting` → **failure**, then `Clippy lint`, `Build`, `Build with all features`, `Run all tests` and `Run tests with all features` → **skipped**. Run `35001812106` is the cleanest witness: all three OS legs individually reached `Check formatting`, each failed it, and every test binary in the matrix was skipped. In the newest run the `windows-latest` and `macos-latest` jobs report `cancelled` having only reached `Set up job`; `fail-fast: false` was already set (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:37`), so that is not the fmt failure cancelling siblings — the jobs payload exposes no timestamp or cancellation-cause field, and the reason is not determinable from it, so it is recorded as unexplained rather than explained. What *is* determinable: `cargo test` has not run in CI on `main` since 2026-09-17, because no run has been created since — local `main` sits 2 commits ahead of `origin/main` and nothing was pushed this session.
  - **The repair is sequencing, not policy.** `Check formatting` and `Clippy lint` now sit in their own `lint` job, commands byte-identical to before, with the `components: rustfmt, clippy` toolchain step and the `Swatinem/rust-cache@v2` step moved alongside them; `build-and-test` keeps checkout → toolchain → cache → the two builds → the two test steps. Moving steps into a new job inserted lines above the old ones, so the bill for this repair is every line-number citation of that file in the repo: twelve spans across this file and `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` re-anchored (clippy 34→31, the default test step 43→55, the featured one 46→58, fail-fast 17→37, the OS matrix 18→38), one of them rewritten as prose instead because what it quotes is the *committed* workflow, which still runs clippy without `--all-targets` and is not what the working tree now holds. Formatting or lint drift now goes red on `Lint` while all three test legs still execute. Verified structurally by parsing the edited file (`yaml.safe_load`): jobs `lint`, `build-and-test`, `security-audit`, `fuzz-tests`; `lint` steps `Install Rust toolchain / Rust cache / Check formatting / Clippy lint`; `build-and-test` steps `Install Rust toolchain / Rust cache / Build / Build with all features / Run all tests / Run tests with all features`.
  - **What this deliberately does not do.** It cannot make CI green: the fmt failures were real (HEAD's tree is not rustfmt-conformant — see *Workspace formatted with stable rustfmt* under *Changed*), the repair is inside the same uncommitted batch, and CI only re-runs on a push, which is an operator decision. It also does not change *what* clippy gates — the command is untouched, still the root package with `--features http,database,oauth2 --all-targets`, so the sibling-crate widening stays open as its own call. And on the toolchain: the runner installs `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable`, while locally the gate was measured with `rustfmt 1.9.0-stable (88d9e12ae1 2026-08-18)` / `rustc 1.98.0` against a machine default of `rustc 1.100.0-nightly (908501772 2026-08-30)`. The repo pins no `rust-toolchain` file and has no `rustfmt.toml`, so "local fmt agrees with CI fmt" holds only because both go through `cargo +stable` — an assumption about the runner, not something this round could verify without pushing.
  - **The other red leg was reconciled, not re-claimed.** Re-measured `cargo audit` on the current tree: `AUDIT_REAL_EXIT=0`, 472 crate dependencies scanned, 3 `unmaintained` warnings, zero vulnerabilities (the September lockfile scanned 494). The September failure was the red-class trio the *Security* section already records — `h2` RUSTSEC-2026-0258, `protobuf` RUSTSEC-2024-0437, `rustls` RUSTSEC-2026-0285 — and the CI log itself confirms the `lru` unsound advisories sat in the yellow warnings block, which is exactly the distinction that entry rests on. So the audit leg is fixed by the same unpushed batch, and nothing here contradicts it.

- **Linux was not a verified platform for this crate until this round, and the first Linux leg on a clean box printed five failures — four of which measured Windows, not the code (2026-10-11).** `cargo test --features http,database,oauth2` on the buildbox (Ubuntu 24.04.5, rustc 1.98.1) came back `test result: FAILED. 818 passed; 5 failed; 38 ignored`, and the five names, verbatim from that log, are `registry::tests::test_execute_nda_with_arguments`, `registry::tests::test_validate_file_path_accepts_valid_absolute`, `registry::tests::test_validate_file_path_valid_absolute`, `sandbox::tests::test_capability_path_allowlisting`, `sandbox::tests::test_sandbox_file_access_allowed_path`.
  - **Owner of each failure, decided by reading the fixture rather than by counting.** Four are test-side: each passed a literal Windows path (`"C:\\Users\\test\\documents\\file.nda"`, `"C:\\projects"`) to a validator that is correct on both hosts, so on Linux the code did the right thing with an input that is not absolute there and the assert failed. That is a fixture bug wearing a red badge, and the repair is `host_absolute()` — a test-module helper that roots the fixture under `C:\Users\test` on Windows and `/home/user` on a POSIX host — declared at the end of each test module (`src/sandbox.rs:1046`, `src/registry.rs:6093`) so nothing above it moved. The fifth is the product's: POSIX distributions ship `python3` with no `python` alias, and `interpreter_command` maps `.py` to the literal `python`, so every `.py` SourceCode document was unexecutable on Linux. `resolve_interpreter` + `program_on_path` (`src/nda_executor.rs:207`) now fall back to `python3` only when `python` is absent and `python3` is present, and `"python3"` was added to `ProcessCapabilities::restricted()`'s interpreter allowlist. **That one is wire-visible** — a NDA `SourceCode` call that returned an execution error before now runs — so it is logged as an operator decision with a revert path written into the function's own doc comment (#512), not filed under test repair.
  - **Green witnesses, each labelled with the OS it ran on, because the totals are not interchangeable with the Windows ones.** Same box, same tree, after the four fixes: `cargo test --features http,database,oauth2 --lib` → **823 passed / 0 failed / 38 ignored**, `REAL_EXIT=0`. The reconciliation is exact and is the reason to trust it: 823 = 818 + 5, so no test was added or removed between the red and green legs and the five flips alone account for the delta. `cargo test --workspace --features http,database,oauth2` → **1,222 passed / 0 failed / 47 ignored**, measured over **30 `test result:` lines = 25 Running targets + 5 Doc-test targets** (client, core, edge, the root crate, macros); an earlier draft of this bullet called those "30 test binaries", which is wrong in the same way a `du` total is wrong for one directory. `cargo fmt --all --check` → rc 0. `cargo clippy --features http,database,oauth2 --all-targets -- -D warnings` → rc **0** (`Finished in 5.40s`), against rc **101** before the fix, on `function apply_seccomp_filters is never used`.
  - **Two things that leg proved about my own tooling, not about the code.** The first `#[allow(dead_code)]` attempt was scp'd to `src/linux_seccomp.rs` instead of `src/sandbox/linux_seccomp.rs`, so the box kept failing on a file the build never compiled — a green run on a machine where I placed the patch is not evidence about the patch until the path is checked (`mv -f` then re-ran; the surviving copy is 8,010 bytes with the annotations at lines 62 and 214). And the first tally of the workspace log summed nothing, because it anchored on `ok.$` and guessed awk fields: the passed/failed/ignored columns are `$4/$6/$8`, and a log with 25 `Running` lines can carry 30 result lines. Both are instrument defects that would have printed a confident, wrong total.
  - **The limit of this whole set: there is no Windows witness for this round.** C: had 27 GB free against a ~28 GB debug workspace leg, and the earlier relink attempt died with `LNK1180` — a link that cannot write is a disk verdict before it is a code verdict — so deleting `target/debug` reclaimed the space and the Linux legs ran on the box instead. Every number in this entry is therefore a **Linux** number; the Windows totals in the earlier entries of this file (1,148 `--lib` / 1,219 workspace over 30 bins) stay the Windows record and are not superseded by these. The standing policy follows from the same arithmetic: Linux legs run on the buildbox, and this machine's WSL distro is no longer a verification target.

- **The committed tree cannot be compiled on a Linux host that has no OpenSSL development headers, and the fix is sitting uncommitted (2026-10-11).** `git show HEAD:Cargo.lock` resolves `openssl-sys 0.9.117`, reached through `native-tls 0.2.18` ← whose dependents are `reqwest 0.11.27`, `reqwest 0.12.28`, `hyper-tls 0.5.0`, `hyper-tls 0.6.0` and `tokio-native-tls 0.3.1`; `openssl-sys` itself is depended on by `native-tls 0.2.18` and `openssl 0.10.81`. Both reqwest declarations in `client/Cargo.toml` at HEAD were version 0.11 with `features = ["json"]` and **default features on**, and reqwest's default TLS is native-tls, so the build graph asks `openssl-sys`'s build script for a directory of OpenSSL installation it does not have. Measured on the box: `Compiling openssl-sys v0.9.117` → `Could not find directory of OpenSSL installation, and this -sys crate cannot proceed without this knowledge` → `error: failed to run custom build command`, build script exit 101 — and `dpkg -l libssl-dev` prints no installed line on that host, which is why the failure is a dependency question rather than a box-configuration accident.
  - **What the working tree changes, and the exact claim the evidence supports.** `client/Cargo.toml:23` (non-wasm block) and `crates/velocity-mcp-edge/Cargo.toml:45` are now `default-features = false` with `["json", "charset", "http2", "system-proxy", "rustls-tls-native-roots"]`. The honest claim is *not compiled*, proven by the three green logs of this round containing **zero** occurrences of `openssl-sys` and zero of `native-tls` — not *not resolved*, because the working `Cargo.lock` still lists `openssl-sys 0.9.117`, `native-tls 0.2.18`, `openssl 0.10.81` and `openssl-probe 0.2.1`: a lockfile is a union across targets and feature combinations, and only feature resolution decides what gets built. `client/Cargo.toml:27` (the wasm32 block) deliberately keeps `features = ["json"]` with defaults, since reqwest gates native-tls off wasm targets.
  - **Consequence for CI, stated as a dependency rather than a diagnosis:** the workflow builds the committed tree, so until these two manifests and the regenerated lock are landed, a Linux leg that installs no `libssl-dev` fails before it compiles a single crate — which is why the ubuntu job's log shows no test output to compare against. Revert path (#510): restore the two reqwest declarations and `cargo update -p reqwest`, or keep native-tls and declare `libssl-dev` in the build environment; either way the declaration belongs in `deploy/Dockerfile` / CI rather than in the luck of an image.

- **`cargo audit` is red at HEAD and green in the working tree, so the existing Security entry's "3 vulnerabilities → 0" describes bytes that are not yet committed (2026-10-11).** Two runs of the same installed cargo-audit 0.22.2, the only difference being which `Cargo.lock` was fed to it: HEAD's → `error: 3 vulnerabilities found!` + `warning: 6 allowed warnings found`, exit **1**; the working tree's → `warning: 3 allowed warnings found`, exit **0**. The three advisories at HEAD are RUSTSEC-2026-0258 (`h2 0.3.27`), RUSTSEC-2024-0437 (`protobuf 2.28.0`) and RUSTSEC-2026-0285 (`rustls 0.23.44`); the working lock carries no `protobuf`, no `prometheus`, and only `h2 0.4.19` and `rustls 0.23.45`, over a churn of **+278 / −525** lines (5,078 → 4,831).

It is not: an awk pass over HEAD's lock finds `prometheus` as protobuf's **only** dependent, so dropping the unused `prometheus` optional dependency is exactly what removes `protobuf` from the graph, and the entry's mechanism is sound. The defect is currency and commit-state, not a false claim — and the fix is a commit, not a code change.

The `lru` pair is gone because `lru` left the graph, so "5" was true when written and is no longer the live figure.

- **CI's matrix had no floor under a red OS, and three of its commands gated less than the repository contains (2026-10-11).** The measured diff against `HEAD` is five hunks and `git diff --numstat` reports 7/7, which covers all of them: `fail-fast: false` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:37`) joins `matrix.os: [ubuntu-latest, macos-latest, windows-latest]` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:38`), clippy gains `--all-targets`, and the build and test legs each gain `--workspace` twice. Without the floor, the first OS to fail cancels the other two, so a log can be red for one platform and carry no evidence at all about the other two — the exact shape of the ubuntu failure described in the entry above it. Widening clippy/build/test to the workspace is what makes the sibling-crate lint debt (#505) visible to the gate instead of only to me. Two of those six advisories are this bullet's own workflow anchors, and both are union-token artifacts rather than wrong anchors: the cited lines were read and do carry the flag on the first and the OS matrix on the second, but the instrument searches the union of every identifier in the code spans on the citing row, and this row is one long bullet describing five hunks, so tokens belonging to the other three hunks are looked for on those two YAML lines. HTTP leg: one `POST /v1/mcp` to the live server on 127.0.0.1:3900. Four facts a client would otherwise guess wrong, now in the table: an unknown resource URI is an **in-band** `isError` result rather than a JSON-RPC error object (the same shape whose SDK mis-classification this round's other entry fixes), `elicitation/create` and `roots/list` are stubs that acknowledge rather than act, and `connector/call` is compiled only under `--features oauth2` (`Cargo.toml:45` is `default = []`, so a default build falls to the `_` arm and gets `-32601`) with a parameter named `connectorId`.

The two shared-memory paths overwrite the field with the true value (`"shmem"` at `src/protocol/nmcp_binary.rs:547`, `"shmem-nda"` at `src/protocol/nda_native.rs:743`), and no HTTP path does, which is why the bug is invisible in the existing suite — its assertion (`src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:906`) checks `"stdio"` against the handler that just wrote `"stdio"`. The honest repair is to thread the running transport into `handle_request`, whose call sites are `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs` (stdio), `src/transport/http.rs:407`, `:591` and `:1290`, plus the shmem loop — a signature and wire-value change in a crate published to crates.io and Docker Hub, so it is an operator decision, logged as task #490, not something smuggled in under "docs fix". The handler's whole body is those three reads plus one `debug!`; nothing is stored and nothing is forwarded, which the new section now says plainly instead of implying the streaming feature consumes it.

Re-run after this entry existed: **264 / 177 / 0 hard / 79 advisory / 8 allowed, exit 0**. The twelve-citation jump is this entry citing the code it describes, so the "252" figure belongs to the doc repair and the "264" figure to this file — publishing one number as the other's would be the self-perturbing-measurement trap, hence both are recorded with the run each describes. Two further runs exist because this file then edited itself twice more, and each step reconciles to the edit that caused it rather than being re-counted from scratch: **263 / 177 / 0 hard / 78 advisory / 8 allowed, exit 0** after the self-reference near the top of this batch was re-addressed by content instead of by line number (checked −1, advisory −1, populated unchanged — exactly one citation removed, no other movement), then **264 / 179 / 0 hard / 77 advisory / 8 allowed, exit 0** after five of this file's own anchors were re-pointed (checked +1 because a pointer that had been bare came with a path so the checker could see it at all; populated +2 as two re-pointed anchors landed on lines that carry the name the prose gives them; advisory −1 net, because one advisory became populated while the newly-visible pointer took its place). This sentence was itself measured mid-edit: an earlier draft spelled the error type as a code span, and that one token pushed two correct pointers on this very line from populated to advisory — 287 / 194 / 0 / 85 / 8 — because every anchor a citing line carries inherits the union of that line's code spans. Re-spelling it in prose restored 196/83, which is why the published figure is written as a run over the final text and not as a target this entry aims at. docs/API.md is LF-only and its table kept three cells per row, and this sentence adds no path-qualified span of its own (checked +12 over the previous published figure = 7 populated + 5 advisory: ten path-qualified spans inside that bullet's appended passage, every one landing on a populated line — the five that pass do so on a token the citing sentence supplies, and the five advisory ones are precisely the pointers this round re-quotes as drifted, which is evidence about the instrument rather than the anchor — plus the two this ledger sentence re-quotes with filenames while saying so, which is why the figure is written here as a measurement of the final text and not as a target the sentence aims at). Net for the pass: four anchors moved, one moved back, two historical annotations kept while naming their live positions alongside, and every tally delta above is one sentence, not a subtraction. The remaining 83 do not mean 83 suspect citations (5 of them are this round's own re-quoted drifted pointers, disclosed on the bullet that carries them): adjudicating the 78-row set that preceded these edits by pairing each backtick span with the anchor it sits beside, rather than the whole-line token union the checker uses, put 38 of them in the union artifact class, 7 in correct-anchor-but-off-by-one-to-two, and 33 needing a human read — which found the five drifts now repaired and left 4 of the 33 flagged but deliberately historical (the π-literal fixture sites, the classifier's pre-extraction line, and a doc pointer kept as a statement about the past). That bucket was wrong about its first member, and the correction is a measurement rather than a re-reading: `git show HEAD~1:src/protocol/nda_native.rs` carries that file's three π literals at `:2570`, `:2575` and `:2673` and the fixed tree at `:2619`, `:2624` and `:2757`, so the printed `:2605/2607/2705` matches no revision at all — drift, not history — and it now names its live positions on the clippy bullet itself (`src/protocol/nda_native.rs:2659` and `src/registry.rs:5406`), leaving 3 of the 33 historical by construction. Two structural limits decide that a correct anchor can still print advisory, and both were observed while repairing rather than predicted: bare `:NNN` pointers carry no filename so no regex that requires an extension will ever see them, and a span containing a colon yields no token at all, which is why prose that names a field by writing `name: value` cannot vouch for the line it cites. No Rust code changed in this entry, so no test run is claimed for it; the doc batch stays parked under #489 alongside the error-code and SDK hunks, since those still cite `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs` and `crates/velocity-mcp-core/src/lib.rs` lines that are themselves uncommitted. Re-measured 2026-10-11 after the error-classification bullet was re-pointed and its premise scoped: **300 / 209 / 0 / 83 / 8, exit 0**, reproduced byte-identically over two consecutive runs. That is checked +11 and populated +11 against the 289 / 198 the gate printed at the end of the previous pass, with advisory unchanged, because every pointer that passage added lands on a content line — the one advisory it briefly created was the closing brace at the end of the classifier's range, and citing the function head instead of a range whose tail is a brace removed it. The same passage now names its own five as-printed-then pointers as deliberately stale, which is the disclosure this ledger asks of every other historical anchor rather than leaving a reviewer to rediscover it. **Superseded within the same round by 300 / 208 / 0 / 84 / 8, exit 0**, also reproduced byte-identically over two runs: a later edit in this round rewrote the dashboard doc's CDP paragraph at a preserved line count, and one of the lines it rewrote is the very line this file's verification bullet points at as an example of a citation living inside untracked documentation. The pointer is still accurate — that file is still untracked and that citation is still in it — but the rewritten sentence carries no token that overlaps the thousands-of-characters citing row, so the keyword-adjacency channel moved one row from populated to advisory: the count changed and the finding did not. The reconciliation chosen is to republish the number, not to re-word the prose until a heuristic happens to match it; a gate that measures keyword adjacency cannot be satisfied by edits aimed at its keywords, and that row is already classified non-defective by the bullet named above. This sentence deliberately adds no path-qualified line pointer, so it cannot move the tally it reports.

- **Both NMCP opcode↔name tables mapped 8 of the 15 declared codes, so seven live method codes had no name in either direction — repaired, with an exhaustive round-trip test proven red against `HEAD`'s tables (2026-10-11).** `src/protocol/nda_native.rs:58-72` declares fifteen codes; `method_name` (`:976`) and `method_from_str` (`:997`) each carried eight arms plus a catch-all, which is a measured count rather than an impression — `git show HEAD:src/protocol/nda_native.rs` has **9** `=>` arrows in each function body (`awk '/^pub fn method_name/,/^}/'`), and the fixed tree has **16** in each. The seven missing were `0x07` resources/list, `0x08` resources/read, `0x09` resources/templates/list, `0x0A` prompts/list, `0x0B` prompts/get, `0x0C` `sampling/createMessage` and `0x12` notifications/progress — none of them theoretical: the binary dispatcher routes every one of them (`src/protocol/nmcp_binary.rs:287`, `:297`, `:311`, `:321`, `:331`, `:346`, `:366`), and the client crate's own table already names them (`client/src/transport/nda_codec.rs:33-51` maps fourteen names including `sampling/createMessage`). The two functions differ in blast radius, and the difference decides what kind of defect this was:
  - `method_name` has **exactly one** non-test call site — `src/protocol/nmcp_binary.rs:144`, `debug!(method = nda_native::method_name(req.method), "NDA-native request")` — so those seven requests reached the log as `method = "unknown"` while being dispatched correctly. That is an observability and audit-fidelity gap: the frames that were hardest to see in a log were the resource, prompt and sampling calls. Stated as the derivation it is: one call site (measured by grep) × seven unnamed codes (measured by arrow count); I did not capture a live `method = "unknown"` line, because reading it needs a debug-logging server run for a value the two measurements already fix.
  - `method_from_str` has **zero** non-test call sites, so nothing was ever mis-routed. It is a public function of a published crate, so the honest options were *complete it* or *break the API by deleting it*; it is completed. Behaviour of the completion: `method_from_str("resources/read")` and six other names move from `None` to `Some(code)`. No opcode value, header field, or frame byte changed, so this is not a wire-format change and needs no protocol-version movement.
  - The mechanism that let it drift is the same one that hides this whole class: `_ => "unknown"` and `_ => None` absorb an unlisted code silently, so adding a constant to the `:58-72` block costs nothing and fails nothing. The new `test_method_tables_cover_every_declared_opcode` replaces that with a gate: it walks a pinned fifteen-entry `(code, name)` table asserting `method_name(code) == name` **and** `method_from_str(name) == Some(code)`, then asserts `unknown`/`None` for the gaps that are deliberately absent (`0x0D`, `0x0E`, `0x0F`, `0x13`, `0xFF`, and `"resources/subscribe"`). Non-vacuity measured, not assumed: reverting **only** the two match bodies to HEAD's arms turned it red at the first assert, which in that reverted tree sits at line 2675 of `nda_native.rs` — fourteen lines nearer the top, because the two short bodies cut fourteen lines above the test. The failure read `assertion left == right failed: method_name(0x07)` / `left: "unknown"` / `right: "resources/list"`, exit **101**, and restoring the file from a hash-pinned copy (sha1 `340bd1a5…`, arrow counts back to 16/16, zero CR bytes) turned it green again. With the fix in place that assert is `src/protocol/nda_native.rs:2729`.
  - Docs corrected in the same pass, because `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` was the artifact that asserted the opposite of the code: line 79 claimed "`method_name` :976 and `method_from_str` :990 map both directions" — the second cite now reads `:997` (the fix shifted it by seven lines) and the sentence names the gate test so a reviewer can find the check rather than trust the prose. The `0x0C` row also listed the method as `sampling/create`; the JSON-RPC arm and the client codec both say `sampling/createMessage` (`src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:429`, `client/src/transport/nda_codec.rs:46`), and a grep for bare `sampling/create` across `*.md` now returns **0** hits. The table's other fourteen rows already named the methods correctly, which is how the gap was visible at all: the doc was right and the code was behind it.
  - Verification: `cargo +stable check --features http --lib` exit **0**; `cargo +stable test --features http --lib` **788 passed, 0 failed, 38 ignored** in 26.97 s; `cargo +stable test --features http --lib -- method` **13 passed** (826 in the target, 813 filtered); `cargo +stable fmt --all -- --check` exit **0**; the CI-exact `cargo +stable clippy --features http,database,oauth2 -- -D warnings` exit **0**. Not run, and not claimed: the full workspace suite and the three dashboard suites. The workspace build is out of reach on this disk — measured 2026-10-11 with `du -sh`: `target` is **23 G** (`target/debug/deps` 14 G) against **6.1 G free**, 99 % used on `C:`. An earlier round in this file quoted 25.9 GiB at 7.4 G free; that described that tree at that moment and is superseded by these figures. The dashboard suites gate HTTP/WASM tool behaviour, which this patch does not touch. Still open in the same surface, unchanged by this fix: `resources/subscribe`/`unsubscribe` are JSON-path-only arms (`src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:369`, `:387`) with no opcode at all, so "all fifteen codes now have names" must not be read as "every MCP method is reachable over NMCP binary".
- **All three SDK clients silently swallowed the servers' `resources/read` and `prompts/get` failures — repaired, with the same nine-arm classification table run red against `HEAD` and green against the working tree on every client (2026-10-10/11).** The defect was two-sided, because the two dispatchers report the same condition in two different shapes and neither client handled either one. `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:357` (`resources/read`) and `:425` (`prompts/get`) answer an `Err` with a **JSON-RPC success envelope** carrying `{"content":[{"type":"text","text":…}],"isError":true}` and no `error` object at all; `crates/velocity-mcp-core/src/lib.rs:332`/`:367` (the dispatcher Edge routes through) answer the same condition out of band with `-32001`. `HEAD`'s clients fed both straight into their result models, so: Python raised `pydantic_core.ValidationError` instead of any MCP error (the in-band body has no `contents`/`description` key), TypeScript returned the error envelope as a successful `ResourceReadResult`/`PromptGetResult`, and Go returned `(nil, nil)`-adjacent success values — a caller checking `err` saw a clean read of a resource that had failed. Measured red baselines on the identical nine-arm table: **Python 3/9** (four `ValidationError` arms + two leaked `ProtocolError: Protocol error -32001: …`, exit 1), **TypeScript 3/9** (four `returned` + two `ProtocolError`, exit 1), **Go 3/9** (same six arms, exit 1). After the fix all three are **9/9, exit 0**, and the Go leg now has a checked-in regression test: `sdk/go/classification_test.go` (new) prints the six named FAIL lines and exit 1 against `git show HEAD:sdk/go/client.go`, and `go test -count=1 ./...` → `ok … 1.496s`, `go build`/`go vet` exit 0 against the patched tree.
  - **This is convention alignment, not new semantics.** The rule applied to both shapes: core's `-32001` → the typed not-found class; a native in-band result whose text is the not-found sentence → the typed not-found class; any other in-band cause → the existing generic server-error class; every other protocol code passes through untouched. Both the class and the pattern already existed in each file — Python's `call_tool` keys on `tool_result.is_error`, and Go's client already returns `&ServerError{…}` for the six invalid-result paths (`sdk/go/client.go:107`, `:140`, `:145`, `:185`, `:241`, `:246`) — so no new error type was introduced anywhere. The `-32601` passthrough arm is the negative control that proves the widening is not a blanket conversion.
  - **Why the in-band text is discriminated rather than trusted.** The native message body is `sandbox::sanitize_error(&e)` over a `Result<_, String>`, so it carries *any* cause, not only not-found; blanket-labelling it `ResourceNotFoundError` would have mis-reported real failures. The discriminator is the exact sentence both servers emit for the not-found case — `src/resources.rs:239`/`:358` and core `:332` produce `"Resource not found: <uri>"`, `src/resources.rs:806` and core `:367` produce `"Prompt not found: <name>"` — and the two non-not-found arms in the table are **captured server text**, not invented: `Failed to open file: The system cannot find the path specified. (os error 3)` and `Missing required argument: topic`, both read off the stdio loop of `target/debug/velocity_mcp.exe` (exe mtime 2026-10-10T21:31, newer than the newest source it cites at 20:04). Each is asserted to land in `ServerError` **and** to *not* satisfy the not-found class.
  - **Message fidelity is deliberately asymmetric across the three clients**, because the error classes differ: Python's `ResourceNotFoundError`/`PromptNotFoundError` are `McpError` subclasses that take a message verbatim, so Python surfaces the server's text; TypeScript's constructors build the string from the identifier (`Resource not found: ${uri}`) and Go's carry `URI`/`Name` fields with their own `Error()`, so those two surface the identifier instead. Documented in the Python docstrings rather than smoothed over.
  - **Verification limits, stated rather than assumed.** The TypeScript leg is runtime-only: `tsc` cannot run because there is no `node_modules` in `sdk/typescript`, so **the types are not checked** — the harness ran a temp copy of `client.ts` under Node 24.19 type-stripping, with the only edits being import-specifier rewrites (`./transport` → `import type … "./transport.ts"`, same for `./types`/`./errors`); the classification logic is byte-identical to what is committed. The Python and Go legs ran the committed files directly (Go needed no network: `gorilla/websocket v1.5.1` resolves from the module cache). Every harness lives in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Temp\velo_round3\`, not in the repo.
  - **What this fix does not close, because each is an operator call:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` has **no job for any of the three SDKs** (its three jobs run `cargo fmt`/`clippy`/`build`/`test`/`audit`/`fuzz_tests` only), so `sdk/go/classification_test.go` gates nothing in CI and is run by hand; worse, it **cannot be run by a fresh clone at all**, because `sdk/go` has no tracked `go.sum` (`git ls-files sdk/go` returns README/client/errors/go.mod/transport/types only, and the file is untracked here) — a `GOPROXY=off go test ./...` over exactly those tracked files plus the new test fails at setup with `transport.go:13:2: missing go.sum entry for module providing package github.com/gorilla/websocket`, which is a pre-existing condition of the package, not of the test, and its remedy belongs to the same operator decision as the `go.mod` module-path change already pending in this batch; `gofmt -l sdk/go` still flags `transport.go` and `types.go` (the two files this change does not touch); `sdk/go/client.go:13` still declares `ClientVersion = "3.0.0"` while the Python and TypeScript clients declare `3.2.0` (`sdk/python/velocity_mcp_client/client.py:28`, `sdk/typescript/src/client.ts:27`) — the leftover is attributable: `fb610d8` bumped the Rust, Python and TypeScript pins and never touched `sdk/go`; and `resources/subscribe`/`resources/unsubscribe` answer in band the same way (`src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:383`, `:401`) but no SDK exposes those methods, so there is nothing to repair there yet. The SDKs are **not** published (PyPI/npm 404, measured), so no existing consumer is affected by the behaviour change — unlike the server and core crates, which are on crates.io and Docker Hub; the server-side half of this finding (the docs surface) is its own bullet below.
- **`docs/API.md` advertised six custom JSON-RPC error codes and four of them have never existed.** `git show HEAD:docs/API.md` lines 859–864 listed `-32000` Server error, `-32001` **Unknown tool**, `-32002` Tool execution failed, `-32003` Resource not found, `-32004` Prompt not found, `-32005` Sampling failed. The workspace emits two custom codes: a grep for `-32002`, `-32003`, `-32004`, `-32005` across `src/`, `crates/`, `client/`, `sdk/`, `tests/` and `benches/` returns **zero producers**, and `-32001` is named wrongly — it is core's not-found code, never an unknown-tool signal. The real reason a client sees no code for a failed `tools/call` is that the native dispatcher answers failures **in band**: `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:325` (shared `tools/call` tail), `:357`, `:383`, `:401`, `:425` build a JSON-RPC *success* envelope whose `result` carries MCP's `isError` flag, so there is no `error` object and no code to document. Repaired in `docs/API.md` (the two-row table at `:1007`/`:1008` now states what actually produces each code, the absence claim sits at `:1010`, and the in-band mechanism is spelled out over `:1010`–`:1017`), and the correction is **measured rather than read off the enum**: feeding five failing requests to the stdio loop of a binary built from the current tree returned `isError: true` for the resource and prompt lookups, `-32601` for the unknown method, `-32603` for sampling, and no `-3200x` in any response. The doc now carries that capture plus the `printf … | velocity_mcp.exe` command to re-measure it. Every file:line in the repaired section was re-opened and confirmed to land on the cited construct, which is how the one surviving error in it was found and fixed: the in-band list cited `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:402` (a closing `})),`) for the `resources/unsubscribe` envelope, whose body is at `:401`.
- **The shipped Grafana dashboard could never have been provisioned, and three of its panel expressions keyed on series this server has not ever exported.** The oracle was the exporter itself: `src/transport/http.rs` is the only file in the tree that emits a `# TYPE` line (gated, not assumed — the sweep over every Rust file outside `target/` and `sdk/` returns that one path), and a live `GET /v1/metrics/prometheus` against the running server yields the same seven series the code builds, so the census is not a paraphrase of either source. Against it, the dashboard JSON queried `velocity_mcp_sessions_active`, `velocity_mcp_plugins_total` and `velocity_mcp_plugins_installed` across two panels, none of which appears anywhere in `src/`, `crates/` or `client/`, while three series the server does export (`velocity_mcp_auth_failures_total`, `velocity_mcp_latency_microseconds`, `velocity_mcp_sse_connections_active`) had no panel at all. The larger defect was structural: compose mounted `deploy/grafana/dashboards` at `/etc/grafana/provisioning/dashboards`, which is the directory Grafana scans for *provider* YAML rather than for dashboards, and no provider file existed anywhere in the repository (a grep for the `providers` key over every YAML in the tree, tracked and untracked, returns only the file this fix adds). So `docker compose --profile monitoring up` would have started Grafana with a working datasource and zero dashboards, and no edit a dashboard author made to the shipped JSON would ever have appeared. Repaired on the configuration side only: a provider at a new `deploy/grafana/provisioning/dashboards/velocity-mcp.yml` (apiVersion 1, type file, `options.path` at `/var/lib/grafana/dashboards`, disableDeletion true, updateIntervalSeconds 30, allowUiUpdates false); the two compose mounts retargeted so that path is where the JSON actually lands, read-only; and the three dead expressions repointed at the three exported-but-unshown series, which makes every panel query a name that exists and every exported name visible on some panel. The API-payload wrapper `{"dashboard": …, "overwrite": true}` was deliberately left as shipped: Grafana documents that shape for provisioned files, and flattening it to a bare dashboard model would have broken the `POST` to `/api/dashboards/db` that the deployment guide tells readers to run against this same file. Verification was a throwaway gate over the rendered configuration rather than a container — **34 checks, real exit 0** — pinning source census == live payload, referenced series being a subset of exported *and* exported a subset of referenced, `rate()` appearing exactly three times with every use wrapping a counter (five of the seven series are counters, two are gauges, and the average-latency gauge must never be rate-averaged), provider `options.path` equalling the destination of the mount that supplies the dashboard JSON, the provider directory holding only YAML while the dashboard directory holds only JSON, the scrape `metrics_path` being a route the router actually serves, and the alert census re-derived rule-by-rule from each rule's own comment block instead of counting the word UNFIREABLE, which the file's own header also contains — a naive word count says **6**, the correct per-rule count is **5**. Five of the 34 are negative controls that replay the pre-fix inputs (the three deleted expressions, a `rate()` over the average-latency gauge, the old mount string, a provider aimed at its own scan directory) and all five read red, so the green is not vacuous. **What remains unproven, stated plainly:** no Grafana container was started, so nothing here is an observed dashboard sync — the daemon was down (`docker info` fails on the named pipe) and the free space on this volume would not safely cover a Grafana-plus-Prometheus pull, so the gate asserts the documented contract, not runtime behaviour. The seven panels also keep Grafana's legacy `graph` type and none carries an explicit datasource, which resolves to the provisioned default by documented behaviour but was not rendered here; converting them is a change to make against a live Grafana, not a guess. This bullet supersedes two clauses of the alerting round below, which recorded the provider file and the dead panels as open tasks and declined to ship a config whose effect it could not observe; the config now ships with its contract gated and the container proof still outstanding. The alert files are untouched and remain accurate: eleven rules, still unloaded, five keying on absent series, two on external `container_*` exporters, four able to fire. `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` gained the provisioning and alerting reality (its line count is free to move — it carries no inbound citations) and the user guide's panel list and closing FAQ now describe the fixed state line-count-neutrally (1181 lines, 0 CR) so the guide's three inbound citations still land. Citation census for the batch unmoved at **217 checked / 154 populated / 0 hard / 55 advisory / 8 allowed, exit 0**.

- **Three of the four documented SDK install commands named a package that its registry does not have.** Measured per ecosystem against the live registry and the repo's own manifest, not against the prose. **Go — never importable, and now structurally correct:** the repository is `github.com/UnitBuilds-CC/V.E.L.O.C.I.T.Y.-MCP` (its own `origin` remote), while `sdk/go/go.mod` declared the module as `github.com/UnitBuilds-CC/velocity-mcp/sdk/go`. Both halves were probed with the real toolchain: the declared path fails at resolution (`git ls-remote` → "Repository not found"), and the repo's actual path reaches the repository through the public proxy — it resolves to pseudo-version `v0.0.0-20260917231518-c28d72ef7abf` — then aborts in `go get` with Go's own verdict, "module declares its path as … but was required as …". So the mismatch was not cosmetic: no consumer could ever have imported this SDK, and no existing consumer can be broken by correcting it. The declaration is fixed and the eight documentation lines that carried the wrong specifier now match it (`sdk/go/README.md` ×3, `README.md` ×2, `docs/CLIENT_INTEGRATION.md` ×2, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ×1); `go build ./...` inside `sdk/go` exits **0** on the corrected tree. **Disclosed limit:** the `go get` line only starts working for other people once this is committed *and pushed*, because the proxy and GitHub serve the committed `go.mod` — the current `main` and that September commit both still declare the old path (read from raw.githubusercontent). `sdk/go/go.sum` now exists (4 lines, written by that build) but is **untracked**, and its status needs the distinction I first got wrong: a *downstream* consumer's own module verifies the dependency hashes, so nothing was ever broken for an importer — but the module root itself needs a lockfile, and re-measured 2026-10-11 from `git archive HEAD` of only the tracked `sdk/go` files, `go build ./...` there exits **1** on "missing go.sum entry for module providing package github.com/gorilla/websocket", at line 13 column 2 of that SDK's transport file. So the honest claim is scoped: importers were fine, and building the SDK from a clean clone was not. The file's own integrity is proven rather than assumed — `go mod verify` over those four recorded lines prints "all modules verified" and exits **0** — so committing it is a pure gain, and is still the operator's call as part of the same logical group as the `go.mod` fix. **Python and TypeScript — authored, never published:** `pip install velocity-mcp-client` and `npm install @velocity-mcp/client` (plus its `yarn add` twin) hit **404** on PyPI and npm respectively, while `sdk/python/pyproject.toml` and `sdk/typescript/package.json` both declare exactly those names at version 3.2.0. Each README's install section now says there is no registry package and gives the path that works, keeping the import specifiers readers' code already uses (the Python module name matches its package directory through `[tool.setuptools.packages.find]`, so `from velocity_mcp_client import …` is correct once installed from source; installing the built TypeScript directory registers it under `@velocity-mcp/client`, so its examples stay valid). **Rust is the control, and it passes:** `cargo install velocity_mcp` is the published crate at 3.2.0. Two measurement lessons attached to that control: crates.io answers **403** to a User-Agent-less `curl` and 200 to the same request with one, so a bare probe looks like an empty registry — which is exactly how this crate was mis-read earlier in this file — and crates.io's lookup folds `-` into `_`, meaning the API answers for `velocity-mcp` while Cargo requires the underscore spelling the docs do use. **A packaging defect found but deliberately not edited:** `sdk/typescript/package.json` ships `files` = `dist/**/*` + `README.md` with `main` pointing into `dist/`, and `dist/` is produced only by `npm run build`, yet no `prepare`/`prepack`/`prepublishOnly` hook exists — so `npm pack --dry-run` packs **2 files and no JavaScript**. The repair is a one-line script hook, but it only fires at publish time, cannot be verified without installing the crate's full dev-dependency tree on a volume holding ~9.7 G, and publishing is the operator's decision; it is recorded here instead of applied. **A hazard this round's own probe created:** `pip install --dry-run ./sdk/python` wrote `sdk/python/velocity_mcp_client.egg-info/` **into the source tree**, where it sat untracked and *not* ignored — one `git add -A` from being committed. Its mtime dated it to minutes rather than months, so it was this probe's artifact and was removed; `.gitignore` gained an `*.egg-info/` rule at its tail, confirmed to fire with `git check-ignore -v`, because the README repaired in this same round now tells every reader to run `pip install -e .` in that directory. The one tracked place the old Go path still appears is this file's own dated entry about the earlier SDK-sample fix, which quotes it as a finding rather than prescribing it, and is left as written.

- **Every documented install path for the current release pointed at a download that answers 404.** Found while census-ing `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` against the tracked deploy assets, then confirmed against GitHub rather than the prose: `gh release list` shows the latest published release is **v3.1.0 with exactly one asset, `velocity_mcp.exe`**, and `gh release view v3.2.0` reports the release does not exist. The install sections nevertheless told readers to fetch `velocity_mcp`, `velocity_mcp_linux_x86_64` and `velocity_mcp_macos_arm64` from `releases/latest/download/…`; measured per name with `curl -sIL`, those three return **404** while `velocity_mcp.exe` returns **200**. The names cannot match even in principle, because the release workflow renames each artifact with the version and a per-platform suffix (`win_x64.exe`, `linux_x64`, `macos_x64`), so an unversioned name is never what gets uploaded and there is no Apple-silicon asset at all. Repaired doc-side rather than by inventing assets: the pre-built-binaries section now states which asset resolves, gives the one command that re-measures it (`gh release view --json tagName,assets`, printing the tag then each asset name), and points at the two paths that actually work for the current version — `cargo install velocity_mcp`, verified against crates.io (the API returns newest and max stable **3.2.0**, not yanked, on a request that carries a User-Agent; a bare `curl` without one gets a non-JSON answer, which is why the first check here looked like an empty crate), and `docker pull unitbuilds/velocity-mcp:latest`, which is what `README.md` already documented. The same blocks in `README.md` and `docs/GETTING_STARTED.md` were carrying the identical dead URLs and are corrected the same way; the Windows-only `curl` was kept because it is the one that succeeds, and `docs/GETTING_STARTED.md`'s twelve `./velocity_mcp` invocations became `velocity_mcp`, since the install step puts the binary on `PATH`. **Why v3.2.0 has no release:** that workflow run (2026-09-11) failed at the `Build release binary` step on ubuntu-latest with repeated `error[E0433]: cannot find module or crate libc in this scope`, which came from the Linux seccomp sandbox module; the `libc = "0.2"` entry in that target block was added three days later (2026-09-14, commit `88a92de`), so the cause is fixed on the current tree but was never re-run. **Publishing these assets means pushing a new tag, which is the operator's call** — no tag was pushed and no workflow was edited here.
- **`scripts/quickstart.sh` could not install on any platform, and its own usage line pointed at a path that is not in the repository.** The script built its download URL from a hard-coded `VERSION="3.0.0"` plus a name pattern of its own invention, then appended `.exe` for Windows. Every one of those URLs was measured: the pattern resolves to 404 under both tags that exist (`velocity_mcp_v3.0.0_linux_x64`, `_macos_x64`, `_win_x64.exe`, `velocity_mcp_v3.1.0_linux_x64`), because the assets actually published under those tags are named `velocity_mcp-v3.0.0-windows-x86_64.exe` (+ `SHA256SUMS.txt`) and `velocity_mcp.exe` — a third and a fourth naming convention, neither of which the script or the release workflow uses. So the defect is the URL template, not the stale version pin, and **no value of `VERSION` makes the old template correct on any platform**. Reproduced from a clean local clone, which failed exactly as predicted with a bare `curl: (22) … 404` and exit 22. The script now reads the download URL out of the release's own asset list (`/releases/latest` for the tag unless `VELOCITY_VERSION` pins one, then `/releases/tags/<tag>` filtered by a per-platform pattern) instead of constructing one, and when a release genuinely has no asset for the reader's platform it prints the files that release does publish and the two install paths that work today, then exits 1 with a named reason rather than a raw curl abort. Its header comment also told users to pipe `…/main/quickstart.sh`, which is 404 — the tracked file is at `scripts/quickstart.sh`, and that raw URL answers 200. Verified by running it, not by reading it: the win branch against the latest tag downloaded the 8,472,064-byte published binary, and feeding that install an `initialize` request returned a well-formed response from **serverInfo version 3.1.0** with exit 0 — which independently confirms the published release binary is one version behind the tree's 3.2.0; the linux and macos branches were driven with a stubbed `uname` against tags `v3.0.0`, `v3.1.0` and a non-existent one, printing the three-way gate correctly, and `bash -n` passes on both the LF working file and a CRLF copy. Checked and found **not** to be a defect: `core.autocrlf=true` with no `.gitattributes` in the repo means a fresh Windows clone does check every `*.sh` out with CRLF (measured in the throwaway clone: 64 CR in a 64-line file), but that turns out not to break execution here — the same CRLF copy resolved the platform, matched the arch case, and produced byte-identical gate output, so no line-ending rule was added on a theory. `--mode http --addr 0.0.0.0:3000` was checked against the router and is correct; the `/performance` hint this bullet first recorded as correct was **not** — probed against the running server, `/performance` returns 404 while `/v1/performance` returns 200, and that class of omission is the subject of the bullet below.

- **Eleven documented URLs in six files pointed at native HTTP endpoints the router only serves under `/v1`.** `src/transport/http.rs` assembles one `protected` router holding every functional route, then mounts it with `.route("/health", …)` outside and `.nest("/v1", protected)` — so `/health` is the only unversioned path on the server. Confirmed against the running instance rather than from the nesting alone: `/performance`, `/metrics` and `/mcp` each answer **404**, while `/v1/performance` and `/v1/metrics` answer **200**, `/v1/mcp` answers 400 to an empty POST and **200 with a real tool list** to the `tools/list` body the example sends, and `/v1/ws` answers 400 to a bare upgrade request (route present) where `/ws` answers 404. Repaired in six tracked files: `examples/http-server.sh` had the failing `tools/list`, `file_read` and `shell_exec` posts plus its performance curl, so the shipped HTTP example could not have completed a single one of its four requests; `examples/README.md`'s endpoint list and `scripts/quickstart.sh`'s closing hint both pointed at 404s; `sdk/go/README.md` passed `ws://localhost:3000/ws` to `NewWebSocketTransport` in two places; and `compare_native_vs_edge.sh` set `NATIVE_URL` to the unversioned path. Every other URL in the repo already carried the prefix — `docs/API.md`, `docs/CLIENT_INTEGRATION.md`, `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`, `client/README.md`, the Python/TypeScript SDKs, and `deploy/prometheus/prometheus.yml`, whose `metrics_path` is `/v1/metrics/prometheus` — which is what makes these omissions defects rather than a disagreement about convention. The generated wiki page `.qoder/repowiki/en/content/Development Guide.md` carried the same dead curl and is corrected here, though it is a build artifact that will be re-derived from the tree on the next knowledge pass. Deliberately **not** changed: `compare_native_vs_edge.sh`'s `EDGE_URL`, because the Edge crate dispatches on its own `path == "/mcp"` test (its 404 fallback fires for anything else) and therefore genuinely serves the unversioned path — the two sides of that comparison have different URL shapes, which the script's reader would otherwise have been entitled to treat as a typo. Probe hazard found the hard way: GET on a streaming route such as `/v1/sse` holds the socket open, so it hangs a bare `curl` and blew a two-minute tool timeout; every bounded probe in this round carries `--max-time`.

- **Four further statements in the deployment guide contradicted the files they describe.** Each was checked by reading the source of truth rather than the other doc: the Docker section's example `CMD` now matches the tracked image recipe, which names only `--mode http` — the listen address therefore comes from the built-in default `0.0.0.0:3000`, which is exactly what that recipe's `EXPOSE 3000` advertises, and the guide no longer implies the image takes an address flag it never passes. The `[features]` config block gained the disclosure that **only `nda_merkle` changes behaviour**: `database`, `oauth2` and `http` are parsed and ignored, because those three are selected at build time by the Cargo features of the same names — measured by listing every read of `FeaturesConfig` outside the config module, which returns that one field plus its default. The database-scaling section said to enable SQLite by setting the config key; it now says the switch is the build-time feature and that the only live key in that section is the Merkle toggle, and the backup script's database-path comment no longer names a config key that does not exist — the file is whatever the embedding application passed to `velocity_mcp::resources::set_database_path()`, where the first call wins and later ones are ignored. The page footer still carried `Last updated: 2026-08-30`; it reads 2026-10-10. Verified-true and deliberately untouched: the env-var block, the systemd/launchd/NSSM units, the openssl and certbot TLS steps and their "both flags must be present together" claim, logrotate, the metrics and audit curls, the `AuditEntry` JSON example, the startup-rejected-values list, and the hard-coded 30-minute session TTL with its explicit session-delete call.

- **Five of the eleven shipped Prometheus alert rules can never fire, and the file holding them is not loaded at all.** Established by listing what the server exports and then reading every rule: the Prometheus text handler emits exactly seven series — requests total, successful and failed, auth failures, rate-limit hits, an average-latency gauge and active SSE connections — while `deploy/prometheus/alerts.yml` queries a duration histogram, a TLS certificate-expiry gauge, a plugin-install-failure counter and an audit-buffer occupancy gauge. Grepping each of those names across `src/` and `crates/` returns nothing, so those five expressions evaluate over empty vectors and would stay silent on a broken server; two more read `container_*` series that only a cAdvisor-style exporter supplies, and the compose stack defines exactly three services (`velocity-mcp`, `prometheus`, `grafana`). The finding that decides how much of that matters today is the wiring: `deploy/prometheus/prometheus.yml` declares no `rule_files:` list and compose mounts only the scrape config, so the whole file is never loaded and even its four healthy rules evaluate nowhere. **Annotated rather than rewritten, deliberately**: the p95 histogram and the average gauge measure different quantities, so retyping the latency rule as a threshold on the mean would swap a tail test for an average test under an unchanged label, and adding `rule_files` plus a mount would start firing real notifications on a deployment that fires none today — both operator calls. Each unfireable rule now carries a comment naming the missing series, the file's header records the wiring it lacks and states that its expressions were left as written, `deploy/prometheus/prometheus.yml` says it loads no rules, and the list in `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` that presented all eleven as available pre-configured rules now splits them four live / five impossible / two external. Same pass, two more provable doc divergences: that guide's Grafana bullet advertised an "Active Sessions" panel that queries a sessions gauge no handler exports (the dashboard JSON holds seven panels, of which two query three series that do not exist — **Active Sessions** on a sessions gauge, **Plugin Statistics** on `velocity_mcp_plugins_installed` plus `velocity_mcp_plugins_total` — recorded as an open task rather than annotated blind, because panel behaviour cannot be verified without the release binary a relink needs the operator to unlock), and `deploy/kubernetes/deployment.yml` pulled `velocity-mcp:latest`, a tag no workflow builds (`release.yml` publishes binaries, not images) and no document names, so it now pulls the image `README.md` documents, with a note that a local tag only resolves on a node that can see it. Two further doc lines in the same pass were measured before being touched: `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`'s environment section told readers to use "`VELOCITY_API_KEY` (and the other variables below)" above a sample that names eight of the ten variables the config layer actually reads, so it now says the config layer reads ten fixed names and sends the reader to the user guide's table (`src/config.rs`'s override block is the source of that count), and the guide's closing FAQ, which presented both dashboards and alerting rules as simply "included", now states that the rules are not loaded and that two of the seven panels query series that do not exist. The deployment guide's Grafana steps were the worst line in the section: they sent the reader to a third-party community dashboard ID for a metric namespace only this project defines, and never once named the dashboard JSON the repository itself ships (grep the file for its own path and the old text returned nothing), so that section now names the shipped file, gives the API post against the port compose maps for Grafana, states plainly that two panels ask for gauges the handler does not export, and records that the directory mounted at the dashboard-provisioning path contains no provider file — the sibling datasource directory does, which is how the asymmetry was caught. **Those last two descriptions of `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` are themselves stale now**: the round that shipped the provider also removed the empty-panel sentence when it repointed the expressions, so that section describes the fixed state and the asymmetry survives only as this file's history. Adding that provider file is left as a task, not a repair: provisioning cannot be exercised while the Docker daemon is down (`docker info` fails on the named pipe), and shipping a config file whose effect I could not observe is exactly the kind of claim this round exists to stop making. **Superseded on those two points by the first bullet of this section**, which ships the provider file, retargets the mounts and repoints the dead panel expressions — while the same bullet keeps this one's discipline: the observed container sync it refused to fake is still not observed, and what stands in its place is a configuration-contract gate. All edits to the cited guide were line-count-neutral (1181 lines, 0 CR bytes, and the config parenthetical a changelog self-reference points at still sits on its documented line), the four touched YAML files re-parse (`safe_load_all` still reads six documents from the Kubernetes manifest and eleven rules from the alert file), and **this round's insertions hold the census fixed point at 217 checked / 154 populated / 0 hard failures / 55 advisory / 8 allowed, real exit 0**.

- **The shipped monitoring and compose assets could not reach the server they deploy.** Found by reading the router assembly rather than the prose: every route except `/health` is registered on one `protected` router that is mounted under `/v1`, so the Prometheus text endpoint is `/v1/metrics/prometheus`, while `deploy/prometheus/prometheus.yml` scraped `/metrics` and the Kubernetes pod annotation advertised `/metrics` — a scrape that answers 404 forever. `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` already documented the correct path, so the deploy files contradicted both the code and the project's own guide. Both files now carry the working path, and the Prometheus config gained a comment naming the bearer-token middleware, because a key-protected `/v1` route is not scrapeable without an `authorization` block. The compose file was worse than a wrong path: `docker compose -f deploy/docker-compose.yml --profile monitoring config` resolved its build context to `deploy/`, where no `Cargo.toml` exists, and its bind sources to `deploy/deploy/grafana/…`, `deploy/deploy/prometheus/prometheus.yml` and `deploy/plugins` — none of which exist, because Compose resolves relative paths against the directory holding the file. It now builds the repository root with `dockerfile: deploy/Dockerfile` and mounts the real directories; re-rendered with the same command, every resolved source exists on disk, the obsolete-`version` warning is gone, and all three files re-parse (`yaml.safe_load_all` reads six documents from the Kubernetes manifest, exit 0).
- **The deployment guide taught a different Docker build than the one the repository ships.** Its Docker section showed a hard-pinned `rust:1.75` builder, no `--bin`, no non-root user and no healthcheck, and told the reader to run `docker build -t velocity-mcp .` from the repository root — which cannot work, because the only tracked `Dockerfile` is `deploy/Dockerfile` (`git ls-files` returns exactly that one, plus the root `.dockerignore`). The section now names the shipped recipe with `-f`, describes what it actually does (workspace-manifest staging layer, floating `rust:1-bookworm` builder, `velocity` user, `curl` health-check on `/health`, `plugins/` baked in), and documents the compose entry point with its `monitoring` profile, which no guide mentioned before. The `- Rust 1.75+` prerequisite line was replaced rather than defended: no manifest in the workspace declares a `rust-version`, and I measured no floor, so the guide now states only what the shipped recipe records — that the floor drifts upward as locked dependencies raise theirs, which is why the image floats its toolchain instead of pinning it.
- **`VELOCITY_PLUGIN_DIR` is set in three deploy files and read by nothing** — a grep of `src/` and `crates/` for that name returns zero hits, `apply_env_overrides` has no branch for it, and the live knob is the root `plugin_dir` config key, consumed at startup by `registry::load_plugins`. The containers still load plugins because `WORKDIR /app` plus the default value `plugins` already resolve to the directory the image bakes, so the variable was decorative, not harmful. Annotated as decorative in the `Dockerfile`, the compose file and the Kubernetes manifest instead of deleted: making it real means adding an env override to a published crate, which is new semantics under an unchanged version, so that wiring is an operator call.
- **CI's lint gate covers less than its build and test gates** (recorded, not repaired): `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `cargo clippy --features http,database,oauth2 --all-targets -- -D warnings` from the workspace root without `--workspace`, while the build and test steps below it do pass `--workspace`, so the four sibling crates are built and tested but never lint-gated — an asymmetry the still-uncommitted tranche itself created, since that diff is what added `--all-targets` to clippy and `--workspace` to the build and test steps. **I first scoped this as unaffordable to measure** — a full dependency-graph compile with C: at 98 percent — **and the premise failed the moment I ran it**: the widened command on the current tree finished in 2m 11s (log kept outside the repo, 38 warning lines) and attributes 36 of them to one target, the edge crate's integration tests, where 32 are a single lint that clippy offers to rewrite mechanically and 4 are assertions the compiler proves always true; that run exits 0 only because it omits `-D warnings`, so adding `--workspace` to the CI step today would turn it red on lint debt the step has never looked at. Clearing the debt first is the operator's call rather than a line I can change and leave green. The rest of the workflow census found nothing to repair — `release.yml` names `velocity_mcp` and `velocity_mcp.exe`, which match the package bin, and its root-only build/test is fine because CI already covers the workspace; `deploy-edge.yml`'s `WASM_TARGET` and `BIN_NAME` chain matches what `deploy/build-edge.py` copies, and `wasmer.toml` names the same `velocity-edge` module; every file those workflows invoke (`deploy/build-edge.py`, `deploy/edge-wasix.lock`, `tests/fuzz_tests.rs`, all five workspace manifests) exists.
- **Two "admin" audit endpoints were a second name for the same bytes, the `admin` namespace gated nothing, and a failed flush said 200.** Found by closing the route census rather than by reading prose: four of the nine index-only audit rows in `docs/API.md` describe two pairs of handlers — `audit_export_json` / `admin_audit_export_json` and the CSV equivalents — whose bodies the table implied differ ("Audit entries, all sessions" versus "Merged audit entries from all sessions"). Read side by side, each pair is identical except for the function name: both call `audit_registry().aggregate_all()`, both serialize with `serde_json::to_string_pretty` (or the same `format_audit_csv`), both return the same `Content-Type`, so the responses are byte-identical and the documented distinction does not exist. The wider point is the naming: all of these routes are registered on one `protected` router inside `build_router`, which carries a single `auth_middleware` layer for every route under the API version prefix and is nested exactly once — there is no role check, no second credential and no separate layer anywhere in the chain, so `/v1/admin/audit/*` asks precisely what `/v1/audit/*` asks. Third: `audit_flush` and `session_audit_flush` both take the registry's `Err` path and return `Ok(Json({status:"error", error:…}))`, which axum renders as **HTTP 200**, so a client that checks the status code alone records a failed disk flush as a successful one; only the `status` field distinguishes them. **Repaired as documentation, because that is the part I can change truthfully**: the four rows now state the pairs return the identical aggregate body, that the admin path adds no privilege, and that both flush routes answer 200 on failure with the error in the body — kept line-count-neutral (3 rows then 3 rows, then 3 more) and audited at 4 cells per row against the 4-column header, so the table structure and every `path:NNN` citation in the tree are untouched; the census held its fixed point through all three doc edits — 218 checked / 155 resolving to a populated line / 0 hard failures / 55 advisory / 8 allowed, real exit 0 each time. **Then the checker caught a drift this very edit caused:** inserting three bullets at the top of *Fixed* moved a self-referential line anchor inside this file (the pointer at old line ninety-one, aimed at the bullet that holds the deliberately-historical HTTP-server citation) three lines down, and the tally went 155 populated → 154, 55 advisory → 56 with the total unchanged — the exact failure mode the citation-rot bullet above describes, reproduced on my own edit within one round. Fixed by content-anchoring that self-reference, which removes one token from the corpus, so the figures this round leaves behind are **217 checked / 154 populated / 0 hard failures / 55 advisory / 8 allowed, real exit 0**, and those are the numbers to compare against next time, not the 218/155 pair quoted earlier in this paragraph. Stating them cost two more iterations, because the sentence that explained the fix named the broken anchor in `path:line` form and re-created it, and a figure written into a file that the same checker counts moves that figure — so this paragraph is deliberately token-free and the numbers in it are the ones the run after it reproduces. **What this does not do, deliberately:** merging the duplicate handlers, dropping the `admin` prefix, or making a failed flush return 5xx are behaviour changes to a published crate — the first two delete routes a client may already call, and the third flips a response code any client that today parses `{"status":"error"}` from a 200 would start seeing as a transport error. Those need an operator decision, so they are recorded as open rather than fixed here.
- **Four configuration keys the user guide documented as switches were parsed, stored and then never read** — `[features].database`, `[features].oauth2`, `[features].http` and root `csharp_path`. Established by reading every access site, not by absence of evidence: the only `FeaturesConfig` field the server consumes is `nda_merkle` (two reads in `src/main.rs` plus its `VELOCITY_NDA_MERKLE` override in `apply_env_overrides`), and the `csharp_path` *field* is referenced nowhere outside `src/config.rs` at all — declaration, default, the env override that writes it and one unit test asserting that write are its only sites in the crate, while every runtime consumer (the benchmark harness, `main.rs`'s startup log line, and the dynamic-tool call paths in `src/registry.rs`) calls `resolve_csharp_path()` instead, which reads `VELOCITY_CSHARP_PATH` from the environment directly and falls back to the crate's own default constant, never consulting the configuration file. So `[features].http = true` did nothing, `[features] database`/`oauth2` did nothing, and a `csharp_path` in `config.toml` was silently overridden by nothing. The real switches for the first three are the cargo build features of the same names declared in `Cargo.toml`'s `[features]` block (with `default = []`, so they are compiled out unless passed to `--features`), plus `--mode http` for the transport itself. Repaired in `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`: the four sample comments now say *inert: parsed, never read* and name the live switch, the `nda_merkle` comment now records that it is the only `[features]` key the server reads, and the paragraph that already warned "a key the server does not recognise is ignored" now carries the reverse case — a recognised key can be equally dead. **This is a documentation repair, not a behaviour change**: no key was removed and no parsing changed, which matters because the crate is published — deleting the fields would break every existing `config.toml` that sets them, while reading them would change runtime behaviour under the same version. Edit was line-count-neutral by construction (three replacements of 1→1, 5→5 and 1→1 lines) and verified as such by the citation census holding its fixed point — 218 citations checked, 155 resolving to a populated line, **0 hard failures**, 55 advisory, 8 allowed, real exit 0, identical to the pre-edit run — that pairing is the measurement at this point in the round, and the audit-route bullet above records where the tally finished — with `tr -dc '\r' | wc -c` reporting 0 CR bytes across the file, so no CRLF churn rode along.
