- **The config-surface census, and the two instrument bugs that nearly made it lie.** `config_census.py` (in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Temp`, deliberately not in the repo) reconciled three surfaces against the docs with 28 self-check gates, all true on the final run: **config keys 41 implemented = 17 documented + 24 undocumented**, **environment variables 17 read (10 of them by the config layer) = 17 documented, 0 undocumented, 0 phantom**, **CLI flags 9 implemented = 9 documented, 0 phantom**, plus `deny_unknown_fields` count 0 anywhere in `src/`, which is what makes the guide's "unrecognised keys are ignored, not rejected" claim true rather than hopeful. The 24 undocumented keys reconcile exactly: 13 languages × 2 (`enabled`, `wasm_path`) = 26, minus the 2 the sample spells out for `javascript` — i.e. the entire gap is the guide's one generic "each of the 13 languages has its own block, e.g." clause, accepted as intentional rather than padded out with 26 table rows. Two class bugs surfaced while building it, and both would have produced wrong verdicts rather than no verdicts: **(a)** the struct-field regex required a comma *followed by a newline*, so it silently dropped the last field of every struct — `features.nda_merkle`, `http.cors_origins`, `logging.level` and `wasm_runtimes.instruction_limit` were invisible, which manufactured three false "documented but phantom" keys before the corrected pattern flipped them to real; **(b)** the TOML key pattern used `[a-z_]+`, which cannot spell `oauth2`, so the digit-bearing keys never registered as documented and the count read 16 where the truth is 17. A third, smaller trap: the read-site rule initially excluded `src/config.rs` itself, which branded all 13 `wasm_path` keys inert — they are read through the local alias `lang.wasm_path` in `resolve_language()` — proving that an inertness verdict is a statement about the grep pattern before it is a statement about the code.
- **`{"requests":[42]}` came back "processed", and three transports answered a structurally invalid body with the silence reserved for notifications.** Measured live on `:3900` before the edit (25 single-request cases plus 10 batch/parse cases, in `spec_probe.mjs` and `spec_probe2.mjs`, both kept in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Temp` so nothing was written into the repo): `POST /v1/mcp/batch` with `{"requests":[42]}` returned `HTTP=200 [{"status":"notification processed"}]`, the identical object for a real notification; `[{"jsonrpc":"2.0","method":"notifications/initialized"}]` returned `array(1)` carrying an object with no `jsonrpc` key and neither `result` nor `error`, which a spec-conformant client cannot parse; a bare JSON array to `/v1/mcp` returned `HTTP=204` with 0 bytes, as did `"just a string"`. One root cause: `handle_request` (`src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:120`) returns `None` both for a notification and for anything it cannot dispatch — three named notification arms plus the `_` arm's `id.is_null()` guard at `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:492` — so `src/middleware.rs:211`'s `None` arm had no way to tell them apart and guessed "processed". The fix is one predicate, `is_request_shaped` (`src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:69`), asking only whether the value carries a string `method`, applied at all three transports: batch (`src/middleware.rs:208`) omits notifications and gives an unshapeable element its own `-32600`; HTTP (`src/transport/http.rs:415`) keeps bodyless 204 for shaped requests only and returns `HTTP=400` with `-32600` naming `/v1/mcp/batch` for an array; stdio (`src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:711`) prints a `-32600` object instead of swallowing the line, mirroring the `-32700` object that path already emits for a parse failure at `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:690`, which is also the in-repo reference for the gap left open below. A unit test pinned the invented object (`responses[0]["status"] == "notification processed"`); its `len()==1` is retargeted to `len()==0` (`src/middleware.rs:467`) and the other half of what it claimed is now gated twice over — `src/middleware.rs:471` proves a notification does not swallow a sibling's real response (mixed batch to `ping id:7` yields `len()==1`, id preserved, no `status` key) and `src/middleware.rs:493` proves both unshapeable elements get `-32600` with a `jsonrpc` string — while `src/transport/http.rs:2392` gates the HTTP refusal on the endpoint name appearing in the message. `grep -rn "notification processed"` over `src/`, `tests/`, `docs/` and `dashboard/` now returns 0 hits, so no client-facing artifact still expects the invented object. **Refutation recorded:** the batch section of `docs/API.md` is correct — `{"requests":[...]}` measured `HTTP=200 array(2)`, `array(0)` for an empty list, `array(20)` for 20 mixed, and `-32601` preserved per element inside the array for an unknown method — so the `HTTP=422` bodies I first hit were my own wrong-shaped probes (bare array, single object), not a broken endpoint, and `docs/API.md:64`'s label stands; the rules block added at `docs/API.md:406` records the shape plus the four response rules now enforced. **Residual, named not fixed:** a JSON *parse* failure at `/v1/mcp` and `/v1/mcp/batch` still answers `HTTP=400 text/plain` ("Failed to parse the request body as JSON: key must be a string at line 1 column 2") with no `-32700` object, measured at both endpoints, because that text is axum's `Json` extractor rejection rather than this crate's — closing it means changing the extractor type on the two handlers, which also gates the `HTTP=415` Content-Type refusal measured live today, and I will not reshape a security boundary without a compiler. Also residual: a notification method that carries an `id` gets no response on any transport; that is the `_` arm's own collapse and predates this fix.
- **Editing a cited file rots every line-number citation above the edit, and the range-only census reported 0 problems while 19 citations went wrong.** `cite_sweep3.py` tests `line <= file length`, so `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:1135` stayed in range and passed after the test it names had moved to `:1157`. Measured population this session: 26 citations into the 5 files touched, of which 19 point into the three edited today (`src/protocol/json_rpc.rs` 12, `src/transport/http.rs` 6, `src/middleware.rs` 2, plus 3 in `docs/API.md` and `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md`), and the shift is not a constant even inside one bullet — `:174` moved +10, `:225` +10, `:764` +26, `:1131` +26 — because each depends on how many inserts preceded it, so no delta arithmetic recovers them; `git diff --numstat` is useless here too, reporting 38/2 for `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs` because the working tree carries two rounds of uncommitted edits against HEAD. Every one was re-anchored by grepping the referent's literal text and then re-verified by printing the source line at the new number: `fn constant_time_eq` at `src/transport/http.rs:1673`, `"auth_enabled"` at `:893`, `async fn nda_capabilities` at `:836`, `format!(` at `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:268`, `let (error_type, hint)` at `:235` with the classifier's arm block closing at `:266`. Stated as the historical record it is, because all six numbers in that re-verification list have since moved: `fn constant_time_eq` 1673→1747, `"auth_enabled"` 893→959, `async fn nda_capabilities` 836→902, `format!(` 268→297, `let (error_type, hint)` 235→294 and the arm-block close 266→118 — and the last pair moved because the classifier was *extracted into `classify_tool_error`* this round rather than merely shifting, which is the same lesson one level up: a citation into a block of code is only as durable as that block's shape. Current anchors for all six are in the classifier and verification bullets below. One citation was deliberately left alone: the *Two stale code citations in the reviewer docs* bullet's `src/transport/http.rs:731` records what a doc pointed at *at that time*, which is a historical statement, not a live anchor. (That pointer used to read `CHANGELOG.md` by line number; adding this round's bullets above it moved the target by three lines and the census caught it as one more advisory, so the self-reference is now content-addressed — a line-numbered anchor into a file that grows at the top cannot hold.) A bare `route :1448` in `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md:378` (the route cite in that doc is now `src/transport/http.rs:1530`) carries no path prefix at all and escaped the sweep entirely — found by hand while reading the prose. Four more of this file's own anchors were still printing pre-shift numbers and are re-pointed now, all intra-line: the blank-API-key bullet's `constant_time_eq` copy-check moved +74, its `auth_enabled` status line +66, its startup-warning reference +81 once it was written out with a path (it had been a bare `:1523`, invisible to the checker by construction), and a test-fixture citation in the clippy bullet moved **−3** — downward, because the cited line had shrunk into a closing brace. Those five deltas (+81, +74, +66, +2, −3) inside two bullets of one file are this bullet's own claim restated against itself: no arithmetic recovers a drifted anchor, only grepping the referent's text does, and a changelog that grows at the top rots its own line numbers between being written and being read. Stated plainly rather than papered over: a range-only census cannot detect this class, and the durable fix is a content-anchored checker (referent text to current line, per citing file) that CI runs; the sweep lives in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Temp` and nothing in CI runs it, so every future edit to a cited file repeats this rot until that exists.
- **Verification for these edits, and what they still owe.** `cargo fmt --all -- --check` exits 0 with zero-byte output over the edited tree — true exit code captured by redirecting to a file rather than piping, so `head` cannot mask it — which re-parses every workspace file and proves the new match guard, the `pub fn` and the three new tests are syntactically valid and rustfmt-clean. It is not a type check: the `&request` borrow in the new `None if` arms, the `Err((StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST, Json(…)))` tuple and `Value::as_str` inside `and_then` are argued from identical idioms already compiling in the same functions (the shutdown arm's `Err((StatusCode::SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE, Json(json!(…))))`, `request.get("id")`) rather than executed. One defect was caught in review before it could ship, not by a tool: the first stdio draft wrote `warn!(line = trimmed.truncate(120), …)`, and `String::truncate` returns `()`, so it would not have compiled; it is now a fieldless `warn!`. `:3900` still runs the pre-edit image, so every probe number above is a "before" measurement and none of them re-verifies the fix; the post-fix leg owed a rebuild because `target/` had been deleted to free space, plus the lib test-binary run that must prove the new `len()==0`, that `test_notification_returns_bodyless_204` still gets its 204, and that no suite elsewhere depended on the removed object. **Both have now run, and the debt is cleared.** Measured 2026-10-10 on the rebuilt tree, `cargo +stable test --features http,database,oauth2 --lib` exits **0**: **812 passed / 0 failed / 38 ignored**, 33.56 s. The immediately earlier run of the same command - the tree before this round's capability work - was **809 passed / 2 failed / 38 ignored** over 849 tests, and the two failures were `resources::tests::test_notify_and_poll_resource_updates` (`src/resources.rs:1260`) and `resources::tests::test_notify_nonexistent_resource_produces_no_update` (`src/resources.rs:1268`), neither of which this fix touched (both numbers are as-of-that-run annotations and both tests still exist: they declare at `src/resources.rs:1263` and `src/resources.rs:1279` today, +3 and +11; the second of the two was written as a bare pointer, so the citation checker — which requires a filename before the colon — could not see it rot, and it is path-qualified here for that reason, not because the old number was wrong for its own sentence): both drain the process-global `RESOURCE_REGISTRY` update queue, so they race with any other test in the same binary that queues an update. They are green in the later run with no change to either test, which is the flake being confirmed, not a fix being claimed - the real remedy (assert on owned state under a unique key, as `test_peer_buckets_are_tracked` does) is still open. The four assertions this bullet owed are individually green in the 812-run: `middleware::tests::test_batch_request_notification_does_not_swallow_response`, `middleware::tests::test_batch_request_invalid_element_is_rejected_not_acked` (the retargeted `assert_eq!(responses.len(), 0)`), `transport::http::tests::test_notification_returns_bodyless_204`, and the stdio `-32600` path, which no pre-existing suite asserted. `CHANGELOG.md` keeps a CRLF count equal to its line count after the 16 citation re-anchors and this bullet block, so no line-ending churn was introduced.

- **Every SSE frame was framed twice, so no conforming client could ever name an event.** The server assembled the whole wire frame as one string (`event: connected\ndata: {…}\n\n`) and handed that string to axum as a single event *payload*; axum then escaped and prefixed every line with `data:`, so a live subscription produced `data: event: connected` / `data: data: {"sessionId": …}`. A browser `EventSource` therefore saw only unnamed `message` events and no listener registered on an event name could fire. `src/transport/http.rs:780` now carries the name and the payload separately (`SseFrame::Event { name, data }` / `SseFrame::Comment`) and lets axum write the framing. Re-measured on the fixed build 2026-10-10 against `GET /v1/sse?session_id=framing2`: the stream is `event: connected` + `data: {"sessionId": "framing2"}` + blank line, then `event: notification` + `data: {"jsonrpc":"2.0","method":"notifications/tools/list_changed"}`, LF-only, with exactly one `list_changed` frame per registry bump. `dashboard/index.html` `openSSE()` moved from the newline-splitting `onmessage` workaround to real `addEventListener` listeners on `connected` / `notification` / `request_completed`. Two more claims in `docs/API.md` §SSE were wrong and are now measured rather than asserted: the keepalive comment is `: heartbeat` (with a space) and arrives once per 15 s although **two** 15 s sources are wired — a spawned task and axum's `KeepAlive` — because `KeepAlive` resets its deadline whenever the inner stream yields, so the task always beats it; measured cadence over a 40 s idle subscription was `connected` +87 ms, `: heartbeat` +99 ms (the interval's first tick fires immediately), then +15,099 ms and +30,098 ms. And `request_completed` is **not** emitted by `POST /v1/mcp`: two live subscribers received zero frames for a `tools/list` posted there, while the same method posted to `POST /v1/mcp/stream` reached both (+547 ms, +552 ms) — and the `sessionId` in that payload is the poster's server-assigned session, not the subscriber's, so it cannot be used to filter to one's own requests. `POST /v1/mcp/stream` is self-contained: its body is `text/event-stream` delivering `event: session` then `event: response`, measured at HTTP 200 / 15,425 bytes. The §SSE section of `docs/API.md` is rewritten from these captures.
- **`velocity-mcp-core` published the same inverted capability direction as the main crate, and its own test suite pinned the error.** `crates/velocity-mcp-core/src/lib.rs` declared `roots` and `elicitation` in the *server* capability map — both are client capabilities in the MCP spec — and a test asserted `capabilities.roots` was an object, so the mistake was load-bearing. The map is now server-side only (`tools`, `resources` with both flags false, `prompts`, plus the ones it actually serves), `crates/velocity-mcp-core/src/lib.rs:526` asserts `roots` is absent, and the three server→client requests `elicitation/create`, `roots/list` and `sampling/createMessage` are refused with `-32601` rather than answered locally (`crates/velocity-mcp-core/src/lib.rs:731` onward is the differential test that walks all three). `cargo +stable test -p velocity-mcp-core` exits **0**: 30 passed / 0 failed. The Edge user guide was **not** updated by that pass and kept publishing the old shape, which this round found by reading its method inventory against its own parenthetical: the bullet claimed "All 14 MCP JSON-RPC methods" and then enumerated **fifteen** names, three of which the crate now refuses, and its `initialize` sample still listed `elicitation` and `roots`. Both are repaired — the sample is the server-side map the crate actually returns and the bullet reads twelve dispatched methods with the three client-direction requests named as refused with `-32601`. The twelve is counted from the dispatch block, not from the prose: `initialize`, `notifications/initialized`, `notifications/cancelled`, `ping`, `tools/list`, `tools/call`, `resources/list`, `resources/read`, `resources/templates/list`, `prompts/list`, `prompts/get`, `completion/complete`.
- **The lint inventory the changelog carried was wrong because the measuring command was, and the debt is now closed with CI widened to catch it.** Earlier rounds recorded 45 and later 52 open lints; both counts came from `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, whose **first failing target aborts the rest**, so it structurally under-reports across targets. Measured without `-D` the true remaining figure was **20** — 7 in the lib-test target, 13 across `tests/integration.rs`, `tests/binary_protocol_test.rs`, `benches/wasm_isolation.rs`, `benches/tool_compatibility_test.rs` and `benches/comprehensive_benchmark.rs`. All 20 are fixed: `single_match` → `if let`, two `type_complexity` sites given a named alias or a targeted `#[allow]` where the alias would hide the very signature the probe exists to pin, and the rest dead-code removal (a self-recursive `json_contains_hello` with no caller, four unused consts, two unused struct fields and the 10 call sites their parameter disappeared from). `cargo +stable fmt --all -- --check` exits 0 and `cargo +stable clippy --features http,database,oauth2 --all-targets -- -D warnings` exits 0 with zero `error` lines; `.github/workflows/ci.yml:31` now runs that widened step. Verification totals on this tree: featured `cargo +stable test --workspace --features http,database,oauth2` **1,210 passed / 0 failed / 53 ignored** (exit 0) and plain `cargo +stable test --workspace` **1,139 passed / 0 failed / 53 ignored** across 29 test binaries (exit 0). Both are 6 below the previously published 1,216 / 1,145, which is exactly the 7 core tests deleted by the capability-direction fix above minus the 1 replacement test — not a suite that stopped running.
- **The NDA-vs-JSON differential the docs quoted had no committed runner, and the wire-level decode discriminator it described is not reachable over the wire**: `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` §9 carried a "16-tool differential" figure with nothing in the repo producing it — `grep -rn "0x99"` over `tests/`, `src/` and `bench_tools/interp_core/` returns no producer, so a reviewer could not replay the claim, and the probe the method paragraph named (a `0x99` type tag sent to `plugins::call_wasm_tool_binary`) is an **in-process** measurement that does not survive HTTP: measured 2026-10-09 by posting that tag inside a `tools/call` frame to `/v1/mcp/nda` for all 22 WASM tools, every one refused with `Malformed tools/call payload: TLV skip:` before any guest ran, because `extract_tools_call_fields` (`src/protocol/nda_native.rs:646`) walks every field value with `skip_tlv_value` first. `dashboard/diff_nda.mjs` is now the in-tree runner: a fixture per WASM tool, JSON leg then NDA leg, normalized payload comparison, capability report re-read before and after, exit 1 on any payload deviation. Its first full run produced **22/22 payload-identical, exit 0** against the long-running release server and the same against a debug build of current source.
- **The new runner's own two flaws, both caught by re-reading its output rather than trusting a green row**: (1) it required NDA `status == 0` for "identical", so a tool broken over *both* transports counted as a transport split — `analyze_logs_go` answers `Compilation failed: TinyGo not found` on this machine, and both legs return that text identically, so the runner now separates payload agreement from tool health and prints the second as `identical-but-erroring 1`. (2) Its poison leg was presented as a decode-site proof; measurement refuted that. An empty TLV **array** in `arguments` (the one shape the extractor imposes no constraint on, and the only malformed frame that reaches a guest over HTTP) came back as `{"error":"undefined variable 'name' at line 1"}` from `perl_greet` and `{"error":"unknown operation: "}` from `lua_math_tools` — a guest that tolerates the missing field answers the same whether it decoded the TLV or received the host's re-serialized JSON `[]`, so the column is labelled advisory, the prediction gate is gone, and the decode-site split still rests on the export census. That leg is now opt-in (`--poison`) because it is not read-only: one malformed frame moved `py_unit_converter` from `binaryExecutor: true` to `false` on a debug build of current source, and a full pass took the release server from 16/6 to 13/9. **Left open, not fixed here**: the MicroPython binary entry point hard-traps on a non-object `arguments` (`RuntimeError: unreachable` after `FATAL: Python exception raised but stack unwinding not supported`), and the trap discards the runtime and can downgrade the tool — a refusal would be the correct guest answer, and `bench_tools/interp_core/interp.c:2951`'s `ERROR: TLV decode failed or not an object` shows the shape the Python guest should produce.
- **`probe_nda.mjs` ran its six checks as an import side effect**: making its codec reusable meant exporting eight names, and importing that module executed the whole probe — a stray `6 pass, 0 fail` preamble in every consumer's output. The codec moved to `dashboard/nda_codec.mjs` (`NDA_HDR`, `METHODS`, `u32be`, `tlvEnc`, `tlvDec`, `sha256`, `buildRequest`, `parseResponse`), which performs no I/O on import (verified: importing it prints only its export list), and both consumers now import it. The probe re-verified after the split: **6 pass, 0 fail, exit 0** against `:3900`, including `tools/list` = 40 tools and the corrupted-frame and garbage-byte paths returning HTTP 400.
- **Cold-vs-warm capability reports: the cause recorded as "open rather than assumed" is now measured, and it was neither warm drift nor a stale process**: `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` §9 claimed a cold server advertised 22/0 while the day-served demo server advertised 16/6 (exactly the six tree-walk greets) and left the reason open. A/B on the same machine: a fresh server started from `target/release/velocity_mcp.exe` on `:3902` reported **16 binary / 6 fallback at startup**, while a debug build of current source on `:3903` reported **22 / 0** with an empty fallback set. So the release artifact predates the manifest-keyed capability report; "cold 22/0" is true of current source and false of the shipped exe, and the demo server's 16/6 was never drift. Genuine drift exists but is one tool wide: a single differential pass on `:3903` took it 22/0 → 21/1, the downgrade being `analyze_logs_go`, whose TinyGo failure passes the pre-execution classifier — `record_json_fallback` (`src/registry.rs:626`) remains the only writer of `false`. The docs' capability and tree-walk rows now state the two-binary split, and the wording is cause-neutral, because a `false` record says only that the classifier fired, not why.

- **CI's `Check formatting` failure silently disabled the entire test gate**: the CI-scope entry below is a *local* measurement — the widened `--workspace` commands were green on this tree and were never executed by the pipeline. The last run on the remote (`35286038255`, 2026-09-17, head `c28d72ef`, still the newest run as of 2026-10-09) has **two independent red causes in two different jobs**, and only one is visible from the job verdicts. (1) Job `105418460978` *Build & Test (ubuntu-latest)*: step 5 `Check formatting` = **failure**, and steps **6 `Clippy lint`, 7 `Build`, 8 `Build with all features`, 9 `Run all tests`, 10 `Run tests with all features` = skipped**. One formatting failure consumed the whole test gate: on none of the recent heads did CI compile, build or run a single test. That is the mechanism that kept the clean-clone artifact defect below invisible — locally every micropython test passed, so nothing the gate reported contradicted it. (2) Job `105418460714` *Dependency Audit*: its step 5 `Run cargo audit` = failure. Checked separately because the severities differ: the CI step is plain `cargo audit` with no `--deny` flag, so only true vulnerabilities fail it (the `unsound` advisories are allowed warnings and exit 0), and it now exits **0** on this lockfile. The two OS jobs `105418460956` (windows-latest) and `105418461036` (macos-latest) are **`cancelled`, not `failure`** — matrix fail-fast killed them when ubuntu red-flagged, so their status carries no verdict about those platforms and must not be read as one. `105418460984` *Property-Based Fuzz Tests* = success. Both causes are closed in-tree: `cargo +stable fmt --all --check` exits **0** and `cargo audit` exits **0** with 3 allowed warnings (re-measured 2026-10-09). The badge stays red until this batch is pushed — there is no newer run to turn it.
- **The MicroPython tests, benchmark and verifier all loaded a gitignored artifact — green locally, unbuildable from a clean clone**: four sites read `bench_tools/micropython_wasm/micropython-1.24.1/ports/webassembly/build-wasi/micropython.wasm`, a path inside the extracted-tarball directory that `.gitignore:23` (`bench_tools/micropython_wasm/micropython-1.24.1/`) excludes — confirmed with `git check-ignore -v`, which resolves exactly that file through that rule (rc 0). The sites were the test helper `wasm_path()` in `src/wasm_runtime/micropython.rs` (which feeds **7** `.expect("MicroPython WASM not found")` call sites, six of them not `#[ignore]`d), `src/benchmark.rs:1723`, `benches/wasm_tool_latency.rs:92` and `examples/verify_micropython.rs:5`. On a fresh clone the directory does not exist, so the lib-test binary **panics** — an `.expect()` with no skip guard, in the very steps (9 and 10 above) that CI had never reached. All four now point at the tracked `bench_tools/micropython_wasm/wasi-reactor/build/micropython.wasm`, which is byte-identical to the copy they used (588,146 B, sha256 `b7de8712…`, sha1 `cc712a34…`) and is already the production default at `src/config.rs:351`, so the retarget changes no local behaviour and makes the tested bytes the shipped bytes. The reason the tracked copy existed at all is that `build.sh` never installed it: it ended in `exec "$MAKE" -f Makefile.wasi …`, and `bench_tools/micropython_wasm/wasi-reactor/Makefile.wasi:7` documents its product as `build-wasi/micropython.wasm` — the gitignored location. It now runs the make step and copies the artifact into `wasi-reactor/build/`, printing the install path. Proof, not inference: with `micropython-1.24.1/` moved out of the tree entirely, `cargo +stable test --lib micropython` gives **4 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored**; before the retarget the same run aborts on the missing file.
- **`test_peer_buckets_are_tracked` raced on process-global state, and its assertion could not have caught the bug it was written for**: after the test-scope widening put `rate_limit` and `middleware` in one test binary this test failed (`left: 4, right: 3`) — first attributed to the `lru` bump, then cleared by A/B: run alone it passed 10/10, and the same two-module combination with `--test-threads=1` passed 22/22. That is a concurrency signature, not a dependency one. The old assertion compared `assert_eq!(tracked_peers(), after_first)`, but `tracked_peers()` returns the **total size of the shared `PEER_LIMITERS` LRU**, which any concurrently running test that touches a peer bucket legitimately raises — `assert_eq!` on a global count cannot be stable. It was also blind to the thing it appeared to check: a runtime that discarded and rebuilt a peer's bucket on every call keeps the *key count* identical. Fixed by asserting on owned state instead of the global total: a unique `198.51.100.9:peer-tracking` key (TEST-NET, so no other test can collide with it), `peer_available_tokens()` = `None` before the first call, then `>= before + 1` (the `||` arm covers the `MAX_PEER_BUCKETS` ceiling, the one case where the count legitimately cannot rise), then a drain over `burst + 8` calls asserting refusal, then that the surviving balance is **less than half the configured burst** — which a resetting bucket cannot satisfy. The new diagnostic `peer_available_tokens` (`src/rate_limit.rs:225`, +11 from the anchor this bullet first printed) reads through `LruCache::peek`, deliberately: `get` would reorder the list the eviction policy depends on, so a measurement must not change what it measures. Non-vacuity proven by mutation — rebuilding a fresh bucket on exhaustion while still refusing turns the test red at `src/rate_limit.rs:347` (+15; the file is 364 lines against HEAD's 202, and both of this bullet's anchors were written before that growth: `:214`/`:332` now land on an `.map(|c| c.len())` expression and a comment respectively) with `later calls reset the bucket instead of reusing it: 100 of 100 left` (exit 101); the mutation was reverted and the test re-run green (`--features http --lib -- rate_limit middleware`: **22 passed, 0 failed**). Neither drift is reportable by `scripts/check_citations.py`: the checker's token set is the union of every backtick span on the citing line, and other anchors on this same line match their own targets, so a wrong pointer here satisfies the gate — the same structural blindness recorded on the `src/rate_limit.rs:189` repair above. Only reading the cited line finds it.
- **CI's PR gate tested one of the five workspace crates**: `ci.yml` ran `cargo build`/`cargo test` without `--workspace` (`:37`, `:40`, `:43`, `:46`), and the root `Cargo.toml` lists five members (`:41`) with no `default-members`, so those steps resolved to the `velocity_mcp` package alone. Measured on the same tree: the CI-exact `cargo test --verbose` runs **15 test binaries, 853 passed, exit 0**, while `cargo test --workspace` runs **29 binaries, 1,144 passed** — i.e. **291 tests in 14 binaries were never gated by the pipeline that blocks a merge**, measured per crate (`cargo test -p <crate>`): `velocity-mcp-edge` **236** (39 lib + 69 `velocity_edge` + `e2e_tests` 13 + `integration_tests` 36 + `unit_tests` 79), `velocity-mcp-client` **19** (16 lib + 2 `shmem_integration_test` + 1 doc-test), `velocity-mcp-core` **36**, `velocity-mcp-macros` **0** — 291 in total, matching the workspace/root-package difference exactly. The only place any of them ran was `.github/workflows/deploy-edge.yml:116` (`cargo test --locked -p velocity-mcp-core -p velocity-mcp-edge`), which fires on push to `main` or manual dispatch under a path filter, never on a PR, and not at all for `client/` — so a pull request touching the SDK had no test coverage anywhere before merge. All four steps now carry `--workspace`; the widened commands are the ones already measured green on this tree (default features 1,144/0, `--features http,database,oauth2` 1,211/0). Deliberately *not* widened in the same pass: `cargo fmt --all --check` (`:31`) already covers the workspace, but `cargo clippy --features http,database,oauth2 -- -D warnings` (`:34`) is still root-only. `cargo +stable clippy --workspace --features http,database,oauth2 -- -D warnings` exits **0** here, yet that is one platform's verdict on a three-OS matrix with `-D warnings` attached, so it is recorded as the next candidate rather than changed silently alongside a test-scope fix. `.github/workflows/release.yml:40` keeps the same root-only scope; widening it would gate tag-triggered releases on the sibling crates.
- **Six `clippy::correctness` errors were latent in test fixtures, and the benchmark banner announced the wrong version**: `clippy::approx_constant` is deny-by-default, and six test float literals were near enough to π to be errors — `src/protocol/nda_native.rs:2616/2607/2705` and `src/registry.rs:3943/3979/5406`. They never broke CI because the clippy step committed at HEAD runs `cargo clippy --features http,database,oauth2 -- -D warnings`, which does not compile `#[cfg(test)]` code; `--all-targets` is where they surface. All six fixtures now use values that are exactly representable and not close to a constant (`-12.5`, `2.5`), which keeps them better JSON round-trip inputs than an irrational approximation anyway; `cargo +stable clippy --features http,database,oauth2 --all-targets` now reports **0 errors** (was 6), the CI-exact command still exits **0**, and the affected tests pass (`--lib tlv_roundtrip`: 13 passed; `--lib skip_tlv`: 17 passed). Also cleared two warnings that pointed at dead code in tests: `if cfg!(windows) { "echo test" } else { "echo test" }` in `tests/integration.rs:1176` had identical branches, and `HEADER_SIZE + 1 * TRIPLE_SIZE` in `src/nda_document.rs:992` was a no-op multiply. Separately, `src/benchmark.rs:19,22` printed `v3.0.0` in both its `info!` line and its banner while the crate is 3.2.0 — both now interpolate `crate::VERSION` (`src/lib.rs:60`, `env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")`), and a grep for `3.0.0`/`3.1.0` across `src/`, `crates/` and `client/src/` returns no other stale literal. Remaining `--all-targets` warnings, re-measured 2026-10-09 after a doc-comment pass converted the twelve `///` property comments in `tests/fuzz_tests.rs` to `//`: each one sat directly above a `proptest! { … }` macro invocation, and a doc comment on a macro statement documents nothing, so all twelve were `unused_doc_comments` warnings.: **26** in the lib test target and **14** in `benches/comprehensive_benchmark.rs`, **2** each in `tests/fuzz_tests.rs` (down from 14), `tests/binary_protocol_test.rs`, `tests/integration.rs`, `benches/tool_compatibility_test.rs` and `benches/wasm_isolation.rs`, and **1** each in `tests/interpreter_audit.rs` and `benches/wasm_vs_native.rs`. 52 in total, all style lints, none correctness, none gating CI. Re-measured 2026-10-11, and three of this bullet's pointers had drifted. The six are six occurrences as written — each fixture's literal plus its use, three per file — and their current positions are `src/protocol/nda_native.rs:2659/2653/2786` and `src/registry.rs:5406` alongside the two that still land. Only the registry pair is still exact: the printed `src/protocol/nda_native.rs:2616/2607/2705` matches no committed revision of that file — the pre-fix tree (`HEAD~1`) carries its three literals at `:2570`, `:2575` and `:2673`, a uniform +32 against those numbers, and the fixed tree carries them at `:2619`, `:2624` and `:2757` — so the printed trio was read off a working state 32 lines longer above that point than any tree now in history; `src/registry.rs:5408` is +2 off the repaired `"float": 2.5` line at `:5404`. The pointers that still land do so because the repairs were intra-line: `src/benchmark.rs:19` opens the `info!` that interpolates `crate::VERSION`, although its banner partner is now `:24` (the printed `:22` is a closing `);`), `src/nda_document.rs:992` is the repaired `HEADER_SIZE + TRIPLE_SIZE` expression, `tests/integration.rs:1176` is the de-conditioned `"command": "echo test"` line (`grep -rn` for `cfg!(windows)` over `tests/` now returns nothing), `src/lib.rs:60` is `pub const VERSION: &str = env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION");`, and `.github/workflows/ci.yml:31` is the same lint step this bullet quotes without `--all-targets` but has since been widened — the lint-inventory bullet above records that widening, so the latent window described here is that round's gate, not today's. The closing `52 in total` is superseded there too (measured 20, all fixed); cross-referenced, not re-counted. None of this was reportable by `scripts/check_citations.py`: every drifted line it was asked about is populated, and the citing sentence itself supplies the tokens — the union-matching blindness recorded on the `src/rate_limit.rs:189` repair.
- **Two blank-value authentication paths: one panicked the caller, one locked the operator out**: (1) `constant_time_eq` (`src/oauth2.rs:414`) padded the shorter slice by cycling it — `b[i % b.len()]` — so an empty side was a divisor of zero. Its only caller compares an HMAC digest (never empty) against the *caller's* signature string, so a present-but-blank signature reaching `handle_incoming_webhook` (`:538`, `Some("")`) panicked instead of refusing. Measured by compiling HEAD's body standalone and calling it with a 64-byte expected value and an empty actual: `attempt to calculate the remainder with a divisor of zero`. Scope, stated honestly: no HTTP route calls `handle_incoming_webhook` yet (grep of `src/transport/` finds no webhook handler), so this was a library-surface panic, not a remotely reachable one. The guard is the only behavioural change — the unequal-length arms are untouched, since HEAD already seeded `result` from the length difference. Cross-checked the helper's two other copies: `src/transport/http.rs:1747` already had both the empty-side guard and a test for it, and Edge's `timing_safe_eq` (`crates/velocity-mcp-edge/src/main.rs:350`) pads to a fixed 256 bytes and cannot divide by zero. (2) `VELOCITY_API_KEY=""` — or whitespace-only, e.g. a blank line in a systemd/env-file entry — produced `Some("")`, which the auth middleware deliberately fails closed on, so **every** authenticated request returned 401 with no key that could work, while the status endpoint still reported `auth_enabled: true` (`src/transport/http.rs:959`) and the "HTTP transport without an API key" startup warning (`src/transport/http.rs:1604`) stayed silent. `normalize_api_key()` (`src/config.rs:91`) now maps blank to `None`, at the env override (`:478`) and again at the config→security handoff (`src/main.rs:235-239`), so a blank key means *not configured* on both the env and TOML paths and the operator gets the warning they were being denied. Tests: `test_constant_time_eq_empty_and_unequal_sides` (new; proven non-vacuous — re-applying HEAD's body in place makes it fail with exactly that divisor-zero panic and exit 101, the fixed body passes), blank arms added to `test_webhook_signature` and `test_handle_incoming_webhook`, `test_normalize_api_key`, and a blank-value arm in `test_apply_env_overrides_api_key` (kept in one test because the env var is process-global). `cargo +stable test --features oauth2 --lib oauth2`: **19 passed, 0 failed**. Note that `src/lib.rs:29` compiles this module out under default features, so the default `cargo test` run does not gate it — CI's `cargo test --workspace --features http,database,oauth2` step (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:58`) does.
- **MicroPython's in-guest binary path was never reachable, and its TLV decoder mistook siblings for nesting**: two defects in the shipped guest. (1) `bench_tools/micropython_wasm/wasi-reactor/Makefile.wasi` exported nine `mp_wasi_*` entry points but not `mp_wasi_call_tool_binary`, even though `bench_tools/micropython_wasm/wasi-reactor/wasi_main.c:774` implements it. Parsing the export section of the tracked artifact (the path `src/config.rs:351` makes the production default) showed **15 exports, none of them the binary entry**, so `src/wasm_runtime/micropython.rs:177` got `None` on every load and `call_tool_binary` silently took the host-decode-then-JSON fall-through at `:274-283` — the guest's TLV decoder was dead code in every build, and the single-WASI-call path the runtime was written for never ran. Measured on one artifact, release profile, 2000 hot calls per arm, 40-byte string argument, each call with the same per-call `reset_instruction_budget()` the registry applies: JSON path **8419 / 8391 ns**, in-guest TLV path **7649 / 7555 ns** across two runs — roughly **10 % (~800 ns/call)** given up per call. (2) With that path live, `tlv_decode_value`'s guard became reachable: `bench_tools/micropython_wasm/wasi-reactor/wasi_main.c:697` rejects `parser->depth > TLV_MAX_DEPTH` (32) while HEAD's `TLV_TAG_ARRAY`/`TLV_TAG_OBJECT` arms did `parser->depth++` and returned without ever decrementing, so the counter tallied *containers seen* rather than *levels*, and the 33rd sibling of one flat array was refused. Each refused value is substituted, not raised (`mp_obj_new_str("TLV depth exceeded", 19)`), so the tool still returned valid JSON — silently, since `{"dicts":31,"n":40}` is a well-formed answer about data the guest mis-typed. Both arms now restore the depth around the recursive call (`:723-732`). Regression test `test_tlv_sibling_containers_do_not_accumulate_depth` sends 40 sibling `{}` inside one array and asserts both counts are 40; it is proven non-vacuous by an A/B over two artifacts built from the same Makefile — HEAD's `wasi_main.c` fails with exactly the predicted `{"dicts":31,"n":40}` (exit 101) and the fixed source passes. The rebuilt guest (**588,146 B**, sha1 `cc712a34…`, sha256 `b7de8712…`, **16 exports** including `mp_wasi_call_tool_binary` — the export set enumerated through `WebAssembly.Module.exports`, not a hand-written section parser) replaces the tracked `wasi-reactor/build/micropython.wasm`, which is now the *only* path any consumer loads: `src/config.rs:351`, the tests, `src/benchmark.rs:1723`, `benches/wasm_tool_latency.rs:92` and `examples/verify_micropython.rs:5` all read it, so the shipped bytes and the tested bytes are one file (see the retarget entry under *Fixed*). `cargo +stable test --lib micropython`: **4 passed, 0 failed**, 3 ignored. Still open in the same fall-through shape: `bench_tools/quickjs_wasm/quickjs.wasm` exports `qjs_tool_call` but not `qjs_tool_call_binary` (so QuickJS's `:466` lookup falls through the same way, though its batched `qjs_tool_call` path is active), and `ruby.rs` looks up no binary export at all; the six tree-walk guests do export theirs.
- **An oversized tool argument was truncated by the guest instead of refused**: nothing on the host bounded the argument payload before it was handed to a fixed-slot WASM runtime. The MicroPython guest clamps its own copy (`bench_tools/micropython_wasm/wasi-reactor/wasi_main.c:525`, `ARGS_BUF_SIZE` = 4096 at `:228`), so a 5011-byte payload measured against the real guest came back as a **successful** call reporting `len: 4086` — the tool ran on data the client never sent — or aborted the interpreter through `nlr_jump`, after which the same cached runtime could not serve even a 4-character payload (only a metering trap evicts a runtime, so that abort outlived the request). The layout made the silent case worse than a crash: the host writes arguments at `ARGS_SLOT` (4 KB) ahead of `NAME_SLOT` (8 KB), and Wasmer bounds-checks against the *memory size*, not the slot, so an oversized payload overwrote the tool name inside valid linear memory (`src/wasm_runtime/mod.rs:38-44`). Fix: a `WasmRuntime::args_slot_capacity()` hook (`:371`) — default `Some(memory_layout::ARGS_CAPACITY)` = 4096 for the runtimes that share `memory_layout`, QuickJS reports its own `EXEC_SLOT`, Ruby its `SLOT_NAME − SLOT_ARGS`, Go and Rust return `None` because they allocate their own guest buffer, TypeScript forwards to QuickJS — and `check_args_fit()` (`:60`) called at the three funnels before anything reaches the guest: `WasmRuntimeRegistry::call_tool` (`:469`), `execute_wasm_plugin_tool` on the JSON path (`src/plugins/mod.rs:484`) and on the binary TLV path (`:614`). The refusal is deliberately worded so the registry's binary-fallback classifier reads it as neither request-level nor pre-execution: nothing ran, and retrying would fail identically and then mark a healthy tool JSON-only forever. Tests: `test_args_slot_guard_refuses_oversized_payload` (slot−1 accepted, slot rejected, `None`-capacity runtime accepted at 16 MB), `test_default_args_slot_capacity_matches_shared_layout` (pins the 4096), and the real-guest `test_registry_refuses_payload_larger_than_args_slot`, which after the refusal calls the *same* runtime with a 4-character payload and asserts `"len":4` — proving the refusal left nothing corrupted. `cargo +stable test --lib args_slot`: **3 passed, 0 failed**, exit 0.
- **The dashboard CDP suites could hijack the operator's own browser**: `test_cdp.mjs`, `test_interactive.mjs` and `screenshot.mjs` each hardcoded a `--remote-debugging-port` (9333, 9336, 9335) and then navigated whatever `/json/list` returned. A browser already open on that debug port silently won — the suite would drive the user's real tabs instead of its own headless instance, and `test_cdp.mjs` had gone unrun since 2026-09-18 for exactly this reason. All three now launch with `--remote-debugging-port=0` and resolve the ephemeral port from the first line of `<user-data-dir>/DevToolsActivePort`. Unblocked, `test_cdp.mjs` ran for the first time in three weeks: **5/5 pass** (connection pill, SSE feed, 50-call burst, real-clock latency distribution avg 1.6 ms / p50 1.5 ms / p95 2.6 ms, no 10 ms timer quantization).
- **`probe_nda.mjs` exited 127 after a fully green run**: the last check leaves two undici keep-alive sockets closing, and `process.exit()` landed inside that window. On Windows libuv aborts on `Assertion failed: !(handle->flags & UV_HANDLE_CLOSING)` (`src/win/async.c` in libuv's own tree — a path this repo does not contain), so a 6/6 pass printed its own tally and then returned a failure status — red to any caller trusting `$?`. It now sets `process.exitCode` and lets Node drain naturally. Both paths verified: 6/6 against the live server exits **0** in 0.2 s, and against an unreachable base URL it still exits **1**.
- **Six tree-walk interpreter guests refused every NDA call before running anything**: `perl_greet`, `r_greet`, `php_greet`, `java_greet`, `julia_greet` and `csharp_greet` were advertised `binaryExecutor: true` yet answered every binary request with `ERROR: Unknown tool '<name>'`, because `interp_call_tool_binary` looked the tool name up in the interpreter scope it had just reset instead of running the source registered through `interp_register_tool`. Nothing ever executed, so the call fell to the decoded-JSON path and returned the *correct* text — the defect was invisible in payload comparisons and only showed as an `NDA error:` line in the dashboard. Both entry points now call one shared `exec_registered_tool()` in `bench_tools/interp_core/interp.c`, and the six guests were rebuilt with wasi-sdk clang (276,888–277,005 B each, ~1.5 s per build). The host-side `NO_BINARY_EXEC_LANGUAGES` blocklist that masked this is deleted. Measured after the rebuild: the cold capability report goes **16 binary / 6 fallback → 22 / 0**, and a deliberately malformed frame (`0x99` type tag) now produces the *guest's* refusal (`ERROR: TLV decode failed or not an object`) instead of the host codec's `Unknown TLV type tag: 0x99` — the behavioural probe that distinguishes an in-sandbox decode from a host-decoded fall-through, and the reason a payload-equality check alone is not a sufficient gate. Regression test `test_interpreter_guests_binary_matches_json` asserts the same payload through both encodings **and** `binaryExecutor == true`; it is proven non-vacuous by running it red against the pre-fix guest from `git show HEAD:` (`tests/binary_protocol_test.rs:280`). `cargo +stable test --workspace`: **1,143 passed, 0 failed** across 29 binaries. Re-measured 2026-10-09 rather than left queued: `test_interactive.mjs` is **37 passed / 0 failed, exit 0** (it grew from 33 to 37 when the capability-gate block was rewritten, see *Fixed* in this round), and the WASM-tool differential is now an in-tree runner, `dashboard/diff_nda.mjs`, reporting **22/22 payload-identical** against both the release server and a debug build of current source. What the differential cannot do is run the six guests through the *release* artifact: that suite spawns its own server from `target/release/velocity_mcp.exe`, and the running demo server holds the file lock, so the relink is an operator decision. On current source the six guests hold `binaryExecutor: true` through a full differential pass; the 16/6 figure belongs to the old binary. The three suites that talk to the already-running server were re-greened the same day and do not depend on the rebuild: `test_dashboard.sh` 14/14, `test_cdp.mjs` 5/5, `probe_nda.mjs` 6/6 — none of their legs reach the tree-walk guests (`test_cdp.mjs`'s only WASM tool is QuickJS `js_calculator`; the probe's legs are `ping`, `tools/list` and the native `echo`).
- **HTTP server aborted at startup when `cors_origins` contained `"*"`**: `build_router` fed the whole configured list to tower-http's `AllowOrigin::list`, and 0.6.11 panics on a wildcard entry (`tower-http-0.6.11/src/cors/allow_origin.rs:61`). Measured 2026-10-09 with the shipped `dashboard/demo.toml` (`cors_origins = ["*"]`, the configuration `docs/DASHBOARD.md:301` documents on purpose): the log reaches `HTTP server address addr="0.0.0.0:3900"`, then `thread 'main' panicked at ...: Wildcard origin (* ) cannot be passed to AllowOrigin::list`, the process exits and `GET /health` never answers — so the demo config could not start the server at all. A `*` entry now selects `AllowOrigin::any()` (with a boot `WARN` naming the open CORS) and is filtered out of the list; the restrictive localhost-predicate default is untouched. Regression test `test_cors_wildcard_origin_builds_router_and_allows_any_origin` asserts the router constructs and echoes `access-control-allow-origin: *`; `test_cors_allows_every_configured_origin` still passes (2 passed, 0 failed, `cargo +stable test --features http,database,oauth2 --lib cors`).
- **`velocity-mcp-client` could not link on Windows**: the client declares `timeBeginPeriod` / `timeEndPeriod` for the high-resolution timer around the shmem event wait, but without `#[link(name = "winmm")]` the MSVC linker never pulls `winmm.lib`. Measured 2026-10-09 on `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`: `cargo +stable test -p velocity-mcp-client --test shmem_integration_test --no-run` → `error LNK2019: unresolved external symbol timeBeginPeriod referenced in ... enable_high_resolution_timer`, the same for `timeEndPeriod`, then `fatal error LNK1120: 2 unresolved externals` — for both the integration test and the `shmem_client` example (gate exit 101). The server-side copy (`src/ipc/shmem.rs:35`) already carried the attribute; the published client crate did not, so any Windows consumer that pulls in the shmem transport fails at link. The two declarations now sit in their own `#[cfg(target_os = "windows")] #[link(name = "winmm")]` block, matching the server. Verified: `cargo +stable test -p velocity-mcp-client --no-run` and `cargo +stable build -p velocity-mcp-client --example shmem_client` both exit 0 and emit their executables.
- **README Client SDK samples did not compile against the shipped SDKs**: all four samples invented an API no client exports. Measured against the declarations 2026-10-09: Rust has no `velocity_mcp_client::Client::new(url)` — the crate exposes `McpClient::new(transport)` (`client/src/client.rs:18`) over `HttpTransport::new(url, api_key) -> Result<Self>` (`client/src/transport/mod.rs:108`); the Python module is `velocity_mcp_client`, not `velocity_mcp`, and its client is async over a transport (`sdk/python/velocity_mcp_client/__init__.py`); the npm package is `@velocity-mcp/client`, not `velocity-mcp-client`, and `McpClient` takes a `Transport` (`sdk/typescript/src/client.ts:27-32`); the Go import path `.../velocity-mcp/client/go` contradicts `go.mod` (`github.com/UnitBuilds-CC/velocity-mcp/sdk/go`), and the constructor is `NewMcpClient(NewHttpTransport(url, apiKey, timeout))`, not `NewClient(url)`. All four samples rewritten to the real constructors, endpoints (`/v1/mcp`) and method names, each re-read against its declaration.
- **The zero-alloc TLV walker refused frames the allocating decoder accepted**: `skip_tlv_value_inner` guarded array and object nesting with `depth >= TLV_MAX_DEPTH` while `decode_json_value_inner` uses `depth > TLV_MAX_DEPTH` (`src/protocol/nda_native.rs:186`), so a frame nested exactly 32 levels deep decoded fine on the JSON path and errored on the in-place hot path — a transport-dependent answer for identical bytes. The two per-tag guards are replaced by one entry check mirroring the decoder (`:409`), and `skip_and_decode_agree_on_the_depth_boundary` now runs both walkers over the same buffer on both sides of the boundary: 32 levels must decode *and* skip, 33 must fail *both*. Covered by the workspace run below (1,143 passed).
- **Three doc headline claims replaced by figures the repo can show its work for**: (1) `convert_to_nda_tool` was described in `docs/API.md` and `docs/GETTING_STARTED.md` as giving "2.8x faster parsing (measured)" — the multiplier describes a tool-definition conversion, which no harness in this repo re-measures, so both now state what the function does (converts JSON tool definitions into NMCP binary frames dispatched through the zero-alloc TLV path). `grep -r "2\.8x" docs/ README.md` now returns exactly one line, and it is a substring collision rather than a leftover of the claim: the `12.8x` on the R (WebR) row of `docs/benchmark_analysis_wasm_runtimes.md:58`, which that file's Part B defines as first-create (40,199 µs) vs cache-hit (3,150 µs) module creation — R's own compile-vs-cache speedup, with no Node.js term anywhere in that table. (2) The "Up to 27.7x faster than Node.js MCP servers" headline (removed from `docs/GETTING_STARTED.md`, and its two occurrences in `docs/MIGRATION.md` re-scoped) was a cross-transport number presented as a same-transport win; it is replaced by the per-method verdict from the `docs/COMPARISON.md` table (warm JSON/HTTP: ping 2.2x, `tools/call` 1.4x, and Node *faster* on `tools/list` at 4.6x) plus the end-to-end pipeline claim it actually belongs to (14.5x ping / 9.7x `tools/call` for NDA/shmem vs Node JSON/stdio, traceable to `bench_results_core5.txt`). (3) `docs/COMPARISON.md` described its own machine as "Intel Core 5 210H (12 cores)" — the CPU is 8 cores / 12 logical processors, which matters because the same file's scaling note reads "sub-linear past 2 threads"; the hardware line and the note now agree. Re-measured 2026-10-11: the same "(12 cores)" error was still standing in two sibling benchmark docs — `docs/benchmark_analysis_wasm_runtimes.md` and `docs/wasm_runtime_status.md`, each in its methodology/environment line naming the same machine — and is corrected there too, because a claim about one file's hardware has to be fixed wherever that hardware is described. Its reproduction block was also unrunnable as written: `cargo run --release --bin bench_nda` cannot resolve that binary from the repo root, because `bench_nda/Cargo.toml` declares its own `[workspace]` and the root excludes it (`Cargo.toml:42`), so it is now `cargo run --release --manifest-path bench_nda/Cargo.toml`, plus the `--features http,database,oauth2` the tables above actually need and the note that the harness resolves both peers against CWD.
- **Three docs/manifest over-claims**: the root `Cargo.toml` `description` called the server "zero-allocation" — that holds for the NDA/TLV frame path (`parse_nda_request_inplace`, `skip_tlv_value`), not the JSON/HTTP paths, so it now reads "…with a zero-allocation binary frame path". `docs/wasmer_edge_deployment.md` referenced `project-wasm-vs-native-benchmark.md`, a memory file that is not in this repo; it now links `benchmark_analysis_wasm_runtimes.md`, and a full markdown link scan found no other broken relative link under `docs/`. `crates/velocity-mcp-edge/src/lib.rs:10` and `crates/velocity-mcp-edge/src/main.rs:1015-1019` described the CGI/WCGI entry point as *the* Edge path while the gate at `crates/velocity-mcp-edge/src/main.rs:1022` is `all(target_arch = "wasm32", not(target_vendor = "wasmer"))` (a second gate for `fn handler` follows at `:1027`) and the deployed WASIX build runs the socket server whose cfg gate is at `:940`, `any(not(target_arch = "wasm32"), target_vendor = "wasmer")`; the comments now name it the plain-WASI fallback and state the failure at the scope it was measured (`wasm32-wasip1` artifact, in its deployed configuration) rather than generalizing it, consistent with `docs/edge_user_guide.md:601`. Note that the three edge `main.rs` line numbers above are the *post*-rewrite positions, so the cited comment block already carries the corrected wording: `1014` and `1020` are the block's `// ---` rules and the prose is `1015-1019`, which is why the entry-point cite moved from the separator to the text. This pointer reads 601 rather than the 603 it published earlier: the same round that found the core over-claim deleted two lines from the Edge guide's `initialize` sample above this point, so the sentence it names did not move but its number did.
- **Malformed NDA `tools/call` payload dispatched as the tool named `""`**: `dispatch_nda_request` read the call fields with `extract_tools_call_fields(req.data).unwrap_or((None, None))`, so a payload the zero-alloc TLV walker could not read (a field declaring a 60 000-byte string the frame never carries, for example) was discarded and dispatch continued with no tool name — which fell into the catch-all executor and returned `Error running tool '': C# core engine not found`. The extraction error is now reported as an NDA error frame (`status 1`, `Malformed tools/call payload: <reason>`), `WARN`ed, and audit-`Rejected` before any tool runs. Regression test `malformed_tools_call_payload_is_rejected_not_dispatched`. Measured live 2026-10-02: `Malformed tools/call payload: TLV skip: truncated string body`.
- **Capability report keyed to runtime state instead of the manifest**: `GET /v1/nda/capabilities` and the binary-dispatch gate read `WASM_TOOL_METADATA`, which is only written after a successful `register_tool`. On a fresh server every tool is cold, so the report answered "JSON fallback" for tools whose guest does have a binary entry point, and the NDA leg of the dashboard comparison was skipped for them. The report is now keyed on the plugin manifests (the same source dispatch uses to resolve a tool's language) and self-corrects per tool: a guest that cannot register is moved to the fallback set after that one call, and only that tool. Measured 2026-10-02: a cold server reports 16 binary / 6 fallback; after `analyze_logs_go` fails to register (no TinyGo on this host) it reports 15 / 7, with `go_text_stats` unaffected. No tool executes twice on either path.
- **Lua guest leaked binary arguments into every later call**: `lua_wasi_call_tool` (JSON) and `lua_wasi_call_tool_binary` (TLV) share one `lua_State`, and the binary entry point stashed its decoded arguments in the registry field `__tool_args_binary`, which `get_tool_args()` prefers. Nothing cleared it, so after any NDA call every subsequent call in the process — JSON-RPC included — received the *previous* request's arguments. Measured on the pre-fix guest: a binary `lua_math_tools {operation:"sqrt", value:9}` left `{"error":"unknown operation: "}` for the following call and made `lua_string_utils` echo `AGAIN` from an earlier request. Both entry points now retire the slot. The rebuilt guest passes the four-step cross-path sequence in `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` §9.1 and the 22-tool differential in §9 (`dashboard/diff_nda.mjs`, JSON leg first, then the NDA leg — the ordering that pins slot retirement is the Rust-side sequence).
- **Lua guest numeric TLV framing**: `lua_math_tools` returned correct results over NDA while logging `WARN binary arguments rejected … TLV decode error` on *every* request — the tool was advertised binary and actually served by the decoded-JSON fall-through each time. The guest's `TLV_TAG_INTEGER` / `TLV_TAG_FLOAT` branches read a `u32be` length before the 8 payload bytes, a form this codec never emits (`encode_json_value` pushes tag then 8 big-endian bytes), so any `i64` below 2^32 decoded as `len = 0` and aborted the parse; the float branch additionally `memcpy`'d wire bytes straight into a `double`, byte-swapping them on little-endian WASM. Both branches now read 8 raw bytes and assemble big-endian, the float via a `u64` bit pattern. Measured after the rebuild: `sqrt(16)` → `4`, `2 ^ 10` → `1024`, `2.5 ^ 2` → `6.25`, zero `WARN` lines for any Lua tool. String/array/object branches already matched, which is why `lua_string_utils` and `analyze_logs_lua` never showed the defect.
- **Correction to the 3.2.0 binary-protocol entry** (`[3.2.0] — Binary argument protocol for WASM tools`): it states the double-JSON bottleneck was eliminated "across all 12 WASM runtimes" and that Lua, MicroPython and QuickJS/TypeScript "have custom TLV decoders". Measured against the shipped guest binaries on 2026-10-02, only the Lua guest decoded TLV inside the sandbox. Revised twice on 2026-10-09, the second time by the MicroPython entry above: **13 of the 22** WASM tools now decode inside the sandbox — Lua's 3, the six tree-walk guests (see **Six tree-walk interpreter guests refused every NDA call before running anything** below) and MicroPython's 4; the remaining 9 (QuickJS/TypeScript, Ruby, Go, Rust) are still host-decoded. QuickJS's host does look for a guest binary entry point (`qjs_tool_call_binary`) but the shipped module does not export it, and the Ruby/Rust runtimes never override `call_tool_binary`, so those take the trait default: host decodes TLV → re-serializes JSON → the guest `JSON.parse`es it. Go overrides `call_tool_binary` on its own (`src/wasm_runtime/go.rs:279-284`) with that identical decode-then-JSON body, so it arrives at the same place by a different route. The transport-side win (no JSON framing on the wire, no parse of the request envelope) is real for all of them; the interpreter-side one is not. Per-runtime evidence in `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` §9.2, including what each guest still needs.
- **NDA binary capability poisoned by a bad payload**: `call_tool_binary` treated every failed binary attempt as proof the guest has no binary entry point, so one wrongly-shaped call permanently downgraded a working tool — measured 2026-10-02: a single `lua_math_tools` probe with `{a, b}` instead of `{operation, value}` moved the server's own report from 16 binary / 6 fallback to 15 / 7 for the life of the process. Binary failures are now classified: a payload the guest rejects (`TLV decode`) falls back to decoded JSON *for that request only* and the tool keeps its capability; a pre-execution failure (`Unknown tool`, `failed to register`, `not a function`) gets one off-hot-path re-registration retry and is only then recorded as fallback; anything else is returned as an error instead of being re-run silently. The six tree-walk guests were still genuine fallbacks at that point — `interp_call_tool_binary` (`bench_tools/interp_core/interp.c:2869`) resolved the tool name in the interpreter scope while `interp_register_tool` (:2676) kept a single global source slot, so their binary entry point answered `ERROR: Unknown tool '<name>'` before guest code ran. That is the error the dashboard showed as `NDA error: … Perl tool error: ERROR: Unknown tool 'perl_greet'`, and those tools were gated out of the comparison. Superseded 2026-10-09 by **Six tree-walk interpreter guests refused every NDA call before running anything** below, which fixes the guest and deletes that host-side blocklist.
- **tools/list request id preservation**: The `handle_tools_list_stub` and `handle_tools_list` functions were dropping the request id, returning `null` in the JSON-RPC response. This broke client-side response correlation for MCP clients. Both functions now preserve `request.id` via `request.id.clone()`. Added regression test `test_tools_list_preserves_request_ids` checking both stub and executor paths with numeric and string ids.
- **JSON-RPC 2.0 error-code compliance** (native + Edge): Aligned three error paths with the JSON-RPC 2.0 spec, verified live on Wasmer Edge. (1) Malformed JSON in the Edge socket HTTP handler now returns error code `-32700` (Parse error) instead of leaking the HTTP status `400` as the code. (2) A request missing the required `method` member now returns `-32600` (Invalid Request) instead of `-32601` (Method not found) — enforced by structural validation in `velocity-mcp-core::dispatch`. (3) A request whose `jsonrpc` member is not `"2.0"` is now rejected with `-32600` instead of being processed. Added core regression tests for the two dispatch-level fixes.
- **QuickJS multi-tool hijack**: one instance hosts every tool of its language, but `QuickJsRuntime` cached a *single* `wrapper_handle` — each `register_tool` overwrote it, so calls to any JavaScript tool ran whichever tool registered last (observed live: `js_calculator` returning the log analyzer's output). Wrappers are now per-tool globals (`__wrapper_<name>`) resolved by tool name on all three call paths (JSON, batched, binary TLV), with `destroy()` made best-effort teardown. Regression test `test_quickjs_multiple_tools_do_not_hijack_each_other` registers two tools and interleaves calls. Found by the dashboard's interactive suite, not by the runtime tests — the two long-loop ignored tests (`test_destroy_isolation`, `bench_quickjs_batched_vs_legacy`) also trapped because they skipped the per-call `reset_instruction_budget()` the production registry always performs; fixed to mirror production. All 6 QuickJS ignored tests now pass.
- **Client shmem orphan-event race** (`velocity-mcp-client`): the shared-memory `wait_for_response` trusted a `WaitForSingleObject` wake as proof of a new response. When the server's response-event signal landed just after a fast client had already consumed the response via the state spin path, the orphan signal woke the *next* request into the *previous* reply — surfacing as `StaleResponse("Response ID None does not match request ID Some(Number(2))")`, reproducible by running the shmem integration suite in parallel (3/3 failing before, 3/3 green after). Event wakes are now advisory and the authoritative state byte is re-validated after each one; the spin hot-path is unchanged.
- **Edge suite empty-method expectation**: `crates/velocity-mcp-edge` protocol test still asserted `-32601` for an empty `method`; aligned with the corrected dispatch (`-32600` Invalid Request). Caught by the first full `cargo test --workspace` run: 1,133 passed / 0 failed / 46 ignored (2026-09-18).
- **`init_observability` panicked instead of returning `Err`**: it called `tracing_subscriber::…::init()`, which panics when a global subscriber is already installed — the normal case for an embedder that sets up its own logging first. Switched to `try_init()?` so the failure travels back through the function's `Result`.
- **`observability` stub had a different signature from the real one**: with the feature off, `init_observability` returned `Result<(), Box<dyn Error>>` while the featured version returned `Result<ObservabilityGuard, …>`, so embedder code that named the guard type only compiled under one feature state. `ObservabilityGuard` is now defined in both (an empty struct when the feature is off) and both return the same type; the stub still always fails with the "compile with `--features observability`" message.
- **`observability` docs over-claimed metrics**: the module header and the `USER_GUIDE.md` feature table both described "tracing and metrics export". Only a span exporter is built — there is no metrics pipeline. Both now say spans only.
- **Two stale code citations in the reviewer docs, found by sweeping every `path:line` reference in `docs/*.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` and `README.md` — 24 files scanned, 117 citations, every one of them in 5 of those files)**: `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` §8.1 pointed at `src/transport/http.rs:731` for the capability handler and `:1341` for its route, while current source has them at `:820` and `:1448` — the numbers predated this batch's HTTP edits, so a reviewer following the reference landed in unrelated code. Separately, that doc *and* this file cited the libuv abort site as `src/win/async.c:94`, which reads as a repo-relative path and resolves to nothing here (`find . -name async.c`, excluding `target/`, returns no match); the observed fact was the abort *message*, never a line read out of libuv's source, so both now name `src/win/async.c` in libuv's own tree and drop the unmeasured line number. Scope of the sweep, stated honestly: it is a one-off script in the temp directory, not an in-tree gate, and nothing in CI runs it. The first revision indexed every path on disk and had to be re-cut, because a gitignored extracted tarball and two vendored submodule copies of the CI workflow files manufactured phantom "ambiguous" citations that no reviewer can meet — 10 of them, 7 still standing after the *Three docs/manifest over-claims* entry below qualified that bullet's bare `main.rs:` cites. Indexing against `git ls-files` (436 tracked files) so a citation must resolve to something a clean clone actually has gives the figures quoted in this entry's heading. Of those citations: **0** point past end-of-file, **0** are ambiguous, **1** names a path that does not exist in this repo at all — libuv's `src/win/async.c`, kept above as the *text* of the superseded claim rather than as a live pointer — and **3** resolve only to files present on this machine and not in git: `dashboard/index.html` and `docs/DASHBOARD.md` (both still untracked, pending the commit decision) and `bench_tools/quickjs_wasm/src/c/interface.c`, a gitlink-only submodule whose patched source `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` §9.3 itself states exists only in this clone's working tree. Those three are therefore disclosed-in-place rather than wrong, and two of them go away the moment the untracked docs are committed. The three dependency-vendored citations in this file (`lru-0.12.5/src/lib.rs:1109`, `lru-0.18.5/src/lib.rs:1292`, `tower-http-0.6.11/src/cors/allow_origin.rs:61`) resolve under `~/.cargo/registry/src/`, which is where they were read from.
- **The citation instrument was itself the next defect, and re-cutting it moved four more cites**: the sweep behind the entry above was a shell pipeline that dropped vendor directories with a `grep -v` on a `file:line:text` stream, which tests the whole record rather than the path column, so any doc whose prose happened to name an excluded directory disappeared from its own results. Rebuilt as a Python script that filters on the path field and resolves against `git ls-files` (436 tracked files) instead of the working tree, it changed verdicts in both directions. It retired 7 phantom ambiguity rows caused by a gitignored MicroPython tarball and by CI workflow copies sitting inside vendored submodules, and it caught three citations that were genuinely wrong: the trait-default module cite in `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` §9.3, which named only a `mod.rs` basename and now carries its `src/wasm_runtime/` prefix; the Edge gate cite in the *Three docs/manifest over-claims* bullet, which pointed at the comment block's separator rule and two lines above the gate that actually gates wasm32-and-not-wasmer, and now names the qualified file, the gate line, the second gate and the native/WASIX entry point; and §7's Merkle table, whose line range into the untracked capture is now the header the harness prints, for the reason that entry gives. What the re-cut leaves standing, and why none of it is a wrong pointer: one citation resolves to nothing because it is the deliberately quoted libuv abort site recorded above, and three resolve only to files that exist here and not in git — the dashboard HTML and `docs/DASHBOARD.md`, both still untracked, and the QuickJS guest source behind the gitlink that §9.3 itself states lives only in this clone's working tree. Nothing in CI runs this instrument; it is a temp-directory one-off, and two of those three untracked-only cites disappear the moment the uncommitted docs are committed.
- **Two raw benchmark captures every headline table cites exist only on this machine, and three docs credit the wrong program for writing them**: the fix here is attribution, not the numbers. `docs/COMPARISON.md`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md`, `README.md`, `docs/benchmark_analysis_wasm_runtimes.md` and `docs/wasmer_edge_deployment.md` all name `bench_nda_2026-09-17.txt` or `bench_results_core5.txt` as "Raw:" provenance for the published transport tables. Both files are on disk (`45,753 B`, mtime 2026-09-17 23:45; `45,092 B`, mtime 2026-09-13 23:14) and **neither is tracked** (`git ls-files` → 0 matches), so a reviewer who clones the repository cannot open the evidence behind any of the 36x–250x claims — and repo policy is that raw benchmark output stays a local artifact, so a missing file cannot be fixed by committing it. Two misattributions made the citations actively misleading. (1) Three docs credited those rows to `velocity_mcp --benchmark`. They are not that program's output: `grep -c "Warm throughput"` and `grep -c "Avg speedup"` over `src/benchmark.rs` both return **0**, while each string appears once in `bench_nda/src/main.rs` — a different binary, whose banner also stamped `VELOCITY-MCP v3.0.0` on a 3.2.0 crate, which is how the two runs' captures are tellable apart. That stamp was itself a defect and is fixed under **Both benchmark harnesses identified themselves with a stale VELOCITY version** below, so the tell now only works for captures taken before this batch. (2) `README.md`'s ping 800K r/s / 57.1x, `tools/list` 138K / 87.6x and `tools/call` 335K / 15.2x rows were introduced by a sentence about the 2026-09-17 re-run, but those figures are lines 417/419, 444/449 and 467/469 of `bench_results_core5.txt` — the **2026-09-13** run. The 09-17 capture reports ping **1,761,585 r/s at 82.0x** avg vs JSON/stdio for the same row: the published number was not wrong so much as attributed to the wrong run, understating the current figure. Fixed across the six files by state, no invented data: each citation now names its producer and the code line that prints it, says in plain words that the capture is untracked and local, and gives the regeneration command (`cargo run --release --manifest-path bench_nda/Cargo.toml` from the repository root), so the durable record is the table plus a way to rebuild the evidence rather than a file path into someone's working directory. `README.md` now separates the 09-13 table from the 09-17 re-run and quotes both. `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md`'s Merkle table citation carried a line range into that capture — `:573-607`, corrected to `:573-609` once 609 turned out to be the table's last row and 611 a buffer note, and now *removed* in favour of the header the harness prints ("Merkle cost as fraction of total NDA pipeline round-trip"), because a range into a file no clone carries is a path into someone's working directory — the same objection this entry raises about the tables. Its "10 000 iterations" claim is now pinned to code instead of inferred: `let merkle_iters = iterations.max(10000)` in `bench_nda/src/main.rs` floors that phase independently of the pipeline phases' 500 × 3, so the doc's iteration count and the capture's banner count are both correct and describe different phases.
- **`docs/COMPARISON.md` §Scaling published four throughput figures no retained capture contains, under a mechanism label that is wrong**: the section read "Concurrent dispatch throughput (NDA binary, shared memory; `velocity_mcp --benchmark`, 2026-09-17): 1/2/4/8 threads → 1.7M/2.8M/4.2M/5.6M req/s … scales sub-linearly past 2 threads … (memory-bandwidth bound)". Resolved by running the named command rather than reasoning about it: `./target/release/velocity_mcp.exe --benchmark` (release build, 2026-10-09, exit 0, 63,442-byte capture) — **none of those four numbers appears in it**, nor in either retained capture. The mechanism label was also wrong. The phase that prints a thread ladder is `src/benchmark.rs:451-525`, an **in-process parse** benchmark: each thread runs `serde_json::from_str` on a hand-built JSON string, or `nda_native::parse_nda_request` on one pre-built binary frame cloned into the thread, with no transport, no shared memory and no server in the loop. Shared memory is a *different* phase of the same run, and its primitives measure separately: NDA write+read **13.1 ns/op (76.39M ops/s)** vs JSON **38.7 ns/op (25.82M ops/s)** over 200 000 iterations. The section now carries the measured ladder with its date, build and mechanism named (`thread_counts = [1,2,4,8]`, `requests_per_thread = 10_000`): JSON 1.64 / 2.26 / 3.50 / 5.12M req/s against NDA 2.37 / 3.58 / 6.19 / 9.51M, ratio 1.44x → 1.86x — the NDA encoding's per-thread advantage *widens* with thread count, which is the interesting finding and was invisible while the table was mislabelled as transport work. The cause claim was deleted rather than re-asserted: sub-linearity is real from the first doubling (2 threads = 1.51x of 1, 8 threads = 4.01x on the NDA arm) but a throughput counter cannot see memory bandwidth, so the doc says only "not linear on an 8-core / 12-logical-thread box" and names the inherited "memory-bandwidth bound" as an unmeasured attribution. The four superseded figures are stated inside the section, so a reader who quoted them earlier can see they were replaced and not reworded.
- **Both benchmark harnesses announced a stale VELOCITY version, and the Node peer announced one the Rust server never shipped**: the provenance round above traced the misattributions to their source, and the source was lying too. `bench_nda/src/main.rs` opened every capture with `VELOCITY-MCP v3.0.0 — {iterations} iterations × {rounds} rounds` while the server crate is **3.2.0** (`Cargo.toml:3`) and the harness is not the server at all — `bench_nda/Cargo.toml:3` declares `version = "0.1.0"` in its own workspace, so it has no compile-time knowledge of the crate it measures and `env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")` there would be the *harness's* version. `bench_nodejs/benchmark.js:312` printed the same false `VELOCITY-MCP v3.0.0` header, and `bench_nodejs/sdk_server.mjs:16` set its MCP `serverInfo.version` to `3.1.0` under the name `velocity-mcp-node` — a benchmark *peer* identifying as a VELOCITY release that never existed, in the one response field a client reads to decide what it is talking to. Measured before changing anything: `grep -rn "serverInfo" bench_nodejs dashboard` (excluding `node_modules`) returns only that line plus `bench_nodejs/server.js:346`, which already declares the honest `nodejs-mcp-benchmark` / `1.0.0`, so no test, dashboard check or doc quotes the fake version and nothing breaks on it being removed. Fixes, in increasing order of what they buy: the Rust harness banner now interpolates its own `env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")`, so a capture says which harness wrote it — the disambiguation this round had to be reconstructed by grepping for output strings; it also prints the server binary's size and mtime, because the fact a capture actually needs is *which build was measured* (this run: `Server binary: 14652928 bytes, mtime epoch 1791507110` = 2026-10-09 00:51 UTC against a run started 17 h 32 min later — the stale-release-exe question this session kept having to answer by reasoning is now printed by the harness); `benchmark.js`'s header reads "MCP Server Benchmark: Node.js peer vs Rust server" with no version claim; and the SDK peer declares `0.0.0-node-benchmark-peer` so its `serverInfo` can never be read as a product version.
