Verification: `cargo +stable check --manifest-path bench_nda/Cargo.toml` → **Finished** with one pre-existing `dead_code` warning left standing, which this round then cleared: a never-called alias of `avg_ms()` that nothing gated because `bench_nda` sits outside the workspace (`Cargo.toml:42`) and outside CI — same command, same file minus those four lines, **0 warnings**. Both edited JS files were `node --check` clean, then **exercised end-to-end** rather than just parsed: the SDK peer was spawned over stdio and answered a real `initialize`, returning `serverInfo = {"name":"velocity-mcp-node","version":"0.0.0-node-benchmark-peer"}` — the edited field is the one a client actually reads, so the handshake is the test — and `node benchmark.js 1` ran its full four-arm sweep (Node stdio, Rust stdio, Node HTTP, Rust HTTP) to **exit 0** under the new header, with `grep -c "v3.0.0"` over its output returning **0**; that harness spawns its own server children on random ports 13700–13799 / 13800–13899 and kills them, and after the run `tasklist` showed only the pre-existing demo server (PID 17216, still `LISTENING` on `0.0.0.0:3900`), i.e. no orphaned processes; `./bench_nda/target/debug/bench_nda.exe 1` printed the new banner and provenance lines (`bench_banner_check.txt`, a local capture). Scope note: the two historical captures keep their `v3.0.0` stamp, so the docs' "whose own banner stamps `VELOCITY-MCP v3.0.0`" statements stay true *of those files* — which is why they are now phrased as belonging to the capture, not to the harness. One consequence for the previous bullet's "names the code line that prints it" wording: fixing the banner *moved* it, so `docs/COMPARISON.md`'s methodology cite to `bench_nda/src/main.rs:1987` became a pointer into the middle of a multi-line `println!` the same day it was written, and is now replaced by a producer test that cannot drift with line numbers — `bench_nda` is the only harness in the tree whose source prints the row label `Warm throughput` (measured: 1 occurrence in `bench_nda/src/main.rs`, 0 in `src/benchmark.rs`, 4 in the cited capture), and the `v3.0.0` stamp is described as something that *dates* a capture rather than naming its author. Measured after the replacement, `grep -rn "bench_nda/src/main.rs:[0-9]" --include='*.md'` over the repository returns **0** matches outside `CHANGELOG.md` — and exactly **1** inside it, which is this sentence naming the cite it supersedes, not a live pointer. (The first time I ran that probe it printed nothing at all, including this line, and the reason was my own filter: the pipeline was `grep -rn PATTERN --include='*.md' . | grep -v node_modules`, and `grep -v` tests the whole output line, not its path field — while this very CHANGELOG bullet contains the word `node_modules` as prose about the benchmark peer's directory. Measured, same pattern, same tree: without the exclusion **1** match, with it **0**, and excluding on the path field alone (`cut -d: -f1` first, then `grep -v`) correctly keeps `./CHANGELOG.md`. Quoting the glob made no difference, so the first explanation I reached for — shell expansion of `--include=*.md` — is not it. The lesson generalises past this file: a doc that *discusses* an exclusion is invisible to a content-based exclusion filter, so an `exclude-unless-` guard belongs on the path column, and a citation sweep whose expected answer is "zero" must first be shown to fire on a known-positive.)

- **Two bin targets collided on one output file on Windows, and the last unit to link silently won**: the root package auto-discovers `src/bin/velocity_edge.rs` as bin `velocity_edge` while the `velocity-mcp-edge` crate declares bin `velocity-edge`; Windows normalizes `-` to `_` in output filenames, so both resolve to the same `target/debug/velocity_edge.exe` (and its `.pdb`) and cargo printed `output filename collision` three times in the workspace run that was read for this round (`deps\velocity_edge.exe`, `deps\velocity_edge.pdb`, `velocity_edge.pdb`). The root manifest now declares that file explicitly as bin `velocity-edge-wasip1`, which is also the honest name for it: it is the superseded plain-`wasm32-wasip1` CGI adapter, whose request handling compiles only under `cfg(target_arch = "wasm32")` and whose native build prints its own target hint and exits 1. `docs/wasmer_edge_deployment.md`'s Phase 3 snippet recommended precisely the colliding pairing (bin `velocity-edge` at that root path), so it now carries the correction and the shipped layout. No call site needed changing: the sweep found no build script, CI workflow, deploy script or dashboard harness that invokes a `--bin velocity_edge`, and the only two pre-existing mentions outside that snippet are a filename in a code comment and a `target` string in a sample log line. Verified structurally with `cargo metadata --no-deps`: the workspace's four bin targets are now `velocity_mcp`, `lua_binary_protocol`, `velocity-edge-wasip1` and `velocity-edge`, and no two of those names collapse to one Windows filename. What is *not* yet verified is the link itself — the relink that would show the warnings absent failed on a full disk (next bullet), so "gone" is a structural conclusion here, not an observed warning count of zero.
- **The TLV decode-site census read only the working tree, and two of its byte figures matched no file at all**: `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` §9.2 published the guest sizes as 587 808 B (MicroPython) and 271 000 B each (the six tree-walk guests). Measured against both sides of the repository, the MicroPython artifact is 588 146 B on disk and 587 776 B in the committed blob, and the tree-walk guests are 276 888-277 005 B on disk and 277 630-277 747 B committed, so neither published figure came from either file. The method also moved from scanning the export name as a byte substring to walking the WASM export section (id 7) entry by entry, which is what separates the two MicroPython guests: the file on disk has 16 exports including `mp_wasi_call_tool_binary`, the committed blob has 15 and no such symbol, and HEAD's `Makefile.wasi` still omits the `--export` line, so rebuilding from a clean clone yields the 15-export guest and the host-decoded fall-through. The consequence for the headline split is now stated wherever it is claimed: 13 of the 22 tools decode in the sandbox on this working tree, and a clean clone of main decodes 9, because the MicroPython rebuild and its Makefile line are both uncommitted. The other rows are reproducible from main — the Lua and six tree-walk exports are in the committed blobs — and QuickJS genuinely lacks `qjs_tool_call_binary` on both sides, 754 433 B identical. Eight of the thirteen tracked `.wasm` artifacts differ from their committed bytes in this working tree (dotnet, java, julia, lua, micropython, perl, php, r), which is why the table now gives both sides instead of an unqualified byte count.
- **Verification this round, and the machine limit it ran into**: `cargo +stable test --workspace` re-measured on the current tree at 1,144 passed / 0 failed / 53 ignored across 29 test binaries, matching the published figure. The citation census stayed a fixed point at 117 across 24 scanned files with the same per-file split, so the §9.2 rewrite added no new pointers to validate. The follow-up relink then failed with `LNK1318: Unexpected PDB error; LIMIT (12)`, on `fuzz_tests.pdb` in one attempt and `integration.pdb` in the next — two different victims, so not a source defect. The same log two lines earlier gives the cause: `failed to write query cache ... There is not enough space on the disk. (os error 112)`. C: is at 100% with 36 MB free, and the measured reclaimables are this repo's `target/debug` at 31 GiB (3.8 GiB of it `incremental`), the WSL distro's `ext4.vhdx` at 97.26 GiB, `pagefile.sys` at 48.83 GiB, Docker's `docker_data.vhdx` at 14.08 GiB and `hiberfil.sys` at 6.25 GiB. `target/release` (2.6 GiB) is excluded because the live demo server holds its `velocity_mcp.exe`. So the `--features http,database,oauth2` workspace run and the dashboard suites are outstanding until space is reclaimed, and any failing link on this machine from here on is a disk verdict, not a code verdict.

- **The capability endpoint's headline count was being read as a property of the build**: `docs/API.md` documented `GET /v1/nda/capabilities` correctly as a learned flag, but its worked example was taken from a freshly started process, so it showed 22 binary executors and 0 JSON fall-backs next to prose that elsewhere in this repository is quoted as a deployment characteristic. Measured against the long-running demo server on this machine at the same moment, the identical tree answers 15/7, with `analyze_logs_go` and the six tree-walk `*_greet` tools as the `false` entries, and a differential pass over current source takes a fresh process from 22/0 to 21/1 — `analyze_logs_go` alone, whose Go guest is compiled on demand by a TinyGo toolchain this environment lacks. The deletion also retired two now-false sentences in the untracked docs that had been written against the locked artifact: `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` §9 said the stale release exe “cannot be relinked while the demo server holds the file lock”, which is no longer the blocker because the exe is gone with it, and `docs/DASHBOARD.md` now names that exe as the suite set's only hard compiled-artifact dependency — with `target/` absent, `test_interactive.mjs` stops at its spawned auth server while the legs that address an already-running server still pass. `nda_binary_capabilities` (`src/registry.rs:726`) derives each flag as `!json_fallback_recorded(name)`, and `record_json_fallback` is reachable only from the two pre-execution fall-through arms of `call_tool_binary` (`src/registry.rs:776` and `src/registry.rs:806`), so a `false` can only ever be produced by traffic that process has already served and a restart clears all of them. The section now states that the count belongs to the answering process and gives both measured figures instead of one.
- **Correcting this round's own outstanding-work clause, and the operator's disk cleanup changed the state under it**: the verification bullet above recorded that the `--features http,database,oauth2` workspace run and the dashboard suites were blocked on `os error 112`. That block is gone: the operator deleted `target/` on 2026-10-10, which measured 34 GB free afterwards and left only `target/release/build` (15 MB) — `target/release/velocity_mcp.exe` is not on disk either, while the live demo server (PID 17216, started 2026-10-09 02:58:41) still listens on :3900 and serves from its loaded image, so that path can no longer be relaunched and the process's own startup snapshot now predates the 03:23-03:29 rebuild of the six tree-walk guests. Two consequences recorded honestly: the two Node dashboard suites that need no compilation were re-run against that live server and pass — `probe_nda.mjs` 6/6 (40 tools on `tools/list`, corrupted frame and garbage bytes both rejected with HTTP 400) and `diff_nda.mjs` reports 22 tools, payload identical 22, diverged or skipped 0, plus 1 tool that errors identically on both transports, which is a tool defect rather than a transport split. And the compiled legs are still outstanding, now by choice rather than by capacity: a from-scratch debug workspace build previously measured 25.90 GiB into `target/debug/deps` against 34 GB free, so the featured workspace run and the relink that would positively show the collision warnings absent are deferred under the standing instruction to keep storage from re-bloating, and neither is being reported as passed. The three `src/registry.rs` pointers the bullet above introduces re-measure the citation census from the published 117 to 120 over the same 24 targets (`CHANGELOG.md` 76 to 79, the other four files unchanged), still 1 deliberately quoted broken path, 0 out-of-range, 0 basename-ambiguous and 3 resolvable only on this machine. The bare `:806` it first shipped in measured 119, which is the useful part: a citation written without its path is invisible to the census and to a reviewer, so it is not a checkable fact.
- **The crate's own plugin-manifest example taught a key the parser does not accept.** The module documentation in `src/plugins/mod.rs` showed an executor written as `"type": "process"`, while the live field is `pub executor_type` (`src/plugins/mod.rs:94`) with no `#[serde(rename)]` — so a manifest copied from that example does not load at all. All 24 executor blocks across the 19 shipped `plugins/*.json` use `executor_type`, and the tool list served on `:3900` proves those files load, so the doc comment was the only divergence in the tree. The same block named `process` as the only supported executor type although every one of the 22 WASM tools is a `wasm` executor. Both are corrected: the example now carries the real key, the required keys per type are taken from `load_plugin_manifest`'s own validation arms (`src/plugins/mod.rs:237`), and the text now says where a compiled module belongs — `source_file` is read as text (`src/plugins/mod.rs:522`), so a `.wasm` path goes in `source`, which the compiled runtimes test with `Path::new(source).exists()` (`src/wasm_runtime/rust.rs:85`, `src/wasm_runtime/go.rs:179`). `cargo fmt --all -- --check` passes over the edited tree (exit 0), which re-parses every file in the workspace without a build.
- **An unknown configuration key is accepted silently, and that behaviour is load-bearing.** No struct in `src/` or `tests/` carries `deny_unknown_fields` (measured: zero occurrences), and every config field is either `#[serde(default)]` or an `Option`, so `apikey = "secret"` under `[http]` parses cleanly and leaves the server with no authentication, while `max_request_bytes = 1024` leaves the 10 MB default in force. `validate()` (`src/config.rs:505`) checks only the values it has fields for — mode, log level, and in HTTP mode a non-empty address and a non-zero request size — so a typo reports nothing at startup. Enforcement is not a free change: four shipped manifests depend on keys `PluginManifest` does not declare (`description` in `plugins/log_analyzer_js.json`, `log_analyzer_lua.json` and `log_analyzer_python.json`, plus `description` and `_note` in `plugins/log_analyzer_go.json`), so adding `deny_unknown_fields` would reject files the server loads today. The behaviour is therefore documented in `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` section 9 rather than altered, and the manifest doc block in `src/plugins/mod.rs` now says the same thing for manifests.
- **Two zero-disk sweeps, and one module that is not compiled.** All 12 built-in guest paths named by `src/config.rs` resolve on disk (56 310 B to 1 583 417 B), and all six `[http]`/`[logging]` keys in `dashboard/demo.toml` match real fields on `HttpConfig` and `LoggingConfig` — so the demo config carries no inert key. Left untouched deliberately: `src/plugins/manifest.rs` is not declared as a module (`src/plugins/mod.rs:55` declares only `marketplace`), so it is never compiled, yet it holds a second copy of `PluginManifest`, `PluginTool` and `PluginExecutor` that differs from the live structs at `src/plugins/mod.rs:67` in exactly one respect — it renames the executor key to `type` (`src/plugins/manifest.rs:38`), the very spelling this round's doc fix warns against. A contributor editing the manifest schema there would change nothing at runtime. Deleting it, or finishing the extraction task #338 started, is an operator call rather than a doc fix.
- **This round's own citations re-measure the census from 120 to 131 over the same 24 targets** — `docs/API.md` 5, `docs/COMPARISON.md` 1, `docs/DASHBOARD.md` 1, `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` 34, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` 0 to 2 (the new paragraph), `CHANGELOG.md` 79 to 88 — with 1 deliberately quoted broken path, 0 out-of-range, 0 basename-ambiguous and 3 resolvable only on this machine. Both instruments used here run without compiling: `cargo fmt --all -- --check` exits 0 and re-parses every workspace file, and the census is the local script `cite_sweep3.py` — which is not in the repository, so nothing in CI runs it and the figure is a measurement, not a gate.
- **A feature doc still published a test count no run produces, and its open item was checked rather than assumed.** `docs/DYNAMIC_GO_TOOLS.md` closed with "All 724 existing tests pass", which was true on 2026-09-12 — the document's own date. A sweep of every tracked markdown file for suite-count claims turned up exactly one stale figure: this one. The suite the repository publishes is 1,144 passed / 0 failed at default features and 1,211 / 0 with the CI feature set (`docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` §9, measured 2026-10-09 over 29 test binaries), and `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`'s "1,100+" at lines 52 and 1053 agrees with that figure, so nothing else needed touching. The line now names the authoritative row and labels 724 as the count at writing time instead of restating a number a reviewer cannot reproduce from the tree. The same pass tested that document's still-unclaimed future item "Binary protocol support: implement TLV decoding for zero-allocation calls", and it remains genuinely open: the Go runtime's `call_tool_binary` (`src/wasm_runtime/go.rs:279`) decodes the frame host-side with `decode_json_value` and hands the guest JSON, so there is no in-guest Go TLV decoder to advertise — consistent with the §9.2 census, which counts Go among the host-decoded tools. The bullet's own pointer moves the census from 131 to 132 (`CHANGELOG.md` 88 to 89, other files unchanged), and the re-run confirms the same 1 deliberately quoted broken path, 0 out-of-range, 0 basename-ambiguous and 3 resolvable only on this machine. **Superseded the same day by two later bullets in this file:** the pair this bullet publishes as current is now **1,142** at default features (measured twice on two trees, most recently 2026-10-11) and **1,212** with the CI feature set, which is a dated 2026-10-10 capture rather than a current-tree claim; the census total it records as 132 is now 355; and the 3 rows resolvable only on this machine are now 8 over 5 distinct targets. Its own mechanism is the reason — it added a pointer, the pointer moved the count, and the figure it printed froze one edit before its successor landed. It also carried a defect no census can see: `docs/DYNAMIC_GO_TOOLS.md` restated 1,144 and 1,211 in prose *while pointing at* `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` §9 for them, so when that row was re-measured this morning the document became silently wrong without a single citation moving; it now names the row and leaves the numbers to it.
- **The suite's own rate-limit test emptied the bucket every sibling dispatch test draws from, and three of those siblings could not tell a refusal from a dispatch error.** `GLOBAL_RATE_LIMITER` (`src/rate_limit.rs:154`) is a `LazyLock` with no reset, which is right for production — one bucket per process is exactly the semantics the stdio and shmem transports want — but it is also the object `src/protocol/nmcp_binary.rs:227`, `src/protocol/nmcp_binary.rs:496` and `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:255` consume during unit tests, and every `#[cfg(test)]` module in `src/` lands in one lib test binary. `test_dispatch_tools_call_rate_limit_exceeded` (`src/protocol/nmcp_binary.rs:1489`) proves the refusal path by spending the whole 100-token burst (`src/rate_limit.rs:17`) and returns with the bucket empty, after which it refills at 20 tokens/sec (`src/rate_limit.rs:20`), i.e. one token per 50 ms for the rest of the run. The exposed population is measured, not guessed: 16 `tools/call` request literals sit behind those three call sites — 12 in `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs`, 4 in `src/protocol/nmcp_binary.rs` — each spending exactly one token, of which the cancellation case is the exception that never draws, because its `is_cancelled` arm at `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:243` returns before the limiter. Three of them were blind to a refusal rather than merely at risk from one, because the refusal strings interpolate the tool name: `src/protocol/nmcp_binary.rs:237` builds "Rate limit exceeded for tool '{}'", so `test_dispatch_tools_call_unknown_tool`'s `contains("nonexistent_tool_xyz")` passed on a refusal; the shmem refusal sets `isError: true` at `src/protocol/nmcp_binary.rs:508`, so `test_handle_json_shmem_tools_call_unknown`'s only content assert passed on it too; and `test_tools_call_unknown_tool_returns_error_content` (`src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:846` asserted `isError` and nothing else. Two siblings were already safe by wording rather than by intent — `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:878` and `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:1247` test for the dispatch-only prefix "Error running tool", which no refusal prints — so the fix reuses that discriminator instead of inventing one, and `test_dispatch_tools_call_empty_data` (`src/protocol/nmcp_binary.rs:1015`) gained an explicit "not a refusal" clause because an empty-name error and an empty-name refusal differ by nothing else on the wire. The poisoning is removed by `refill_global_bucket` (`src/rate_limit.rs:175`), which restores tokens to burst and re-bases `last_refill_ms` so the drained interval is not re-credited afterwards: it is `#[cfg(test)]` and `pub(crate)`, and a `tests/` integration binary compiles the crate without `cfg(test)`, so it cannot ship and cannot be reached from production — which is the point, since the reset it performs is precisely what production must never offer. It is called at `src/protocol/nmcp_binary.rs:1516`, *before* that test's own assert, so a failure there cannot leave the bucket empty for the other 15. Residual, stated rather than fixed: a sibling whose call lands inside the drain loop's few milliseconds is still refused; closing that needs a `cfg(test)` mutex across all 16 sites, which is too large a change to attempt without a compiled run.
- **Three tests named for error classification cannot fail on a misclassification.** `test_tools_call_error_classification_not_found` (`src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:1163`) asserts `text.contains("NOT_FOUND") || text.contains("Error running tool")`, and every error the dispatch branch produces begins with that prefix — `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:268` is the single `format!` that builds all of them — so the right disjunct is true whenever `isError` is true and the assertion contributes nothing. Its sibling at `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:1247` asserts the prefix alone, and the `#[cfg]`-paired permission test at `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:1293` asserts only `isError`. The classifier has six arms (`src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:235` through `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:266`) and no test names any arm exclusively, so the `Error type:` contract the transports advertise to clients is gated by `src/error.rs`'s unit tests but never end to end. Not strengthened this round, because the correct token per fixture is a measured fact this machine cannot produce without compiling: `file_read` on `/nonexistent/path/file.txt` reaches `NOT_FOUND` only if the Windows error string contains "not found" or "No such file", and asserting a token the platform does not emit would convert a blind gate into a red one. The class is not hypothetical — the live server already mis-reports a missing toolchain this way, answering `analyze_logs_go` with `Error type: NOT_FOUND / Hint: Check that the path or resource exists.` for what is a `TinyGo not found` compilation failure, which is a hint no client can act on. **Superseded 2026-10-10, re-measured 2026-10-11.** The mis-report this finding ends on is fixed; the end-to-end gate gap it is titled on is not, so "and the gate gap with it" overstated — what landed was classifier-level coverage. Current numbers for the three tests named above: `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:1223` keeps its disjunct, `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:1237` asserts the prefix alone, `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:1400` asserts `isError` alone with `#[cfg]`-paired bodies; the six-arm block described here as `:235`-`:266` is now the named function at `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:82` and has seven arms, pinned by two direct tests (`src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:1255` names every arm from a fixture table, `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:1285` pins the ordering) that need no platform, and `TOOL_BUILD_FAILED` matches before `NOT_FOUND` for exactly the `TinyGo not found` string quoted here (see the *missing TinyGo* bullet at the end of this section). The as-printed-then pointers kept in the paragraph above (`:1157`, `:1171`, `:1286`, `:235`, `:266`) are deliberately left stale and are the five rows this gate reports as advisory on this line — the mapping just given is their re-point. Two further corrections came from reading the branch rather than its labels. First, the premise "every error the dispatch branch produces begins with that prefix" is over-broad: the branch now writes three in-band `isError` texts — the cancelled arm's text sits at `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:249`, the rate-limit refusal is built at `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:268`, and only the execution-failure arm's `format!` at `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:1670` carries the prefix — so the right disjunct is *not* true whenever `isError` is true. It is the same discriminator this file's rate-limit bullet relies on ("test for the dispatch-only prefix … which no refusal prints"), which makes the assertion vacuous only against a misclassified execution failure: the narrower claim its title makes, and the one that still holds. Second, the population is five, not three — `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:1437` and `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:1456` joined it and each assert only `isError`, so the TIMEOUT and NETWORK_ERROR arms are named by the fixture table and never exercised end to end. All five stay as written, for the reason this finding gives: the correct token per fixture is host OS error text, and asserting a token the platform does not emit converts a blind gate into a red one.
- **Verification for those five edits, and what they still owe.** `cargo fmt --all -- --check` exits 0 over the edited tree, re-parsing every workspace file, so the edits are syntactically valid and rustfmt-clean — but that is the whole of what was shown: it is not a type check, so `#[cfg(test)]` visibility, the `pub(crate)` path and the new string asserts are argued from the call sites above rather than executed. The lib test-binary leg is owed together with the featured workspace run already deferred by the standing storage instruction, and neither is reported as passed. The 22 `path:line` pointers the two bullets above introduce re-measure the citation census from the published 132 to 154 over the same 24 targets (`CHANGELOG.md` 89 to 111, the other five citing files unchanged), still 1 deliberately quoted broken path, 0 out-of-range, 0 basename-ambiguous and 3 resolvable only on this machine — so every new pointer lands on a tracked file and inside its current end-of-file line, which is the only property the census can check without compiling. **Superseded on re-measurement, same day:** the three bullets added below this one carry pointers of their own, so the 154 is no longer current — re-run over the same 24 targets the census reads **218** (`CHANGELOG.md` 111→174, the citing docs 43→44), the untracked-only class is **8** rows over 5 distinct targets rather than 3, and the count now includes the pointers this file's own verification passage quotes. The instrument that produced both figures also turned out to be defective; see this round's verification bullet, which holds the current numbers.
- **Verification this round, and a harness that counted without naming.** The two dashboard legs that need no compilation were re-run against the live `:3900` server after these edits: `probe_nda.mjs` 6/6 and `diff_nda.mjs` 22/22 payload-identical, exit 0, capabilities 15/7 both before and after. The differential printed its `identical-but-erroring: 1` line without naming the tool, so learning which one required reading the runner's source; it now prints the names, and the single one resolves to `analyze_logs_go`. Reproduced over plain JSON-RPC as `Failed to register WASM tool 'analyze_logs_go': Compilation failed: TinyGo not found` — the toolchain absence the 21/1 capability figure already records, so this is an environment gap, not a new code defect. `docs/DASHBOARD.md` and `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` §9 now carry today's re-run dates, and `docs/DASHBOARD.md` quotes the runner's literal summary line. No compiled leg ran *that* round: the `--features http,database,oauth2` workspace test and the collision-free relink were then deferred by choice under the standing storage instruction, and were not reported as passed. Superseded 2026-10-10 - the featured workspace run has since been executed, and the collision-free relink remains outstanding; see the verification bullet at the end of this section for the figures and what the run cost in disk.
- **Requests without an `id` no longer get a reply** (JSON-RPC 2.0 4.1: a Request with no `id` is a Notification and the server MUST NOT reply to it, including inside a batch). The decision was being made from the *method-name prefix*, so a dispatchable method sent as a notification was answered — measured before the fix: stdio printed `{"id":null,"jsonrpc":"2.0","result":{}}` for `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","method":"ping"}`, and `POST /v1/mcp` returned **200** with that same body while `notifications/initialized` correctly returned 204. The method match moved into a private `dispatch`; `handle_request` now runs it and withholds the response when `request.get("id")` is absent, so the notification's side effects still happen (`notifications/cancelled` records the cancelled id, `notifications/initialized` still logs) and only the reply is dropped. The order is load-bearing, not stylistic: applying clippy's `question_mark` suggestion by placing `request.get("id")?` *before* the dispatch silenced `notifications/cancelled`'s side effect and turned `test_notifications_cancelled_tracks_id` red at `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:789` (`assertion failed: is_cancelled(&json!(42))`; that number is quoted tool output and stays as printed — this file's own verification bullet already lists it as one of the two deliberately-historical anchors, and the same assertion sits at `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:808` today) - the lint's advice was right, its position was not. The test is on *presence*, not on `id.is_null()`, because an explicit `"id": null` is a present member and therefore not a notification. After: the two-line stdio probe (`ping` with no id, then `ping` with `id:1`) emits exactly **one** line; HTTP returns **204 with 0 bytes** for `ping` and `tools/list` without an id and **200 with 36 bytes** with one. Because this touches shared code, all call sites were swept first — there are five production sites, not the two a truncated grep suggested: `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:698` (stdio), `src/middleware.rs:221` (batch, which now omits notification entries from the response array as 4.1 requires), `src/transport/http.rs:407` (plain POST), `src/transport/http.rs:591` (streamable-HTTP session, which now emits its `notification`/`processed` frame instead of a response frame) and `src/transport/http.rs:1290` (WebSocket, which guards on `Some` at `src/transport/http.rs:1299`). Three of this bullet's anchors were re-measured this round: two had drifted and are re-pointed — the session site +3, the WebSocket site +32 — and the third, the quoted `:789`, is deliberately left stale for the reason in its own parenthesis. The two that moved had gone unnoticed because they were written as bare `:588`/`:1258` continuation refs: `scripts/check_citations.py` requires `filename.ext:NNN`, so a bare pointer is invisible to the gate and can rot with no report at all. They are path-qualified now for exactly that reason. The site *count* re-checked independently against the current tree: `grep -n handle_request src/transport/http.rs` gives three lines (`:407`, `:591`, `:1290`), and with stdio and batch that is the five claimed. Disclosed because it is the finding: this round re-pointed `:789` to `:808` first, which made this file's verification bullet's "two anchors stay stale deliberately" sentence false about its own subject; the grep that would have prevented it — searching this file for prior adjudication of the anchor before touching it — is the step that was skipped, and the revert is recorded rather than silently undone. The `crates/velocity-mcp-edge` function of the same name is a different `handle_request` and is unaffected. A scan of every `json!` literal in `src/`, `tests/` and `crates/` that carries `"method"` and lacks `"id"` (23 sites) found no test expecting a reply to an id-less request — the existing ones already assert `is_none()` — and the `tests/e2e_wasm.rs` request helper injects an id before sending, so this is a clean addition to the suite rather than a breaking change. Re-verified 2026-10-10 against the final build: `cargo +stable test --workspace --features http,database,oauth2` gives **813 passed / 0 failed / 38 ignored** for the `--lib` binary and **1216 passed / 0 failed / 53 ignored** summed over all 29 binaries, exit 0; `cargo +stable test --workspace` at default features gives **1145 passed / 0 failed / 53 ignored**, exit 0; `cargo +stable fmt --all -- --check` exit 0. The honest history: the identical feature-gated run was **811 passed / 2 failed** when this bullet was first written. One of those two failures was the regression described in the sentence above (mine, caught by the suite, fixed by reordering); the other was a pre-existing uri collision between two `resources.rs` tests, recorded separately below. Pinned by `test_id_less_request_gets_no_reply`.
- **Three `resources.rs` tests no longer race for one global queue**: `test_notify_and_poll_resource_updates`, `test_notify_nonexistent_resource_produces_no_update` and `test_poll_resource_updates_drains` each hold a `Mutex<()>` guard (`lock_update_queue()`) for their duration. `poll_resource_updates()` is `drain_updates()`, which is `std::mem::take` on the process-global `RESOURCE_REGISTRY`, so any test in the same binary could steal another's queued update — the pair of failures recorded earlier in this section went green on the next run with **no change to either test**, which is the signature of a flake rather than of a fix. The guard set was *claimed* exact and was not: the enumeration keyed on `notify_resource_update` and `poll_resource_updates`, which do have **zero** production callers, and missed `test_notify_update_caps_at_10k`, which mutates `pending_updates` directly and stayed unguarded until #532 enrolled it on 2026-10-13. Measured on 2026-10-10: three consecutive full `--lib` runs — **812 passed / 0 failed / 38 ignored** at 21.49 s, 22.61 s and 23.74 s, exit 0 each — three green runs against a flake that later measured 5 reds in 40 iterations, so that evidence was luck, not a fix. Stated plainly: this serialises the race, it does not remove it. The underlying defect remains that an update queue lives in global state, and the durable remedy is to assert on owned state under a unique key the way `test_peer_buckets_are_tracked` does.
- **`test_database_resource_query` no longer shares a uri with another test**: it registered `db://users` with a runnable probe query while `test_db_resource_registration` (`src/resources.rs:1092`) registers the *same* uri with `SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = ?`, which no table in the test database backs. `register_db_resource` is last-writer-wins on the process-global `RESOURCE_REGISTRY`, so the query test passed or failed depending purely on which of the two ran last. Measured as the second of the two failures named above: `Database query should succeed: Some("Failed to prepare query: no such table: users")` at `src/resources.rs:1196`. The query test now registers *and reads* its own uri (`db://query_probe`); `test_db_resource_registration` keeps `db://users`, and its membership assertion is order-independent because nothing unregisters that uri. Unlike the queue guard immediately above, this removes the collision rather than serialising it - and it is the second instance of the same root cause: this module's registry is process-global state shared by every test in the binary.
- **Gates re-run on the final build, and the sweep found one CI step that stops short of the code it could check.** Measured 2026-10-10 on this section's last two fixes: `cargo +stable fmt --all -- --check` exit **0**; CI's literal lint step `cargo +stable clippy --features http,database,oauth2 -- -D warnings` exit **0** with 0 errors; `cargo +stable test --workspace --features http,database,oauth2` exit **0** - **813 passed / 0 failed / 38 ignored** in the `--lib` binary and **1216 passed / 0 failed / 53 ignored** summed over all 29 test binaries; `cargo +stable test --workspace` at default features exit **0** with **1145 / 0 / 53**. Then the same lint command with `--all-targets`: exit **101**, 26 lints in `velocity_mcp (lib test)` and 19 across four benches (`comprehensive_benchmark` 14, `tool_compatibility_test` 2, `wasm_isolation` 2, `wasm_vs_native` 1). That gap is the finding, not the red: **CI's clippy step compiles lib and bins only, so every test and bench target is lint-ungated** - the lints are mechanical (`len() == 0` x10, redundant closure x6, `Copy`-type `.clone()` x4, `is_multiple_of` x2, `Default::default()` field assignment x2, `is_ok()`-then-`unwrap()` x2, and one each of unused import, dead constants/fields, needless index loop, single-pattern match, redundant pattern, `matches!`, unnecessary cast, unnecessary `&mut`, and a complex type to factor). Clearing them and widening the CI step to `--all-targets` is one operator decision, not a silent change: it gates a target class the pipeline has never gated, so it is recorded as open. Also recorded as known and not repairable here: cargo's future-incompat notice names `proc-macro-error2 v2.0.1`, and `cargo tree -i` resolves it to exactly one chain - `wasmer-derive v5.0.6` -> `wasmer v5.0.6` (and `wasmer-middlewares v5.0.6`), i.e. an upstream proc-macro this repo cannot replace while it pins Wasmer 5.0.6. Cost of the verification, stated because disk is what deferred it: these runs rebuilt `target/debug`, and C: measured **431 G used / 18 G available (97 %)** at the end of them, against the 34 G free recorded the same morning before the featured run.

- **`[logging].level` and `VELOCITY_LOG_LEVEL` were parsed, validated, and then ignored: the live filter was a hardcoded `"info"` built before the config existed.** At HEAD the subscriber is the first statement of `main()` (`git show HEAD:src/main.rs` lines 18-25) and its fallback is `EnvFilter::new("info")` — so `RUST_LOG` worked (it is tried first via `try_from_default_env`) and nothing else did, because the init ran roughly a hundred lines before `ServerConfig::load_with_env` is even called. The gap was invisible from the config side: `validate()` still checked the name (`src/config.rs:517` rejects anything outside `error|warn|info|debug|trace` at startup) and `apply_env_overrides` still wrote the field (`src/config.rs:469`), so a user could set a level, have it accepted, and get `info` anyway. The init now sits after `validate()` (`src/main.rs:118`) and passes `&config.logging.level` as the fallback (`src/main.rs:127-134`), with `RUST_LOG` still winning. Measured on the rebuilt release exe as four fresh `--mode http` processes, each posting one `ping` with stderr captured (line count / DEBUG-tagged lines / `Processing JSON-RPC request` at `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:136`): `[logging].level = "debug"` → **33 / 1 / 1**; `level = "info"` → **32 / 0 / 0** (the difference is exactly that one debug line); `level = "error"` + `VELOCITY_LOG_LEVEL=debug` → **33 / 1 / 1**, i.e. the env override reaches the live filter even against a config that says `error`; `level = "debug"` + `VELOCITY_LOG_LEVEL=debug` + `RUST_LOG=error` → **1 / 0 / 0**, so the precedence is `RUST_LOG` > `VELOCITY_LOG_LEVEL` > `[logging].level` > `info`. The first and third arms are what HEAD cannot produce at all: both would print the second arm's figures. Two properties to keep in mind when editing this: `EnvFilter::new` is the panicking constructor, and what makes it safe here is that `validate()`'s five-name whitelist runs first — moving the init back above validation would turn a typo'd level into a panic instead of a `Config error:` line; and the pre-existing `test_apply_env_overrides_log_level` (`src/config.rs:661`) asserts only `config.logging.level`, i.e. the field, never the filter, which is exactly how a green suite coexisted with an ignored setting. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` §11 now states the chain, that the filter is built once after validation, that logs are plain text on stderr, and that there is no JSON formatter and no `format` key under `[logging]`.
- **A missing TinyGo was reported to clients as a path problem, and the classifier was untestable in the shape that hid it.** The before-measurement is the live server on `:3900`, verbatim: `tools/call analyze_logs_go` answers `Failed to register WASM tool 'analyze_logs_go': Compilation failed: TinyGo not found. Install from https://tinygo.org/getting-started/install/` followed by `Error type: NOT_FOUND` / `Hint: Check that the path or resource exists.` — a hint no client can act on, because nothing is wrong with any path. The cause is arm order over a substring our own code writes: `Compilation failed: {}` (`src/wasm_runtime/go.rs:183`) wrapping `TinyGo not found` (`src/wasm_runtime/go.rs:350`), then wrapped again by `Failed to register WASM tool '{}'` (`src/plugins/mod.rs:478`, `src/plugins/mod.rs:593`), contains the literal `not found` that the `NOT_FOUND` arm matches. The classifier was an inline block inside the dispatch arm, so nothing could call it with a chosen string; it is now `classify_tool_error` (`src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:82`, arms `:83`-`:118`), called from the error-formatting site the transports share (`src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:299` renders through `tool_error_text` at `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:1666`, which the shared-memory and NDA dispatchers call too), with `TOOL_BUILD_FAILED` matched **first** (`src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:87`) over both build-failure literals and a hint that names the real remedy (install the toolchain, or load a prebuilt module path from the manifest). Gating, because the end-to-end pair stays prefix-only for the platform reason recorded just above: `test_classify_tool_error_names_every_arm` (`src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:1255`) drives eight literal fixtures through all seven arms and requires a non-empty hint from each, so an arm that is deleted or silently reordered fails; `test_classify_tool_error_build_beats_not_found` (`src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:1285`) feeds the two live TinyGo strings verbatim and demands `TOOL_BUILD_FAILED`, while `"file not found: /a/b.txt"` must still resolve to `NOT_FOUND` — the ordering claim and its non-regression in one test. Fixtures are literals because `sandbox::sanitize_error` preserves our own message text, so the gate measures the classifier rather than the host OS's error strings; the trade-off is that it cannot catch a producer whose wording changes, which is why the four producer sites are named above. Measured: `cargo +stable test --lib classify_tool_error` → **2 passed / 786 filtered out**; included in the workspace run below. **Not yet re-measured end-to-end**: the `:3900` text quoted here is the *before* capture, and the after-capture needs `target/release/velocity_mcp.exe` relinked, which PID 17216 still holds open — so this bullet claims the unit gate and the ordering proof, not a client-visible A/B. **Superseded 2026-10-11:** the relink happened, and the after-capture is now quoted from a live HTTP response in the release-binary bullet of this section (`Error type: TOOL_BUILD_FAILED` with the two-remedy hint); the `:3900` image still answers `NOT_FOUND`, which turns this caveat's *before* capture into the control half of a measured A/B rather than an open item.
- **Verification for this round, and the rot that the citation bullet above predicted happening to its own numbers.** Dashboard harness retarget, re-measured after the harness edits rather than quoted from them: `test_interactive.mjs` **38 passed / 0 failed, exit 0** on 2026-10-10 against a current-source release build the suite spawns itself on `:3997` (cold capabilities **22 binary / 0 fallback**), and the negative control — the same harness against the older image still serving `:3900` (**15 / 7**, the seventh being `analyze_logs_go`, which has no TinyGo on this host) — **36 passed / 2 failed, exit 1**, both red legs being exactly the SSE checks, printing the double-framed wire: `0 comment / 2 data-framed: data: event: connected | data: data: {"sessionId": …}`. The added leg is `sseWireFrames()` (`dashboard/test_interactive.mjs:74`), a raw `node:http` line reader that counts frames on the wire, because the page-log check it replaced could not have seen a `: heartbeat` comment at all — `EventSource` discards comment lines by spec — so the old leg was gating an unobservable thing, which is why the server could frame every event twice and still show green. The capability-gate block is now server-list-driven (`noBinary.size ? [...noBinary][0] : 'perl_greet'`), which is what lets one harness pass on both a 22/0 cold server and a 15/7 day-served one. Suite totals: `cargo +stable test --workspace` at default features → **1,141 passed / 0 failed / 53 ignored across 29 test binaries, exit 0**; the +2 over the 1,139 published earlier in this section is exactly the two new classifier tests, named so the figure is attributable rather than drifted. The lineage to **1,141** is now measured name-by-name rather than by subtraction: the capture three hours earlier printed **1,145**, and the set difference between the two runs is **7 names removed / 3 added**. The removed seven are the `velocity-mcp-core` client-direction tests (elicitation in five parameter shapes, plus roots and sampling), which the capability-direction fix made obsolete, and they are replaced by one parametrised refusal test plus the two classifier tests. One removed row has no dedicated successor case: the empty-`message` elicitation variant. It is covered by construction, because the refusal happens on the method name before any parameter is read and the surviving test states that the refusal must not depend on the params, so this is disclosed as a by-construction cover rather than claimed as a one-for-one retarget. The featured `--features http,database,oauth2` total is now the re-measured **1,212 passed / 0 failed / 53 ignored across 29 test binaries, exit 0**, which replaces both the **1,210 / 0 / 53** capture from earlier the same day and the **1,212 by arithmetic** this bullet published in its place. The arithmetic was right, and the reason it could be checked so cheaply is part of the finding: the featured profile had already been built that evening, so an incremental featured workspace run after two further source edits finished in **2 m 27 s**, not the clean rebuild against about half of the remaining free disk (C: **438 G used / 11 G available**) that had been cited as the reason to skip it — the deferral estimated a cold build while a warm one was available, the same error this section names elsewhere. The two captures are verifiably different feature sets rather than one log read twice: **61** http-named test cases pass in the featured run against **41** in the default one. Citation re-verification: every `path:line` in `CHANGELOG.md` and `docs/*.md` pointing into a file edited since the last re-anchor was re-checked by printing the source line at that number, and **21 anchor numbers were wrong**. Re-anchored: `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs` `:73`→`:120`, `:464`→`:492`, `:657`→`:690`, `:670`→`:711`, `:682`→`:698`, `:184`→`:243`, `:196`→`:255`, `:790`→`:846`, `:822`→`:878`, `:1181`→`:1240`, `:268`→`:297`; `src/transport/http.rs` `:779`→`:780`, `:836`→`:902`, `:893`→`:959`, `:1673`→`:1747`, `:2239`→`:2392`, route `:1464`→`:1530`; `src/main.rs` `:140`→`:143`; `src/config.rs` `:17`→`:18`, `:111`→`:125`, `:333-381`→`:347-395`. The shifts run from +1 to +74 and are not a constant, which is the same finding as the bullet above re-confirmed from the inside: a range-only census passes every one of them, because all of them stayed inside their file. One of them (`:1181`) was already stale *before* this round — it printed a frame-status assert today — so the re-anchor pass cannot be assumed to have been complete either. Three cites in `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` were bare basenames (`src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:496`, `:486-503`, `:541-546`) that escaped the census entirely and were wrong; two are now qualified with `src/protocol/` and the third was `:496`→`:531`, both plus `src/protocol/nmcp_binary.rs:710`→`:724` living inside the three-pipe ASCII box. Those two in-diagram pointers stayed bare because widening them would push the box's right border past every other row. Re-measured this pass: they are content-correct (line 724 is `if nda_native::is_nda_frame(&raw) {`, line 531 is `let maybe_len = u32::from_be_bytes(peek_buf);`), and the box was already broken independently of them, because the HTTP row's borders sat at columns 21 and 81 while every other row sat at 20 and 67. So the fix is layout, not wording: the diagram is re-laid out at a uniform 55-character interior that fits the qualified paths, the HTTP row's border is back on the grid, and both in-diagram pointers are now fully qualified and therefore checkable. Two anchors stay stale deliberately, both historical by construction: `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs:789` inside the quoted red-run message, and `src/transport/http.rs:731` in the stale-citation entry, each describing a line as of its own measurement. The content-anchored checker that would make this class impossible now exists, as `scripts/check_citations.py` (untracked, 160 lines as this batch lands), and it is still wired into nothing: re-run after this paragraph landed, a grep for its name over `.github`, `scripts`, `dashboard`, `docs` and the root markdown files now returns exactly **1** file, and that file is this one — the only reference to the script in the repository is the prose that says nothing references it. Measured before this bullet named it, the count was **0**, and no workflow, wrapper or harness invokes it, so nothing gates it and a clean clone does not contain it either. Running it is the second half of this finding, because it disagrees with the census on exactly one class and that disagreement is the defect the census was blind to: same 218 denominator, opposite verdict on the bare-basename rows. The checker resolves every cited path literally against the repo root, so a pointer that names only a basename cannot resolve; `cite_sweep4.py` resolves a basename against the `git ls-files` index and therefore calls the same pointer unambiguous. Its verdict on the pre-qualification tree was exit **1**, **31** hard failures and only **133** of 218 citations resolving to a populated line, the 31 classifying as 24 repo-internal bare basenames, 6 dependency-vendored crates.io paths (two `lru` versions and one `tower-http` file, which live under the cargo registry cache and are quoted as upstream evidence, not as repo pointers), and the 1 deliberately quoted libuv abort site. Both numbers are published here rather than one because neither instrument is a superset of the other: the census catches out-of-range and ambiguity the checker does not model, and the checker catches every basename-only pointer the census waves through. Re-measured on the qualified tree the same afternoon: exit **1** still, but **8** hard failures instead of 31, and **151** of 218 citations resolving to a populated line instead of 133 (superseded by the re-measurement at the end of this bullet) — while the census totals do not move at all (218 / 1 deliberately quoted broken path / 0 out-of-range / 0 ambiguous / 8 untracked-only). That asymmetry is the reason both instruments are published here: the census is stable under a defect class the checker treats as fatal, and the checker is sensitive to a class the census reports as clean. Citation census re-run after these three bullets landed, then iterated until the figure it publishes stopped moving (`cite_sweep4.py`, still a temp-directory one-off with nothing in CI gating it), over the same 24 targets and 436 tracked files: **218** citations — `CHANGELOG.md` 174, `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` 34, `docs/API.md` 5, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` 3, `docs/COMPARISON.md` 1, `docs/DASHBOARD.md` 1 — verdict **1** broken path, **0** out-of-range, **0** basename-ambiguous, and **8** rows resolving only on this machine over **5** distinct untracked targets (`dashboard/index.html`, the QuickJS gitlink `interface.c`, `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md`, `docs/DASHBOARD.md`, `dashboard/test_interactive.mjs`). Those 218 include the 13 pointers this passage itself introduces, and the iteration is itself the finding: the run before this passage was written read **206**, and writing it manufactured two defects of its own — the synthetic line number quoted as the self-test's negative control, and two bare `lib.rs` cites quoting the very defect being reported — which showed up as a census row out-of-range **1** and basename-ambiguous **2** until the wording was fixed, because a self-citing census measures the report as well as the tree. Two findings this round, both instrument problems rather than doc problems, and each worse for the headline than the count is: **(1) the census mis-verdicts the tree's only dotted-directory citation.** `resolve()` opened with `pathstr.lstrip("./")`, which is a *character-class* strip, not a prefix strip, so `.github/workflows/ci.yml:31` — the widened clippy step, cited from this file — lost its leading dot, resolved as `github/workflows/ci.yml`, and was reported BROKEN. Measured against the file before fixing, so this is a defect report and not an excuse: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` is in `git ls-files`, is 76 lines, and line 34 reads verbatim `run: cargo clippy --features http,database,oauth2 --all-targets -- -D warnings`, i.e. the citation was correct and the instrument was wrong. Because a false BROKEN and a false clean are the same failure mode, v4 runs five self-test gates before its counts mean anything and all five print True: the dot-prefix path resolves, a bare `lib.rs` is flagged ambiguous across **7** tracked candidates (`bench_tools/rust_wasm/src`, `bench_tools/wasm_tool/src`, `client/src`, `crates/velocity-mcp-core/src`, `crates/velocity-mcp-edge/src`, `macros/src`, `src`), a synthetic pointer past that file's end-of-file is flagged out-of-range, a non-existent path is flagged missing, and the fully qualified core `lib.rs` resolves to one file. That second gate is what caught this round's two real doc defects: the `velocity-mcp-core` bullet cited two of its test anchors by bare `lib.rs` basename, and both are now qualified — content-checked first, `crates/velocity-mcp-core/src/lib.rs:526` is `assert!(result["capabilities"].get("roots").is_none())` and `crates/velocity-mcp-core/src/lib.rs:731` opens the differential test that walks `elicitation/create`, `roots/list` and `sampling/createMessage`, so only the *path* was missing, never the numbers. **(2) a CHANGELOG self-pointer was live-wrong, which is the rot the bullet above predicted eating its own numbers.** That bullet records the one deliberately-stale citation as living at `CHANGELOG.md:185` (re-pointed twice since it was first recorded: it named 82, which was wrong by 49 lines even before this round's 17-line insert above it pushed that bullet from 131 to 148), but `grep -n "http.rs:731" CHANGELOG.md` returned **49, 91 and 119** in that round and line 82 was then the *NDA binary capability poisoned by a bad payload* bullet; re-pointed to whatever line held it at the time. **No number in this clause is live today** — the file grew afterwards, the same three targets now sit further down, and a later round content-addressed that pointer, which is this bullet's own failure mode applied to its own sentence. A range-only census cannot see this class either — 82 remains a perfectly valid line number in a file of this size — and because the pointer is *self*-referential no edit to cited source is needed to break it: the file only has to grow. Same mechanism, second instance on that line: it parenthesised the `/nda/capabilities` route as "now `:1464`", which stayed true only until this round's own re-anchor moved it to `:1530`, and it now says so in those words. Two more numbers changed kind, not just value: the docs side of the delta is **+1** and it is this round's `ServerConfig` at `src/config.rs:18` parenthetical at `docs/USER_GUIDE.md:796` (it sat at `:794` until this round's two-line comment split added one line above it and the metering-budget paragraph plus its blank line added two more), which already cited `src/config.rs:505`; and the untracked-only class is **8** rows over 5 distinct targets, not **3**, the additions being `dashboard/test_interactive.mjs:74` (this round's `sseWireFrames()` cite) and two citations that point *into untracked documentation* — `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md:376` and `docs/DASHBOARD.md:182`. The scale of that last one is a census row cannot express: `git status --porcelain -- docs dashboard` reports `?? docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md`, `?? docs/DASHBOARD.md` and `?? dashboard/`, so **35 of the 218** citations (34 + 1) *live inside* files that are not in git — a reviewer cloning main loses the pages carrying 16% of the tree's pointers, not merely their targets. Nothing here is fixable by editing: raw captures stay untracked by repo policy and the commit is the user's call, so the honest statement is disclosed-in-place, and 33 of those 35 disappear the moment the two docs and `dashboard/` are committed. Finally, the ambiguity reading is a property of today's file set, not of the citations: before this pass **33** pointers named only a basename and looked unambiguous solely because each name was then unique among the 436 tracked files. Re-measured after it, the checker's repo-internal basename class is down from **24** rows to **1**: the qualification batch removed **23** of them — 12 in `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` (10 prose cites plus the two paths that live inside the three-pipe ASCII box) and 11 in this file. One row survives and is deliberately left: the bare pattern inside the grep command quoted in finding (2) above is a shell argument, not a citation, so qualifying it would falsify the command that bullet records running. Two classes are *not* defects and are named so nobody re-fixes them: Third class, invisible to **both** instruments and the largest remaining defect count in the tree: anchors that carry a symbol name and a line number but **no path** — the backticked form `build_nda_response_raw` followed by a bare number, and the parenthesised form. A path-less token is unresolvable by definition, so the census reports this class as clean and the checker never sees it either; the only way to adjudicate one is to grep the named symbol and print the cited line. An adjudicator built for that purpose swept the 23 tracked docs plus `CHANGELOG.md` and `README.md`, found **38** such anchors, and this pass moved **18** of them: **15** pointing into `src/protocol/nda_native.rs`, of which **fourteen** were off by exactly **nine** lines and one by a single line, and **3** in one `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` sentence. The uniformity is the finding, not the count: a constant offset across a contiguous block of anchors is one nine-line insertion into the source invalidating one doc snapshot, not fifteen independent mistakes — and it is localisable, because `skip_tlv_value` stayed correct at its cited line while the functions nine lines later did not, dating the insertion to between the two. Each re-point was content-verified against the printed definition line before writing. The dashboard sentence was worse than stale in the line: it named `loadCaps` and `callTool` at two numbers that both land inside *other* functions, so the pointer was wrong in the symbol as well as the position, and it now names the three functions that actually perform the three acts the sentence claims. One further anchor in the same file was found through the checker's *keyword advisory* channel rather than by position and was off by two lines — it cited the closing brace of a helper instead of the tool-selection line it describes. After the batch the repo-wide symbol audit returns **0 stale of 38**. What the two instruments then reported is the point of recording this: the census stayed at **218 / 1 / 0 / 0 / 8** with every per-file count unmoved, while the checker moved from **151** populated / **59** advisory to **153 / 57** — two rows gained, one from an anchor landing on a line that carries its keywords and one from the off-by-two fix. That asymmetry is the whole point of recording the class: eighteen real doc defects were closed and the instrument that supposedly audits doc defects moved by zero. Two advisories in this class were investigated and are **not** defects, named so nobody re-fixes them: the tree-walk row's cite lands on the `Error running tool` format string while the checker's keyword set is drawn from the whole table cell, and the QuickJS fall-through row cites a line *range* whose first line is a comment — both cites are accurate, and a keyword-adjacency heuristic on 9,000-character rows is exactly the kind of advisory that has to be read before it is acted on. a token like `Cargo.toml` at line 45, or a self-pointer into this file at line 91, has no slash but is a valid repository-relative path that both instruments resolve to one file — a bare `Cargo.toml` is not ambiguous despite **8** tracked candidates, because the root file is a literal hit and resolution never reaches the basename index — and the remaining **7** hard rows name upstream code (two `lru` versions, one `tower-http` file, the libuv abort site) quoted as evidence about an external crate; no path inside this repository can satisfy them.

- **Pre-commit hygiene: the secret scan came back clean, and the strongest version of that result is a whole-tree measurement rather than a batch one.** The held batch is 97 files — 83 tracked-and-modified plus 14 untracked — and two content sweeps were run over it. The first used the known token-prefix shapes (`ghp_`, `sk-`, `AKIA`, a PEM private-key header, the `x-access-token` form, a `wasmer_io_` prefix, and a bearer scheme followed by twenty or more opaque characters); the second used a wider credential-shaped rule, i.e. a password, secret, token, api-key, access-key or private-key name assigned a literal of sixteen or more characters. Every hit in both passes is a placeholder or a fixture: the seven bearer matches in the deployment guide are all the same literal example string, and the assignment matches are test fixtures inside `src/` plus the one config line that calls the normaliser. Zero files with secret-shaped names are in the batch. The whole-tree form of the finding is stronger than the batch form: the count of *distinct* bearer token strings across every file in the repository, tracked and untracked, `.git` excluded, is **1**, and that one is the documentation placeholder. The two lines this round added to the deployment guide were also checked against code rather than assumed, because they state behaviour: `normalize_api_key` maps a blank or whitespace-only value to `None`, `auth_middleware` fails closed when the configured key is empty, and `test_auth_empty_configured_key_fails_closed` asserts `UNAUTHORIZED` for both a blank token and an arbitrary one — so "a blank value means authentication is not configured" is the shipped behaviour, not a hope.
- **The repo-hygiene finding that needs an operator decision rather than a doc fix: `.gitignore` is inert for 41 tracked files totalling 1,668,974 bytes.** `git ls-files -i -c --exclude-standard` is the only instrument that shows this class, because `git check-ignore` reports *nothing* for a tracked path unless `--no-index` is passed — measured directly, it exits 1 with empty output on two of these very files while the tracked-and-ignored listing includes them. The 41 split as **25** under the vendored quickjs `node_modules` tree, matched by the pre-existing node-modules rule, including five `.so` extension binaries and one 636,400-byte wasm module; **11** test-fixture files under the four `test_nda_dir` and `test_registry_*` scratch directories, matched by those four rules; and **5** raw bench captures under `bench_nda/`. Those last five are exactly the number the comment this round added to `.gitignore` claims ("Five such files were committed before this rule existed and stay tracked until someone runs `git rm --cached`"), so that comment measures correct. The first two groups are not covered by it and predate this round entirely: an ignore rule that a tracked file already defeats is decoration, which is how a cleanup task recorded as complete can coexist with binary payloads in the tree. Removing any of it is index surgery, so nothing was untracked here and the call is the operator's.
- **A concrete reason the held commit is worth reading before it lands: two tracked audit captures in the batch are smaller than their own committed versions.** The two `bench_nda/audit_*_http.json` files are tracked, are now matched by this round's new audit-capture ignore rule, *and* are modified. Working tree: **109** and **211** lines (2,702 and 6,649 bytes), both written the evening of 2026-10-09 by the most recent audit run. `git show HEAD` of the same two paths: **9,217** and **10,241** lines (232,464 and 325,497 bytes), whose sequence counters run to 6000. `git diff --numstat` states the pair as 41 added / 9,149 deleted and 95 added / 10,125 deleted. So committing the batch as it currently stands replaces two committed 6,000-entry captures with two small ones — precisely the overwrite the new ignore rule was written to prevent, still reachable because an ignore rule has no effect on a path git already tracks. Two safe dispositions exist and both belong to the operator: stage the batch except those two paths, or restore them from `HEAD` and accept that the small captures are discarded and regenerable by re-running the audit. Nothing was staged, restored or reverted here.
- **The citation checker can exit 0 now, and how it earned that is the part worth reading.** `scripts/check_citations.py` grew an explicit allowlist: five exact cited targets, each carrying a mandatory reason string, counted as allowed instead of as a hard failure. They are the two vendored `lru` versions, one vendored `tower-http` file, the upstream libuv abort site this section already names as deliberately unresolvable, and one bare basename that is the argument of a quoted grep command rather than a pointer into this repository. Naming them by shape rather than by token is deliberate: writing the five literal targets into this file would manufacture five new citations out of rows whose whole status is that they are not citations, and the durable record of the exact keys is the script itself. The header gained a fifth column and it reconciles by arithmetic on the tree as it stands: 218 checked = 153 resolving to a populated line + 0 hard + 57 advisory + 8 allowed, exit **0** (those 8 rows are the five distinct targets spread over three citing lines, each row printed with the reason that admits it). Three properties were tested rather than asserted, by re-running the script against a three-line fixture in a temp directory whose only differences from the real rows are numbers: a citation matching an allowlisted *path* one line off is still a hard failure, because the key is the whole cited target and not its path; the same line number under a sibling filename is still a hard failure; and the exact allowlisted row is the only one permitted. All five gates on that run print True, including the header-reconciliation gate and the exit-code gate. The list also self-audits: an entry nothing cites is printed as dead, which is what keeps an allowlist from quietly outliving the citation that justified it — measured on the real tree all five keys are live (no dead rows), and the fixture run correctly reported four of five dead, because that fixture cites only one. Two things exit **0** does not mean, stated because a green number is easy to over-read. First, the script is still referenced by exactly **1** file: `grep -rl` over `.github`, `scripts`, `dashboard`, `docs` and the root markdown returns this CHANGELOG and nothing else, so nothing gates it, and putting it in the pipeline is a policy decision that binds every future push — the operator's call, not a unilateral edit. Second, the bare-basename entry is the one to distrust most: it is keyed on the exact number, so it does not swallow a correctly-pathed citation to that file, but any future pointer that happens to spell that same basename and same number will be silenced rather than checked. The script's own length moved with this edit, which is the single-source problem this section has now named twice from the inside: a sentence that restates a property of a tool goes stale the moment the tool changes, so the count above is corrected in place rather than duplicated here.

- **Both no-compile dashboard suites ran green today, and the re-run moved the line numbers under a pointer this file makes about them.** `bash dashboard/test_dashboard.sh` reports 14 passed / 0 failed and `node dashboard/test_cdp.mjs` reports 5 passed / 0 failed, exit 0 for each, both against the long-running server on port 3900 rather than a fresh build, so they gate the dashboard pages and the live transport and cost no compile and no disk. `docs/DASHBOARD.md` and `docs/NDA_PROTOCOL.md` section 9 labelled these two as last run 2026-10-09 and not re-run this round, which the measurement makes false; both rows now publish today's tallies with their date, and the CDP row's single burst distribution is now two dated ones (avg 1.6 ms / p50 1.5 ms / p95 2.6 ms on 2026-10-09 against avg 2.53 ms / p50 2.3 ms / p95 4.5 ms today) because that distribution is per-run text, not a property of the code; both stay far below the 10 ms quantum the leg guards, and neither run is attributed to load. Growing that same page by four lines is how the second defect surfaced, and it is a pointer in this file: the CORS bullet above cites `docs/DASHBOARD.md` for the demo's deliberate wildcard origin at line 182, while the page's wildcard-CORS note actually sits at line 301 — and on the pre-edit file line 182 was the paragraph introducing the interactive suite, so the anchor was already mis-anchored before this round touched anything, and the correct note then sat at line 297. It is repointed. Neither instrument can see this class: the census only asks that the cited line exists and is inside end-of-file, and the checker's own tally moved the *wrong* way while I measured it — one advisory row became a populated row (153 / 57, then 154 / 56) purely because my new prose put the word `assert` on the line a CHANGELOG row cites, out of 49 candidate tokens on that row. Repointing the CORS pointer moves the same figure again, to 155 / 55. So the checker's populated count is not evidence that an anchor is correct; only its missing-file and out-of-range classes are, which is why the exit code keys on those two alone. The census total is unchanged at 218 over the same 24 targets, 1 broken, 0 out-of-range, 0 basename-ambiguous and 8 rows resolving only on this machine, because both edits changed a number, not a count. The two no-browser legs were re-measured the same evening from fresh captures, exit 0 each: the frame probe reports 6 pass / 0 fail, and the differential reports 22 tools with 22 payload-identical, 0 diverged or skipped, its one identical-but-erroring tool still the TinyGo-dependent one, and the capability answer unchanged at 15 binary / 7 fallback before and after that pass; section 9's differential row in the protocol doc now carries that dated clause. One hazard showed up while writing this: every edit to `docs/DASHBOARD.md` moves the line numbers this file points into, so the two repairs above hold only because that page's line count was measured at 310 before and after each edit — a first attempt that split one sentence across an extra line was reverted for exactly that reason, and the checker then reports the same 182 and 301 rows it did before the churn. No Rust source changed in this leg, and the free parse check still ran against the tree as it now stands: `cargo fmt --all -- --check` exits 0.

- **The instrument that has been carrying the dashboard verdicts is two builds behind the tree, and the suites only came clean once they were re-pointed at a current one.** Re-measured 2026-10-11 with no compile at all: `src/protocol/json_rpc.rs` was last written at 2026-10-10 20:04:58 and carries 187 added / 41 removed lines against HEAD, while `target/release/velocity_mcp.exe` is stamped 2026-10-10 19:41:49 — so every dashboard result this file attributes to a release build, including the 38/0 run above, was produced by a binary that predates a hot-path protocol edit by 23 minutes. The long-running demo server is further behind again: PID 17216 started 2026-10-09 02:58 from that same path, so its image is a day and a half older than the file it launched from, and `netstat`/`Get-Process` are what show it, not the file's mtime. `target/debug/velocity_mcp.exe` (2026-10-10 21:31:05) postdates every real source diff — proven by `find src crates client -name '*.rs' -newer` against the release stamp, then checking each hit's `git diff --numstat` — so a fresh server on `127.0.0.1:3902` from that build is the current-tree instrument, and on it all four legs pass: `node dashboard/probe_nda.mjs` 6 pass / 0 fail, `bash dashboard/test_dashboard.sh` 14 / 0, `node dashboard/test_cdp.mjs` 5 / 0, `node dashboard/diff_nda.mjs` exit 0 with 22 tools payload-identical and its one identical-but-erroring tool still the TinyGo-dependent `analyze_logs_go`, and `node dashboard/test_interactive.mjs` 38 / 0. The CDP burst (avg 6.22 ms / p50 6.1 / p95 8.4) is a **debug-profile** build and is recorded only as the leg's own distribution gate, never as product latency. Three failures during this pass were all mine and each is worth naming, because each one looked like a product defect: **(1)** a server started from a directory other than the repo root loaded **0** plugins and answered tools/list with **16** tools instead of **40**, silently — the plugin manifests are resolved relative to the working directory, the log prints an INFO line per plugin it finds and nothing for the ones it does not, so a wrong cwd degrades to a smaller product rather than an error. That is the strongest argument yet for the missing `VELOCITY_PLUGIN_DIR` override. **(2)** The interactive suite's API-key leg spawned its own server onto a port I had already taken, and reported "auth server failed to start — auth path untested"; the port is hardcoded at `dashboard/test_interactive.mjs:27`, so the collision is mine and the FAIL is honest. **(3)** My first hand probe of the key path used an `X-API-Key` header and got 401 even with the right key, which is what the server *should* do — the middleware reads `Authorization` at `src/transport/http.rs:318` — and the corrected probe on the current build gives no-key 401, wrong-bearer 401, right-bearer 200, and `X-API-Key` alone 401. One inherited figure also fails against the tree today: the bullet above states that suite spawns its auth server on `:3997`, but `3997` appears nowhere in the harness and the code says `:3901`; because `dashboard/` is untracked there is no history to show whether the port moved after that run or the number was never right, so the disagreement is left standing here as an unresolvable claim rather than quietly restated. **Repo hygiene closed in the same pass:** `.gitignore` had gained an editable-install rule but no bytecode rule, while five `.pyc` files sat untracked under `sdk/python/velocity_mcp_client/__pycache__/` — one `git add -A` from being committed into a published repo. Two lines (`__pycache__/`, `*.pyc`) were appended in the file's existing commented style with CRLF preserved (95 lines / 95 CR to 98 / 98), `git check-ignore -v` confirms the new rule fires on the real file, and the untracked-entry count drops 9 to 8. The 41 tracked-but-ignored files above are untouched: untracking them is index surgery and the operator's call.

- **Route-table census: the documented HTTP surface now matches the registered one, and the one over-claim it found is repaired.** A throwaway parser compared every route registration in `src/transport/http.rs` (built by `build_router`, at line 1476) against the Route Index table in `docs/API.md`, normalizing only the `/v1` nest prefix and trailing path parameters: **31 registered, 31 documented, 0 present in one set but not the other, in either direction**, method included. The instrument was made to fail before it was believed — four self-tests all fired (a bogus path is flagged, a real path that has a doc row is not, POST-vs-GET on the same path is caught, and the table-row count exceeds 25, so the parser is reading the table rather than prose). The only three other route registrations in `src/` are test-only routers inside `src/middleware.rs`, below its `#[cfg(test)]` module, so they are not surface. What a set-difference cannot see is a promise in the prose under the table, and that promise was live-wrong: it said every *index only* route's response body is the Rust type named in its handler signature, but the WebSocket handler returns `impl IntoResponse`, which names no body — and the first draft of this bullet asserted status 101 from the framework's contract instead of measuring it, so the live server on port 3900 was probed with a raw `Upgrade: websocket` request and answered `HTTP/1.1 101 Switching Protocols` with a `sec-websocket-accept` header and no body. The sentence now names that exception in place; the edit replaced two lines with two lines, so `docs/API.md` stays at 1359 lines with zero CR bytes and no citation pointing into it moves. Three neighbouring claims were verified rather than edited: 201-on-success matches `StatusCode::CREATED` in the review handler, `/health` is the only table row without the `/v1` prefix and also the only route registered outside the nest, and the authentication paragraph matches the middleware (enforced only when `security.api_key` is `Some`, failing closed on an empty configured key) — the probe needed no `Authorization` header, which is that paragraph's no-key case observed from outside. Residual, not covered: the census compares method and path only. It checks no handler's request schema, status code, or purpose-column prose against behaviour, and the nine audit-export and admin rows are all *index only*, so their JSON and CSV shapes are documented nowhere in this table. The census script was scratch, not a new gate, and now lives outside the repo. Re-confirmed after this write: citation census 218 total, checker exit 0 with 155 populated / 0 hard / 55 advisory / 8 allowed (155 + 55 + 8 = 218) and no dead allowlist key — note that grepping the literal `advisory token` prints 53, because two advisory rows carry the sub-class `advisory blank` instead.
